Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 51: That's All.
-----------------------
“Uuuugh!”

Seo Daeryong grimaced with every expression imaginable as he pushed himself up from the bed.

“Thirsty. Water! Water!”

“Beside you!”

At someone’s words, Seo Daeryong instinctively reached out and drank the water set on the small table beside the bed.

“Ugh, my head. If I ever drink again, I’m a dog. A dog.”

Seo Daeryong drank straight from the pitcher.

Only after quenching his thirst did he realize he had woken up somewhere unfamiliar. The bed was large and plush, and the bedding was soft and luxurious.

‘Come to think of it, who was it that just said “beside you”?’

Seo Daeryong looked around.

Far off, someone was sitting by the window reading a book. Because of the sunlight, he couldn’t make out who it was.

“Who?”

Gradually, as his eyes adjusted to the light, the man’s figure came into view.

“Aaagh!”

Seo Daeryong screamed. The man reading the book was none other than the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“W-where is this? Why is the Demon Venerable here?”

Seo Daeryong was so startled that he even stammered.

“Because it’s my room.”

“Oh my! Why am I here?”

“You don’t remember?”

“...No.”

“When we were about to part ways at the tavern, you suddenly sprang up and shouted at the top of your lungs that I had to carry you back. You asked how I could abandon my disciple. You even clung to my trouser leg.”

“I did? Surely not?”

Good heavens. Thinking that in the end he really had clung to the man’s trouser leg, Seo Daeryong was so flustered he didn’t know what to do.

“I can’t remember how many decades it’s been since I allowed someone at my feet.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon glanced at Seo Daeryong.

“Do you want to die, but lack the courage to jump off a cliff?”

“...”

At that moment, a question came to Seo Daeryong’s mind.

“But why did you put me to sleep in your bed, sir?”

“Of course, you don’t remember that either.”

Seo Daeryong gasped and tensed.

“When I brought you here and tried to put you in the guest room, you called me a heartless master for making my disciple sleep in such a shabby place. Then you sat down on the floor, said you were a bastard who deserved to be treated that way, and made every kind of pathetic scene, even shedding tears.”

“...”

“Is life hard? It was hard for me too. Holding back the urge to kill you.”

Seo Daeryong scrambled down from the bed and knelt.

“I’m sorry. I’ll quit drinking from now on.”

“Why quit such fine liquor? You need to quit your drunken antics.”

“Yes, I’ll quit it all. Please forgive me. I drank far too much yesterday.”

No matter how much he had shouted and caused trouble, the man could have struck his sleeping acupoint and thrown him not in the guest room but in the stable. The fact that he had put him in his own room, and in his bed no less, was completely unexpected. Of course, it was probably because of Geom Mugeuk, but still, to think he would let him sleep in his bed. Seo Daeryong saw a new side of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Go.”

“Yes.”

His outer robe was neatly folded on the table beside the bed. Of course, a maidservant must have folded it, but he felt as though he had been properly treated before leaving.

Even then, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was reading a book, and the sight felt unfamiliar.

‘Is he trying to act learned in front of me?’

Strangely enough, though, the sight of him reading suited him. Looking around the room, Seo Daeryong saw several bookshelves filled with many books.

Then one book caught his eye.

“Huh? This book?”

Without thinking, Seo Daeryong pulled a book from the shelf. It was an adventure story about a boy who met an unrivaled master and grew into a hero, a book he had read many times since childhood.

“This is my favorite book. Have you read it, by any chance?”

Excited, he turned back to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, on the other hand, was glaring at him terrifyingly.

“I hate bastards who touch my things without permission most of all.”

“Gasp!”

Startled, Seo Daeryong dropped the book, then blocked it with his foot to keep it from hitting the floor. The book bounced off his foot and rolled across the floor.

Silence fell.

Seeing the book lying open and crumpled, Seo Daeryong’s vision went dark. Soon, his own fate would probably be like that book’s.

Seo Daeryong hurried over, picked up the book, and smoothed out the crumpled part with his hand.

“I’m sorry. Ah, luckily it was a boring part that got crumpl— I’m sorry!”

Fortunately, instead of cutting off Seo Daeryong’s arm, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned his gaze back to the book he had been reading. Today, he was the incarnation of forgiveness.

“I’ll be taking my leave now.”

He gave a timid farewell and was about to leave the room when the Blood Heaven Blade Demon abruptly asked.

“Why do you like that book?”

“Ah, because I thought the protagonist was similar to me. He was born poor, had a gloomy personality, and couldn’t fit in well with the people around him. And it’s the story of a fellow like that becoming a hero... I must have read it at least twenty times. It’s a book I keep close by and take out whenever I can’t sleep.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon stared at Seo Daeryong for a moment, then said nothing and returned his gaze to the book he was reading.

“Then I’ll be going. Thank you very much for letting me sleep here.”

At that moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked again.

“Do you think the Second Young Master will become the successor?”

There was no need to hesitate over this question.

“Yes.”

“Why?”

“He is an outstanding man. More than anyone else I’ve ever seen. I believe the main cult will change because of him. In a good way, of course.”

“Can you give your life for him?”

Seo Daeryong thought for a moment, then answered honestly.

“No.”

“You said the Second Young Master is an outstanding person.”

“I’m not. I’m not great enough to sacrifice myself for someone else.”

Though his eyes were still on the book, the corners of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s mouth lifted slightly.

“Wait in the yard.”

“Yes!”

Seo Daeryong stood in the yard and waited for the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

‘Is he going to use my answer that I’m not prepared to die for the Pavilion Master as an excuse to beat me up? Should I have said I could die for him?’

As he was worrying like that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon came out belatedly and threw a large saber.

Thud!

The saber flew lightly and embedded itself in the ground before Seo Daeryong’s feet. It wasn’t as large as the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber, but it was still a great saber, with a blade broader and longer than an ordinary saber.

“Pick it up.”

“Yes.”

Seo Daeryong pulled out the saber and held it. Since the martial arts he had learned until now were sword arts, he had never once trained in saber arts. In fact, he had barely ever even held one like this.

“How is it?”

He didn’t know the intent behind the question, but Seo Daeryong first said what came to mind.

“It’s heavy.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded and said,

“That is everything.”

“Pardon?”

“Understanding that heaviness is the beginning and the end of the saber art I intend to pass on to you.”

“!”

In that instant, something touched Seo Daeryong’s heart.

“My heart just tingled.”

Seo Daeryong, who had been looking down at the saber, lifted his head with an excited face.

“That’s because you drank too much yesterday. Or you have a heart disease.”

“Ah! Yes.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned around.

“Don’t be late for training later.”

“Yes! I won’t be late!”

Seo Daeryong set the saber down in a corner of the yard and left, then came back again, picked up the saber, and walked out.

Even as he walked out of that place, he wondered whether he had really just spoken with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, whether he had truly slept there.

‘Maybe I’m still dreaming...’

And so, feeling a hangover weighing down his whole body as heavily as the saber in his hand, he walked out of that place.

* * *

Seo Daeryong entered my office.

He still looked disheveled, not quite sober, and at his waist hung a great saber I had never seen before. Just by looking at that great saber, I could tell things with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had gone well.

“My right-hand man has arrived?”

“You mean the right-hand man you sent to his death.”

“Fortunately, you came back without getting cut down.”

“No, how could you send me along with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon? You should have looked after me, Pavilion Master!”

“It seems you don’t remember a single thing from yesterday.”

At those words, Seo Daeryong flinched.

“You insisted you would go with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon even if it killed you. Do you know what you even said to me?”

“What did I say?”

“You asked why I was trying to separate master and disciple! I really thought the two of you had been master and disciple for fifty years.”

“...Please don’t lie.”

“I wish it were a lie too. Until you fell asleep, you said you’d rather die than learn martial arts from him, and then you suddenly woke up as his chief disciple. If you doubt me, ask Lord Jang. Lord Jang looked at you and shook his head.”

“Uuuugh. Are you saying such a crazy drunkard really lives inside me?”

Seo Daeryong clutched his head.

“What a shame.”

“What is?”

“You had character when you were gloomy and sinister, but lately you’ve been getting too bright. You’re even funny, like yesterday.”

“Don’t worry. By tomorrow, I’ll be gloomy again.”

“What do you mean?”

“My first training session starts after work today. He’ll probably half-kill me. Ah! I wish work would never end.”

“What a pity. I won’t get to see it.”

“Pardon?”

“I’m leaving the cult today. It should take about two months, so guard the Hwangcheon Pavilion well while I’m gone.”

The playfulness vanished from Seo Daeryong’s face.

“Are you perhaps leaving because of that matter?”

“That matter?”

Then Seo Daeryong pointed at his own eyes with his finger. Only then did I understand what he meant.

Yesterday, before we went drinking, Seo Daeryong had said it in front of the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable’s residence. While looking into my eyes, he said they were the eyes I had shown before accomplishing the impossible.

“That’s right. It’s that matter.”

“It must be dangerous.”

“Fortunately, this matter shouldn’t be all that dangerous. I plan to use a danger I saved up in the past.”

“Pardon?”

Seo Daeryong looked as though he had no idea what I meant, but I did not explain any further.

With the playfulness gone from his face, Seo Daeryong bowed politely.

“Please have a safe trip! I’m sorry that I always only worry with words like this.”

“If you want to lend me even one hand later, learn diligently from the Blade Demon.”

I was about to leave when Seo Daeryong called me.

“Pavilion Master.”

“What?”

“If it weren’t for you, I would never in my life have had the chance to learn martial arts from the Demon Venerable.”

“You never know what will happen in life. If it weren’t for me, you might have had the chance to learn martial arts from my father. Then, keep up the good work.”

“Yes! Don’t worry about this place and go.”

I went straight to find Ian, told her about the training she had to do over the next two months, then quietly left the cult.

* * *

I kept running northwest.

As my realm in the Wind God’s Four Steps rose higher and higher, my Swift Step also became faster.

Unless someone was a master specialized in lightness arts, most experts could no longer match the tips of my feet in speed.

I ran like mad, and when my inner qi ran low, I rested in a deserted place in the forest while circulating my qi. When my inner qi was replenished, I ran again, and when it ran low, I circulated my qi again.

When I got hungry while running, I hunted using the ingenious Qi Release Father had taught me.

By now, I could quickly sense a wild boar deep in the forest far away.

When I caught a boar and roasted it over a campfire like that, passing hunters or herb gatherers would join me, and we would drink together. Before my regression, I had met them so often and grown so close to them that I found them comfortable and pleasant.

Once half the distance to my destination remained, I began controlling my pace.

Since arriving too early would be pointless anyway, I enjoyed the journey itself.

If there was a mountain worth climbing, I climbed it. Of course, I did not simply walk up it; I climbed the cliffs. I climbed using only my physical ability, without inner qi, and examined the state of my body. Whether anything hurt, where my muscles were lacking.

When one mainly uses inner qi, one tends to neglect the body itself, and that is where the difference between a true master and a middling expert appears. The real ones do not miss even the smallest details. Because they know the difference in small things eventually becomes everything, they never overlook even a single corner of their bodies.

If the sun set in the distance while I was climbing a cliff, I would sit on a protruding rock or tree halfway up the cliff and look at the scenery.

When I gazed upon the beauty of great nature like that, it felt as though something blocked inside my chest was being blown wide open.

I had promised Ian that we would travel the Central Plains together, but when I sat like this at the edge of a cliff and watched the sunset, I thought of Father before Ian.

I wanted to travel with Father. I wanted to look at scenery like this together and talk with him. I wanted to know what kind of person Father was, what thoughts he lived with.

Would I ever have that chance? Would such a moment ever come to us?

Before my regression, I truly never dreamed I would come to feel such closeness toward Father. Perhaps I had missed the father with whom I had never even properly had a conversation.

Even while enjoying the journey like this, when it was time to run, I ran until my breath felt like it would burst.

By the time I arrived at my destination, my realm in Swift Step had risen by one level. My running speed had grown faster, and the inner qi it consumed had decreased.

“Hahahahahaha!”

I ran down the road laughing like a madman.

What I felt as my realm in the Wind God’s Four Steps rose was that Swift Step was a martial art that touched upon human nature. Since it raised the exhilaration of running to the extreme, it even had an addictive quality that made me want to keep running.

Now, even if people on the road saw me, I passed at a speed where they could not recognize my form. I truly just whooshed past them.

In fact, I heard conversations like this several times.

“Didn’t a person just pass by?”

“What? I didn’t see anything.”

I had reached a realm where, if they were walking while lost in thought, they would not even know I had brushed past them.

If I rose one more level, they would not know even if they stared with their eyes wide open. They would not know whether a person had passed or a bird.

What would Swift Step be like once it reached great completion?

Perhaps they might not even sense that something had passed.

And the place I arrived at was somewhere no one in the main cult would ever imagine as my destination.

This was Hong Mountain, where the main altar of the Pungcheon Cult beyond the borders was located.

The place where I had obtained the Yin Thunder Bell, the first material for the Great Regression Art.

I had returned here once more.

Chapter 52: If I Hate You Like You Hate Yourself.
-------------------------------------------------
The first place I stopped by was an inn in the village below Hongsan.

The inn was bustling with people, and the workers were frantic with work.

I stood at the entrance for quite some time, but no one came over to me.

Then I spotted a little boy going back and forth between the kitchen and the guests, carrying dishes. The moment I saw him, a smile rose to my lips.

In my life before regression, when I came here to retrieve the Yin Thunder Bell, that little boy had grown into a full-fledged adult and welcomed me. His face was just as it had been when he was young.

Belatedly noticing me, the child trotted over and attended to me.

“Hello, warrior. As you can see, there are no seats right now.”

“Do you have any rooms?”

“No, no rooms either. Guests have been pouring in because of the Blood God Rite (血神祭).”

“When is the Blood God Rite held?”

“Exactly ten days from now. There’s another inn at the end of that road, but I doubt things are any different there.”

The boy, busy beyond belief, left those words behind and ran off when another guest called for him.

Ten days before the Blood God Rite. I had arrived at just the right time.

The Blood God Rite was the grandest ceremony held once a year by the Pungcheon Cult to venerate the Blood God.

This was also why I had needed two months for the third method of blocking the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s sorcery.

The Blood God Rite.

Only on the day this Blood God Rite was held could I obtain the means to subdue the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. Thankfully, the day of the rite was two months away; if the timing had been wrong, killing the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable might have had to wait another year.

In the past, it had taken me years to infiltrate the throne of the Pungcheon Cult Leader, where the Yin Thunder Bell was kept.

That was how long it had taken to discover the secret passage the Pungcheon Cult Leader used to escape in times of crisis.

Actually sneaking in and taking the sacred artifact had not taken even half a quarter-hour, but finding that passage had taken that long.

Though I had painstakingly discovered the secret passage, stealing the Yin Thunder Bell was still far from easy.

The problem was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

He spent most of his time in the place where his throne was.

He ate there, and even slept there. I could not even make use of the brief moments when he relieved himself.

That was because an iron grate made of ten-thousand-year cold iron was lowered over the place where the sacred artifacts, including the Yin Thunder Bell, were stored.

Moreover, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was not the only one there. There was another person guarding the sacred artifacts.

He wore shackles made of ten-thousand-year cold iron and lived near the Yin Thunder Bell like an earthbound spirit (地縛靈). Not only was his martial prowess exceptional, he had also been born with an extraordinary sense of smell. If anyone approached, he noticed at once and warned the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Thus, it was impossible to steal the sacred artifact while avoiding the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s eyes.

In such a situation, how had I managed to bring out the Yin Thunder Bell?

There was one moment, exactly once a year, when the iron grate was raised and the Pungcheon Cult Leader briefly left his post.

It was the day of the Blood God Rite.

On that day, according to the prescribed ritual, the Pungcheon Cult Leader left the throne and performed the rites at the great training ground.

At that time, the shackled master guarding the Yin Thunder Bell would, according to custom, strike the bell thirty-six times. What I targeted was precisely the moment he struck the final, thirty-sixth toll.

After the last toll, the time it took for the Pungcheon Cult Leader to return from the great training ground to the throne was one tea’s span (一茶頃).

In that brief interval, barely long enough to drink a cup of tea, I accomplished a miracle.

Because I had even practiced my escape dozens of times, I was able to flee while evading the pursuit of the Pungcheon cultists.

Investigating, infiltrating, winning people over, fighting, practicing… Countless things from that time flashed past like a revolving lantern.

What I was aiming for this time was kept in the same place as the Yin Thunder Bell. Since it would be simpler than stealing and carrying away the bulky Yin Thunder Bell, this trip to the Pungcheon Cult could be seen as a gift from my past life of hardship.

I stood for a while in front of the inn, watching the crowds come and go.

When the Blood God Rite was held, countless sects that had dealings with the Pungcheon Cult sent congratulatory envoys, so not only the inns but even the streets were packed with people.

That was exactly what I wanted. With this many people, no one would remember my existence. Of course, even in such a situation, I had pulled my bamboo hat low and thoroughly concealed my identity. I would come quietly and vanish without a sound.

At several shops in the marketplace, I bought a few items needed for camping, basic provisions, and a decoction pot for brewing medicine.

And the last place I arrived at was an apothecary (藥房).

“You want Gihwacho?”

The old man at the apothecary looked at me in surprise.

“Gihwacho is expensive.”

Gihwacho was a medicinal herb that grew only here beyond the frontier, and could not be obtained in the Central Plains.

“How much is it per geun?”

“How much do you need?”

“I need two geun.”

“You’ll have to pay eighty taels per geun. I don’t know if you’re aware, but it’s a very rare herb.”

“Understood. I’ll buy two geun at that price.”

“But what are you planning to use Gihwacho for?”

Since Gihwacho was not an herb commonly used, the old man looked at me once again.

However, I had been wearing my bamboo hat pulled low from the moment I entered, so he could not see my face. My voice, too, was different from my own.

“I don’t know either. I’m only buying it because my superiors ordered me to.”

The old man asked no more and brought out the Gihwacho. After letting me inspect the herb, he wrapped it carefully and handed it over. I paid the old man and left.

After leaving the apothecary, I went to another one far away from that place. This time, what I sought was Gwiryong wood and Gyuhwa.

After obtaining those two medicinal ingredients there, I then went to an apothecary in another distant village and purchased thorn apple and white sandalwood.

There was only one reason I was obtaining the herbs from several different places like this: to ensure no one else would know what could be made by gathering these herbs together. Buying them all in one place would not necessarily cause a problem, but this was preparation for the one-in-ten-thousand chance.

There was no harm in being cautious. In ordinary life, inconvenience returned as financial loss, but in the martial world, inconvenience demanded one’s life.

After obtaining all the herbs I needed, I went straight up the mountain.

Deep in the sparsely traveled mountains, the cave where I had stayed in the past remained unchanged. I cleaned it thoroughly, then began brewing the medicine.

Depending on the ingredients, some had to be decocted separately, while others had to be brewed together. It would take at least several days.

This medicine was a secret drug capable of numbing the sense of smell of the master who wore shackles. They had said he had been held captive his entire life since youth, so he was surely still there even now. Back then, the time and cost it took to uncover this secret method had been no small matter.

While brewing the medicine, I sat cross-legged and cultivated the Heavenly Demon Protective Art.

I had nearly died when I first learned it, but now the Heavenly Demon Protective Art had become an old friend I could no longer do without. The more I cultivated it, the more I felt the uniqueness of the Heavenly Demon Protective Art.

Was it because of the characteristics of the Heavenly Demon Protective Art? When danger came, it awakened me and activated on its own, so at times the Heavenly Demon Protective Art often felt like a living martial art. I knew it would become even more powerful when combined with the Nine-Flame Demonic Art, so I could only hope that day would come soon.

After training until late into the night, I left the cave and looked up at the stars in the night sky.

Before my regression, I had stood here and looked up at the night sky as well, but there was one feeling now that was decisively different from then.

Back then, I had only the single-minded determination to return. Now, in the place of that determination to return, there was longing for the people I wanted to see. In particular, I wondered whether Seo Daeryong was learning martial arts well from the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

Though both of them would deny it, they were subtly alike.

‘He must be doing well, right?’

* * *

On the first day of training, Seo Daeryong was waiting for the Blood Heaven Saber Demon in the yard.

He had arrived on time, but the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had yet to come out.

To think he would be learning martial arts from the Blood Heaven Saber Demon—his heart truly felt as if it might burst.

He swung the saber by himself.

Without using internal energy, it was still far too heavy.

Thinking there was no need to waste his strength for nothing, he waited for the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

When the man still did not come out after a long while, Seo Daeryong cautiously approached the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s quarters.

He was peering inside through a crack in the closed window when he felt a chill beside him.

Startled, he turned and saw that the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had appeared at some point and was peering through the window alongside him.

“What are you snooping at?”

“Euaak!”

Seo Daeryong screamed in shock.

“I-I’m sorry! I was waiting, and wondered if you might be inside.”

“I hear the Second Young Master has left the cult?”

“Yes.”

“They say it will take about two months?”

“You heard.”

“That’s plenty of time for someone to go missing, or perhaps to die in an accident.”

Seo Daeryong quickly said,

“The Pavilion Master is such an intelligent person that I doubt he would believe such a tragic incident could occur in a mere two months.”

“Because he is intelligent, he would quickly forget even a tragedy that had already happened.”

With his long, slender fingers, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon made a gesture as if gouging out eyes.

“I will never snoop again!”

“Then what are you doing here?”

“Ah, yes. I’m going!”

Seo Daeryong hurriedly ran to the center of the yard.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon taught Seo Daeryong how to grip the saber and how to swing it.

Then, as he went back inside, he said,

“A thousand times!”

Seo Daeryong was swinging the saber, his entire body soaked in sweat.

“Eight hundred fifty-three, eight hundred fifty-four…”

The saber was so heavy it felt like his arms would fall off.

‘Ugh, I can’t do any more!’

Seo Daeryong flopped onto the ground. Even if the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was watching, there was nothing he could do. He did not have the strength to move a single finger.

Normally, this would be the time he would be lying on his bed at home, eating snacks and reading a book.

‘Did I do something pointless?’

Once his body actually began to suffer, regret came flooding in.

‘No, no. I’ve already made up my mind!’

If he failed to complete his training and got driven out on the very first day, he would have no face to show Geom Mugeuk.

Seo Daeryong sprang to his feet and swung the saber. In that way, he barely managed to complete a thousand swings.

“I did it!”

Sitting down on the ground, Seo Daeryong looked down at the saber in his hand.

This cold, weighty feeling of the saber! Yes, if he followed this feeling, it would surely guide him to a better life than one spent rolling around lazily.

Just then, the window opened and the Blood Heaven Saber Demon appeared.

“If it’s too hard, quit. I won’t stop you.”

Seo Daeryong shot to his feet. His arms felt as if they would fall off, but he spoke firmly.

“I won’t quit.”

“Why?”

“Because I’ll never get a chance like this again in my life.”

“I will never pass down my unique martial arts to you.”

“I don’t even hope for that. The chance I spoke of is the chance to learn martial arts from you, Demon Venerable. Later, when I have children, I’ll tell them. Your father, you see, learned martial arts from the Blood Heaven Saber Demon in his youth. That alone is enough. As a martial artist, having that much of a memory is enough.”

“So you do intend to marry.”

“Yes, I want to.”

“Why?”

“I want to raise a child. And so I want to give them the fatherly love my own father never gave me, not even once.”

There was a story and sorrow in his words that could be felt.

“What if your child hates you the way you hate yourself?”

“Then… would you train my child like this too?”

At Seo Daeryong’s words, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon gave a faint laugh.

Thinking he had made the Blood Heaven Saber Demon laugh, Seo Daeryong’s mood brightened for no reason, and he smiled widely.

As the Blood Heaven Saber Demon closed the window, he said,

“A thousand more!”

Chapter 53: No Variables Allowed.
---------------------------------
“Hah, hah.”

Seo Daeryong’s ragged breaths spread toward the night sky as he lay flat on his back on the ground.

The moon that had been shining so brightly was nowhere to be seen. He had begun training after his day’s work, before evening, and now midnight had long since passed.

‘I finally did it!’

When he had been in a state where it seemed impossible to swing even once more, he had swung another thousand times. To think he had swung this heavy saber two thousand times in a single day.

Seo Daeryong could hardly believe what he had accomplished. Of course, toward the end his form must have completely fallen apart, but he had gritted his teeth and kept swinging.

He thought he should get up and go home now, but Seo Daeryong could not move a finger. Sleep came pouring over him.

Seo Daeryong dreamed.

Geom Mugeuk was surrounded by enemies and in danger. Just as the enemies rushed at him while he was wounded—

A blast of saber qi shot out like lightning and swept through them. The enemies caught in the saber qi were reduced to bloody pulp and vanished.

Then, as though a single plum blossom were falling with lofty grace, he descended between the enemies and Geom Mugeuk. Now that he had become the greatest master of saber arts, he was holding a huge greatsaber like the Blood Heaven Saber Demon.

He looked back at Geom Mugeuk and asked with a faint smile.

“Are you all right, Lord Pavilion Master?”

A satisfied smile formed on the lips of Seo Daeryong, who was asleep.

“My right-hand man! You came!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, laden with surging emotion, Seo Daeryong turned toward the enemies and said handsomely,

“I am here now, so rest! Sleep deeply!”

Then a familiar voice came from somewhere.

“Shouldn’t you be getting up now?”

Seo Daeryong instinctively let those words pass him by and rolled over. He did not want to wake from such a pleasant dream. The dream of becoming a master and roaming the world was, after all, his true dream as well.

Seo Daeryong kept sleeping.

In his dream, after rescuing beauties in peril, he boarded a boat and left with them. He woke from sleep with a pleased smile as he watched the women engage in a subtle battle of wits to monopolize his love. Among them was his junior, Jo Hyang.

It was the first time in his life he had woken up smiling like this.

He felt so good. So good that he did not even want to get up… But where was this?

An unfamiliar ceiling.

No, not entirely unfamiliar.

‘No way?’

Seo Daeryong sprang up and screamed.

“Ugh!”

Quite apart from the wonderful dream, his entire body hurt as though it were about to fall apart. It felt as if someone had beaten him all night with a club.

Seo Daeryong looked around.

‘This place is?’

It was the very sleeping chamber of the Blood Heaven Saber Demon where he had drunk and fallen asleep.

‘Good heavens! Why am I sleeping here?’

He remembered swinging the second thousand times and lying down on the ground.

Fortunately, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was not in the room.

Groaning, he got up and carefully moved his feet.

When Seo Daeryong walked to the window, he flinched in surprise.

In the courtyard where he had swung the saber yesterday, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was swinging his saber. Seo Daeryong, who had been about to run out at once and greet him, stopped.

His gaze had been seized by the sight of him swinging the saber.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon swung the saber in the exact stance he had taught him yesterday. The way he held the saber was the same, and the way he swung it was the same.

At that moment, Seo Daeryong understood. The training he had been taught was the training the Blood Heaven Saber Demon himself had done when he first learned martial arts.

‘He didn’t just make me do it at random to make me suffer.’

Then, as if he had eyes on the back of his head, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, still swinging his saber, suddenly said,

“You think you can slack off because the Pavilion Master isn’t there?”

“Huh! Now that you mention it?”

The sun was already high in the sky.

“Aaagh! I’m late.”

Seo Daeryong hurriedly dressed and came outside.

“I’ll be going now.”

Seo Daeryong bowed deeply and was about to turn away, then turned back again.

“Thank you very much for letting me sleep here again.”

Even if the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had given him money or handed him a treasured saber, he would not have been this moved. Ah, of course he would have been happy, but the nature of the feeling would have been different from what he felt now.

For the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, was letting someone sleep in his own bed perhaps more difficult than giving them money? This feeling of being cherished by him could not be compared to anything else.

“Next time, if something like this happens again, please just throw me into any guest room. Just don’t kill me. Then I’ll see you this afternoon.”

At that, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon asked with an unexpectedly surprised expression,

“You’re coming?”

“Of course I have to come.”

“I won’t go easy on you because you’re sore. Today will be two thousand times too.”

At the words “two thousand times,” his entire body recoiled in horror and screamed that it was impossible, but his mouth said, “Yes!” After all, his mouth was not the part that would suffer.

After answering vigorously, Seo Daeryong ran off.

“I’m late! Late!”

He was so exhausted he felt as if he might die. He hated even imagining training again today, but strangely, he felt good.

He wanted to keep learning from the Blood Heaven Saber Demon. To be honest, he wanted to look good in his eyes.

* * *

The medicine I had been decocting over several days was complete.

I placed the prepared medicine into a vial the size of my palm. There were still five days left until the Blood God Rite.

Now that the medicine was made, I intended to check whether the secret passage was working properly today and scout all the way to the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s throne.

After descending the mountain, the place I arrived at was a wasteland not far from the Pungcheon Cult.

It was a place where a few withered trees and rocks stood, where poisonous insects and venomous snakes swarmed, and where trash blown in by the wind rolled about. Even if one stayed there all day, no one would come.

There was a large boulder there, and I operated the hidden mechanisms below both sides of the boulder in succession.

Srrrk.

Then the boulder opened, revealing a passage. I had admired it back then as well, but even seeing it now, it truly was a well-made mechanism. How could they create a passage that left no trace like this?

Once I entered the boulder, the door closed automatically.

When I operated the hidden mechanism from inside, a door opened in the floor.

I took out a night-shining pearl and descended into the underground passage. The passage was wide. It seemed to have been made so that the Yin Thunder Bell could be transported in an emergency. Thanks to that, I had been able to steal the Yin Thunder Bell through this place.

I took out the vial and applied the medicine to my face and hands.

If I applied this medicine, even a master with an exceptional sense of smell would not be able to catch my scent.

I slowly walked down the passage.

I stopped a considerable distance away from the secret passage leading into the room where the throne was. In the past, I had gone closer to investigate, but there was no need for that now. I had gained a way to examine things safely.

I closed my eyes and emitted my qi. A single strand of qi advanced along the passage. After turning a corner and traveling for quite a while longer, the qi reached the secret passage.

When I first learned this secret technique from Father, I had not known I would be able to emit qi so thinly and over such a long distance to use it for reconnaissance in actual combat.

The qi slipped out through a small gap in the passage.

The instant my qi entered the new space—

I felt an overwhelming presence on one side.

‘It’s the Pungcheon Cult Leader!’

As expected of a cult leader, the aura I sensed from him was far from ordinary. I quickly moved my qi in the opposite direction.

The person I sought next was the master sensitive to scent.

Sure enough, he was near the Yin Thunder Bell.

Once I had confirmed both of them, my qi disappeared. Since this was something I had already experienced once, there was no need to risk being discovered.

Having safely finished my reconnaissance, I returned to the mountain.

After finishing a meal of dried meat, I sat cross-legged and cultivated the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art.

For the remaining five days, I would not budge from the mountain and would stay shut in. If I wandered around for no reason and got swept by fate into some strange incident, problems could arise.

Not creating variables—that was the bare minimum courtesy owed to all my past efforts.

* * *

The day of the Blood God Rite dawned.

Before setting out, I looked up at the sky once and prayed that today’s affair would succeed.

When Father had told me of the two methods that could block the Soul-Capturing Art, I had thought in my heart that there was a method a hundred times easier than those.

Hoping that my thoughts would prove correct and that I could safely bring this matter to an end, I descended the mountain.

The road to the Pungcheon Cult was packed with people who had come to participate in the ceremony.

They were of many kinds. From the heads of great sects that had dealings with the Pungcheon Cult, to martial artists from small sects who wanted to at least show their faces, to merchants trying to sell goods to the attendees.

On the day the Blood God Rite was held, everyone had to go to the Pungcheon Cult on foot like this, regardless of status. This was the authority of the Blood God Rite.

I watched them walking from a distance, then headed for the wasteland where the secret passage was located.

As I went there, all sorts of thoughts crossed my mind.

What if the door suddenly doesn’t open? What if the event is suddenly canceled? What if they change it so the Pungcheon Cult Leader personally rings the bell? What if I was mistaken about how to obtain it?

Thus does anxiety lunge at us, trying to erode our souls.

At times like this, there is nothing to do but trust yourself. The belief that such misfortune will not come, and even if it does, that you will overcome it well. The best weapon against anxiety is faith in oneself.

Through the secret passage, I arrived near the room where the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s throne was and waited there for the right time to come. Of course, I had applied the odor-blocking medicine not only to my face and hands, but to my body as well.

When to move had already been decided. The moment the Yin Thunder Bell began ringing and had been struck thirty-six times would be the time for me to begin the operation.

How long did I wait like that in the darkness?

A faint bell sound began to reach me. I waited while counting the sounds.

When the bell had rung thirty-three times, I slowly moved and went below the secret passage.

When the thirty-fifth bell rang, I quietly opened the secret passage in the floor beside the throne and went outside.

The master bound in shackles was in the middle of striking the final bell. Because the Pungcheon Cult Leader allowed no one else to enter this place where the sacred object was kept, there was only the shackled man here.

The moment he struck the final, thirty-sixth bell—

I unleashed the Wind God’s Four Steps. I approached the shackled martial artist with Dark Shadow Step. Moving toward him soundlessly like a ghost, I struck his paralysis acupoint and sleep acupoint at the same time. Without even realizing he had been struck, he fell asleep standing.

It had happened in an instant, but it was a technique in which the highest level of skill had been displayed in succession.

After subduing him like that, I went to the Blood Buddha standing behind the Yin Thunder Bell.

The time given to me now was the time it would take the Pungcheon Cult Leader to return here—one cup of tea’s worth.

When I firmly pressed the closed eyes of the Blood Buddha, its eyes snapped open.

The Blood Buddha with its eyes open was terrifying.

When I pressed once more, the eyeballs protruded forward.

Inside the protruding eyeballs, they were about one-third filled with a white liquid.

This was the very reason I had run such a long road.

The root of the Soul-Capturing Art cultivated by the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable was the demonic arts of the Blood Cult.

The successor to that Blood Cult was none other than this Pungcheon Cult.

Father knew this much as well, but there was one thing he did not know.

Blood-Eye Pure Water.

It was holy water secretly passed down only to the cult leaders of the Pungcheon Cult.

If this Blood-Eye Pure Water was placed in the eyes, one could see the method to break the Blood Cult’s sorcery. One could discern the essence of sorcery with one’s eyes.

I had heard it had been created and handed down from ancient times to prevent rebellions within the cult, but because the method of its creation and preservation was passed down only to the cult leader and his successor, outsiders absolutely did not know of its existence. The person who had told me this fact was the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s son, Ling Baijun.

When I came here before, I had no time to put this in because I was busy fleeing with the Yin Thunder Bell.

Now, at last, it was the moment I obtained the Blood-Eye Pure Water.

I took out the eyes of the Blood Buddha. There was a small hole above each eyeball.

Drop!

One drop of the Blood-Eye Pure Water contained in the right eyeball went into my right eye.

Drop!

One drop of the Blood-Eye Pure Water contained in the left eyeball went into my left eye.

When the Blood-Eye Pure Water entered my eyes, they stung and burned as though they had caught fire. There was pain, but fortunately, it did not blind me or anything of the sort.

When I placed the eyeballs back where they had been, they sank inside, and the Blood Buddha closed its eyes again.

There was enough pure water remaining inside the eyeballs, so it could be given to the next successor without issue, and they would not even know that two drops of this Blood-Eye Pure Water had disappeared.

I entered the underground passage and, before completely closing the door, sent out two streams of finger wind.

Swish.

The shackled master woke from his sleep.

For a moment, he looked bewildered, but soon confirmed that nothing had happened around him and began doing his work. It was a move no one but a master of my level could possibly have pulled off.

I wondered what story that man had that left him bound like that, but even so, I was not in a situation where I could save someone whose identity I did not even know. He was not someone who belonged to the fate changed by my regression.

I quietly slipped out of the secret passage.

When the time came, I had intended to gather, one by one, the fortuitous opportunities related to the materials for the Great Art, but because of the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, I had obtained the Blood-Eye Pure Water sooner than expected.

Whatever the reason, there is always joy when one accomplishes something great. Especially when, as now, I had gained an ability I had never possessed before, that joy was difficult to put into words.

It was not merely because I had become able to kill the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. It was because I believed this new power would somehow be of help on my journey toward Hwa Mugi.

After leaving the secret passage, I could have enjoyed the Blood God Rite with the composure of a victor, but I did not look back and ran toward the main cult.

I wanted no variables, not until the very end.

Chapter 54: You're Not So Mysterious When Flustered
---------------------------------------------------
The night was quiet and desolate.

When I entered the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s residence, he was sitting by the window, reading a book.

“That is the least fitting sight in the world.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon started and lifted his head. Gladness spread across his startled face.

“I’ll have to scold the fellows standing guard.”

“Please don’t. I slipped in quietly because I wanted to see you in private, Elder.”

He looked me over, then asked with an expression of disbelief.

“Have you perhaps only just returned to the Cult?”

“Yes.”

“Don’t tell me you came to see me first after returning?”

“I’m sorry to ruin the moment, but Father went to bed early.”

“Even so!”

“Yes, that’s right. I thought of you first, Elder.”

As I said that, I shook the bottle of liquor I had brought. It was the liquor the Blood Heaven Blade Demon liked to drink.

We drank in the rear garden of his residence.

I sent away all the subordinates guarding the place so we would not be disturbed.

“Has it already been two months since you left the Cult?”

“Not quite two months.”

Because my realm in Rapid Step had risen during this outing, I had been able to shorten the time it took to return.

“Is Investigator Seo’s martial training going well?”

“That little runt is working rather hard.”

The words “rather hard” had come from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s mouth. That unfamiliar expression plainly showed what their relationship was like now.

“He may seem crooked, but he’s a child with a straight core.”

“He’s never once been crooked with me.”

“He’s also the kind to stretch his legs only after checking where he can lie down.”

He was probably working hard while also watching the elder’s mood, and even in the midst of that, he would still be saying everything he wanted to say. It seemed the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not dislike that sort of fellow all that much.

“By the way, I caught a glimpse earlier, and the book you were reading seemed to be a collection of poems and paintings. Are you truly reading poetry?”

“Why? Do I look like I’m not on the level to read poetry?”

“I don’t know about the level, but at the very least, it doesn’t suit you.”

“In my youth, I liked books as well. I merely lived for a while forgetting that.”

This gaunt, fastidious-looking man in his youth—it was difficult to imagine.

“What were you like when you were young?”

“Well… dark and gloomy.”

“You were similar to Investigator Seo, then.”

For some reason, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon readily nodded.

“What does it matter whether I was this way or that? They are days that have all passed anyway.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon emptied his cup. Without a word, I filled his empty cup. There were no words of comfort for regret over years gone by. I had experienced it once myself, after all.

“Did the matter you went out for go well?”

“Yes.”

“Another storm will be rising, then.”

What sort of confidence had he read from that single yes to say such a thing?

“How do you know what I went out to do?”

“It must be something that will raise a bloody wind.”

“That is a grave misunderstanding of me. I like peace.”

“I like peace too. That still, blood-reeking peace that comes after I’ve made everyone kneel with my own hands.”

I laughed and drank.

“It was nice going out after so long. Next time, come out with me to get some fresh air.”

“When I go out, the world is full of bastards I want to kill… I feel more at ease just staying in the Cult.”

He poured liquor into my cup and continued.

“I don’t know what you’re trying to do, but be careful. In a fight for succession, one misstep and it’s over.”

“If I misstep, what will you do, Elder?”

“What else? I’ll have to get in line with the First Young Master again.”

“How cold.”

“It’s only natural. If the body is dead, what’s the point of the wings flapping? I should go look for another body.”

If it were the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he would truly do that. After feeling regret for a day or two, he would connect himself to the First Young Master as though nothing had happened. What was surprising was that even imagining that, I did not find him particularly hateful.

That was precisely the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s strength. Was this person good or evil? Would he help me to the end or betray me? There was no need to agonize over such things. If I collapsed, he was someone who would leave; if not, he was someone who would guard my side.

“Please do me a favor.”

“What is it?”

“Please drink with me all night tonight.”

“That isn’t difficult.”

I rose from my seat.

“You said we were drinking. Where are you going?”

“I’ll buy more liquor and prepare some side dishes.”

“You can have the boys do that.”

“There’s something I want to buy myself.”

I intended to bring back an extraordinary dish for this drinking table. The dish I had prepared over the past two months.

Today, I had decided to kill the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. No one would imagine that I would kill him on the very day I returned from leaving the Cult.

And I would become the person who had drunk with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon all night. He would serve as witness to my movements.

“I’ll be right back.”

Leaving the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s residence, I unleashed the Dark Shadow Step and flew westward.

* * *

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was unusually excited today.

It was a Heart-Soul Great Art of the sort he had performed every time, yet strangely, his heart was beating fast.

‘Is it because it’s been so long?’

It was the first Heart-Soul Great Art he had performed since Geom Mugeuk had come, threatened him to stop the art, and left.

Because he had threatened him while bringing up the Heavenly Demon, he had been forced to halt the Heart-Soul Great Art for a while.

Of course, it would be fine as long as he wasn’t caught, but since the other party was already certain of the Heart-Soul Great Art, it left him feeling uneasy.

The news that Geom Mugeuk had left the Cult had instead deepened his suspicion. It had seemed like a ploy to put him at ease, then find evidence.

But his patience had reached its limit, and in the end, he resumed the Heart-Soul Great Art today.

Whenever he performed the Heart-Soul Great Art after such an interval, he always chose a child. At times like this, a young heart-soul was the most delicious.

As the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable descended the stairs leading to the secret chamber, he glanced back. For no reason, it felt as though something was following him, and today of all days, his mind would not settle.

But there was no way he was being tailed. The Black Shadow Secret Art, in which his own shadow guarded his back, was being deployed. He was certain of it. With the sole exception of the Heavenly Demon, there was no one in the Cult who could secretly stand behind him.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable entered the secret chamber.

The walls were filled with all manner of bizarre patterns and characters, and incense with a peculiar smell burned all around.

A child lay asleep on the altar at the center of the secret chamber.

A smile formed on the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s lips as he looked down at the child. Just as he reached out his hand to wake the child, someone suddenly seized him by the hair.

‘!’

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable tried to counterattack, but his opponent’s movements were faster.

Smack!

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s head turned as he was struck across the cheek.

Only after helplessly taking that slap was he able to swing both palms and break free from his opponent’s grasp.

As the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable leaped back, his opponent spoke coldly.

“I told you not to, didn’t I?”

Only then did the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable realize who had struck him. It was Geom Mugeuk.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable touched his cheek with an expression of disbelief. No matter that it had been a surprise attack, it was something impossible, shocking, and humiliating.

“How in the world did you get in?”

“When you’re flustered, you’re not mysterious at all.”

For an instant, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable flinched. Normally, he bewitched people with his voice. The sound would reverberate, repeat, and whisper.

But right now, he was simply an ordinary man next door, angry.

“Is this your true nature?”

Though he might well have burst into rage, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable took one deep breath and regained his composure.

“I gravely misjudged the Second Young Master. I deserved to be hit.”

The words “deserved to be hit” repeated as his voice reverberated. He had regained his usual self.

“Now that’s more like you. This makes killing you worthwhile. That look from earlier was disappointing.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable did not waver.

“Second Young Master, answer my question first. How in the world did you enter this place?”

“I followed you in.”

He would think my words were a lie, but my answer was the truth.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable always guarded his rear with the Black Shadow Secret Art. If the Four Steps of the Wind God had reached great completion, it might have been different, but for now, I could not use the Dark Shadow Step while avoiding his Black Shadow Secret Art.

However, with the Blood-Eyed Essence in my eyes, I could see through the Black Shadow Secret Art, and the moment I broke it, my Dark Shadow Step took effect. If anything, it was as though I had hidden within his shadow.

“What does it matter how I got in? What matters is why I got in.”

Even in this situation, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable flatly denied it.

“If you’re still going to prattle on about the Heart-Soul Great Art, leave. I know nothing of that damned art.”

“Then who is this child?”

“A pitiful orphan. I brought the child here to take in and teach martial arts.”

“You’re a coward.”

“What?”

This time, I imitated him.

“Coward, coward, coward…”

My voice echoed like a reverberation.

“Stop!”

At his shout, I snapped coldly.

“How long are you going to pretend ignorance? Does your courage only appear when you pull the heart from the body of the weak?”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s pupils instantly shrank to pinpoints.

The surroundings darkened, then brightened, and a new space was created.

I was standing at the edge of a cliff.

Whoooooosh.

The wind blowing from behind tried to push me down the cliff.

“Have you already forgotten my warning? I told you that if you saw me again, you would never return to your original world for all eternity.”

His voice drifted over, eerie as the wails of ghosts.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable created an atmosphere of fear, but I stood firm where I was and felt no fear at all.

“It seems you can’t endure it unless you frighten your opponent in this manner.”

“How truly astonishing. Even if you are the Cult Leader’s son, how can you show such spirit at that age?”

His pupils repeatedly expanded and contracted, busy searching through me.

“You came to kill me, didn’t you?”

“Yes.”

“What exactly are you relying on?”

The words “relying on” repeated as though echoing. This echo now was not meant to mock me, but showed that his heart was disturbed.

Then the echo abruptly cut off, and he asked.

“Did the Cult Leader send you?”

This time, the question contained no other noise whatsoever. It would be difficult for him to think I would act like this on my own.

“If Father meant to kill you, he would have come in person. Don’t you know Father’s temperament?”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable nodded, and the surroundings grew noisy again. His muttering, the sound of the wind, and all manner of strange noises mingled together. It seemed to reveal his inner world.

“It’s distracting, so let’s speak quietly.”

“It is the Second Young Master’s dying wish. I should grant it.”

The surroundings darkened, then brightened once more.

He and I were sitting face-to-face in a pavilion with a beautiful view. On the tea table between us, even lukewarm tea had been set out.

“Second Young Master.”

His original voice—he had returned to being an ordinary man.

“If we must kill each other, then for the last time, let us speak frankly.”

“Very well.”

“We are demons. Demon Venerables, at that, who reign above all demons. Are our lives so easy that we can afford to give the weak a choice? What is wrong with devouring the hearts of those feeble things and becoming stronger? Is greed not our right? Every choice I made was for the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!”

If Hwa Mugi had not come and I had continued living in the Cult, perhaps I might have pretended not to see this Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. If it meant the Cult could become stronger, perhaps I might have used that justification as an excuse and forced myself to look away.

But my fate had changed. The me of that time and the me of now were undeniably different people.

I had become a person who could not sit at the same table to drink tea or liquor with someone who sought to grow stronger by tearing out a child’s heart. I had become a person who could not breathe the same air as him.

“That is a lie.”

“What?”

“The reason you stole hearts was not for the Cult. It was solely so you could survive longer. It is so now, and it will be so in the future.”

If he had truly been someone who cared for the Cult, he would have shown signs of seeking revenge after Father died. But his choice had been to survive the longest among the Eight Demon Venerables. Into his long, tenacious lifespan were melted the lives of thousands of innocents.

“For yourself alone, and again for yourself alone… you became a monster.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable stared at me and came to one conclusion.

“A mere brat of barely twenty could not understand me this accurately. You could not evade the Black Shadow Secret Art and follow me in, nor could you lay a hand on my body. You are… not the Second Young Master.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s pupils, which until now had only repeatedly expanded and contracted, began to change color. From his eyes, dazzling, resplendent waves of purple began to surge.

Purple light shot from his eyes into mine, burrowing toward the soul that lay beyond.

He asked. His question came deep and heavy, like a resonance from the abyss.

“Who are you?”

Chapter 55: I Know You.
-----------------------
The purple radiance signaled the use of a forbidden demonic art.

The Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art, which forced a truthful answer from one’s opponent, had been unleashed.

The Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art was a forbidden martial art that could not be used on members of the cult unless the Cult Leader granted permission.

Because the martial art itself could read an opponent’s mind and even draw answers from them, there were far too many ways it could be abused.

And yet the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable had broken the taboo and cast the Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art.

All high-level ghost arts practitioners could use the Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art, but its success rate differed according to each practitioner’s skill and internal energy. The higher their skill, the deeper the purple hue.

The dazzling purple light made it clear that the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art could draw out even memories the target himself did not recall.

“Who are you?”

“I am Geom Mugeuk.”

“How do you know about me?”

“I investigated you. From your personality to everything else.”

The Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art, which infiltrated through the eyes, had no effect on me after I had taken the Blood-Eye Essence.

Even so, I answered as though I had been caught in the Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art. By seeing what he asked, I too would learn something about him.

“Is the Cult Leader behind this matter?”

“No.”

Relief flashed across the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s face. As expected, the one he feared most was Father.

“Why did you come?”

“To kill you.”

“Did you tell anyone you were coming here?”

“I told no one.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable released the Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art. It seemed he had decided that if the Cult Leader had not ordered it, there was no reason to keep expending internal energy on the art. The internal energy and mental strength consumed by the Mind-Reading Soul-Stealing Art were greater than any other sorcery.

From his question about whether I had told anyone I was coming here, I could tell.

He had decided to kill me.

“Second Young Master, as of today, you will disappear from your world.”

“Never mind me. Do you think you can handle the aftermath after killing me?”

“Why would I handle the aftermath? The Blood Heaven Blade Demon will. I won’t be the one killing you. You will die by his hand after trying to kill him.”

He meant that he would cast a soul-seizing spell on me and order me to kill the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Since he believed I could not defeat the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, his conclusion was that I would ultimately be killed by him.

“That is so like you.”

“What do you mean, like me? Why do you keep speaking as though you know me so well? You investigated me? The very fact that you think you can kill me means your investigation was worthless.”

The moment he finished speaking, the world spun round and round.

Lights so dazzling and intense that I could not open my eyes came flying at me, and before long I was standing alone in a dark space. Someone whispered beside my ear.

—Kill the Blood Heaven Blade Demon!

Those words repeated endlessly.

At first, it was in the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s voice. Then in Father’s voice, Ian’s voice, Seo Daeryong’s voice. The words repeated in every voice I knew. I even heard my own voice, and the voice of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Even when I covered my ears, those words dug into my head. It was as though the command was being hammered straight into my brain. My head hurt as if it would burst.

Even with the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art activated, it was this bad. If I were an ordinary person, I would already have fallen under the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s spell.

I looked around.

In the pitch-black darkness, I saw a bluish light far away. Instinctively, I felt that light was the way to break this soul-seizing spell. I walked toward it.

The closer I got, the larger the light became, until it was big enough for a person to pass through.

Without hesitation, I walked into it.

Outside, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was standing there. He stared at me with a startled expression.

“How did you open your eyes on your own?”

It seemed the act of walking out through the light was, in reality, the act of opening my eyes.

“You should never be able to open your eyes before the soul-seizing spell ends.”

“It seems I’ve grown quite fond of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. I don’t want to kill him.”

Once again, the surroundings spun round and round as an intense light flew at me. I was in the darkness again.

—Kill the Blood Heaven Blade Demon!

It was an even more powerful soul-seizing spell, into which he had poured more internal energy. If the first spell had hammered the command into my brain with a small hammer, this one was pounding it in with a large one.

But the bluish light still existed, and I was able to escape through it. Even after coming out, my head was dizzy enough to feel as though it would split apart, so I could tell just how powerful the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s spell was.

“Why in the world are you not falling under the soul-seizing spell?”

“I told you, it’s because of my deep bond with Elder Blade Demon.”

“Nonsense!”

“You’re anxious, unlike yourself.”

“Then I suppose I, who have no such bond, will have to step in personally.”

The next moment, the surroundings darkened.

Something wriggled in the darkness.

The floor heaved, and pitch-black shadows raised themselves up. Their eyes and mouths were split long, and their limbs were stretched out in a grotesque fashion—monsters that chilled the liver and guts with a mere glance.

Those things, as though they had crawled up from hell, rushed at me.

I cut them down with the Black Demon Sword.

Bluish lights were marked like dots on the bodies of the pitch-black monsters. Some were on their heads, some on their bellies, and some on their arms. The Blood-Eye Essence showed me precisely how to break this sorcery.

When I stabbed the lights accurately, the creatures turned into clusters of white light and vanished. Had I not struck their vital points precisely, they would have been evil spirits that never died and attacked their target to the end. They were enemies that could never be stopped by mere martial prowess alone.

After I cut down every monster, the dark surroundings brightened, and I saw the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable standing there with a grave expression.

Without saying a word, he unleashed the next sorcery.

When it darkened and then brightened again, I was startled.

I was standing on a massive log in the middle of the sea.

It was real seawater. It was surely an illusion created by manipulating the human mind, but it was also a real sea in which I would die if I fell.

Then came the sound of waves from far away.

When I turned my head, something was bearing down on me from the distance. Astonishingly, it was a gigantic tidal wave.

Swaaaaaaaaaah!

The wrath of nature, which human strength could never hope to block.

I could clearly understand why the orthodox faction considered the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable the most troublesome opponent. How could a mere human possibly overcome an illusion this immense?

The tidal wave surging closer was as high as a cliff. It was so high that its end seemed impossibly distant.

But nowhere on the tidal wave could I see a way to break the spell.

There was nowhere to dodge, so I was swept away by the wave.

A tremendous impact struck me, and I sank into the water as though being crushed. If the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art had not been active, I would have lost consciousness from the shock.

Regardless of my will, I was tossed about underwater.

How much time had passed?

“Pwah!”

I pushed my face above the surface and exhaled the breath I had been holding.

Just as I thought I could finally live—

Another tidal wave began surging toward me from far away. If this tidal wave kept repeating, even the greatest master would eventually be exhausted and die. Before that, I had to find the way to break the spell and escape.

I looked around, then dove underwater to search for a bluish light. But beneath the sea, there was only darkness.

I was swept away by the second tidal wave as well. After minimizing the impact with the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art, I raised my head above the water again. No matter how much I looked around, there was nothing but the boundless sea.

Searching this vast sea for a way to break the spell seemed nearly impossible.

Where on earth could it be? There were no seagulls, no rocky islands—truly nothing but seawater and me in this endless sea.

‘I need there to be something if I’m going to find it.’

At that very moment, a thought flashed through my mind. Now that I thought about it, there was one thing.

‘Surely not?’

I launched myself out of the water. Kicking off the seawater, I flew upward and searched for that one thing. But I failed to find what I was looking for and was swept away by the third tidal wave.

Before the fourth tidal wave came, I once again flew up above the sea and diligently searched for that one thing.

And in the end, I found what I had been looking for.

‘Please! Let this be it!’

What I found was the log I had been standing on when I first entered this place.

When I turned the log over, there was a bluish light shining on its underside.

The Blood-Eye Essence had always shown me the way to break a spell accurately and quickly, but this time, the darkest place had been beneath the lamp. In the vast open sea, the very thing that had been protecting me was the way to break the spell. It made sense.

Or perhaps the light had originally been shining on top, but as the log turned in the waves, the light ended up facing downward.

Just before the fourth tidal wave struck, I cut the log with the Black Demon Sword.

At that very instant—

The tidal wave that had been surging in vanished as though it had been a lie, and at the same time, the sea disappeared.

It must have been his hidden trump card, but the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable did not show me any despair.

A new space immediately unfolded. The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was pouring out the lethal secret arts he possessed.

Around me stood some thirty mirrors.

My life was reflected in them.

There was a mirror showing me as a child, one showing me as I was now, one showing me in middle age, and one showing me as an old man. It was as though the mirrors were showing my entire life in intervals of three or four years.

I slowly walked forward and looked at myself in the mirrors. Most of the ages were ones I had experienced at least once before my return, but from a certain point on, they showed ages even I had never lived through. In particular, the me reflected in the last mirror was so old that it was difficult even to recognize him as myself.

I was on the verge of death.

I asked the me in the mirror.

“What do you regret so much?”

The me in the mirror was withered and dry, lying alone in a large room, staring blankly at the ceiling with hollow eyes. There was no one to accompany me in death. It was truly a lonely and desolate death.

This is not my future. It is an illusion created by the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. Perhaps someone with a quick temper would have broken this mirror. And if they had, the situation would likely have grown even worse.

I reached out and slowly stroked the old man in the mirror.

“You lived with so much blood on your hands. Did you expect a happy death? That is greed, Mugeuk. If you killed Hwa Mugi and saved the people who should have been saved, then even a death like this is fine.”

I comforted myself. What would my actual death be like? Would I die alone and desolate like that? Or would I die smiling, comforting those left behind?

“It’s all right, so go in peace. I know you.”

I stroked the me in the mirror, but perhaps I was actually comforting my present self. Telling myself it was all right, that I was doing well.

I returned to the front and stood before a mirror showing my youth. It was me from a few years later, and a bluish light was hovering around the mirror.

“Now then, shall we leave?”

Crash!

I swung the Black Demon Sword and shattered the mirror.

Beyond the broken mirror, I saw the startled Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.

“How did you know it was that mirror?”

Among the dozens of mirrors, only one was a mirror through which I could leave that place.

“If you broke the wrong one, the number of mirrors would double. If you broke another wrong one, they would double again. You would never escape from there for eternity.”

“I’m lucky.”

“You escaped because you were lucky? That is not something luck can break!”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s expression had completely hardened. His surprise had now turned into shock, and he had reached the one conclusion that must have hovered in his mind throughout each failed attack.

He asked in a trembling voice.

“Did you truly learn the Nine Flames Demonic Art?”

Before I could even answer, he decided that I had.

“That insane Cult Leader! He already chose his successor without telling us?”

That was how he misunderstood the situation. He had no choice but to. There was only one case in which his sorcery would not work.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable burst out in fury.

“Damn it! He uses his own child as the blade to kill me? That filthy bastard! The Cult Leader never once showed his true thoughts. Always striking people in the back.”

“Wait. Let’s clarify one thing. Aside from this matter, when has Father ever struck you in the back?”

“What?”

“You said he always strikes people in the back. Tell me about another time.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable said nothing.

“Father said the person the orthodox faction fears most is you. That means he holds you in high regard. Would he really have struck someone like you in the back? I don’t think so.”

“What are you trying to say?”

“I’m saying don’t run your mouth just because you’re angry. Father hesitated until the very end about killing you. Perhaps I might even lose points with him over this matter.”

“Then why are you doing this?”

“I thought about it carefully. Farming, doing business, attending academies, going to martial halls. They were just ordinary people living decent lives. But one day, without even knowing why, they were dragged here and had their hearts torn out while still alive? Wouldn’t that be too unjust?”

“Life is unfair by nature. If they feel wronged, they should become masters.”

“Then today, you can take all of that injustice upon yourself.”

Even though he misunderstood that I had learned the Nine Flames Demonic Art, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was not cowed.

“You said you investigated me? Yes, you did well. I am a man who has lived with a desire to live long. Did you think a man like me would not have anticipated that a day like this might come? The day the Cult Leader betrayed me and came to kill me?”

He still believed Father had orchestrated this situation.

“Do you know why I immersed myself in the Heart-Soul Great Art? Do you think it was just to gain a little internal energy?”

“Was it not?”

“Of course not.”

A black, smoke-like energy began rising around the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s body.

“Did you say I became a monster? Have you ever even seen a real monster?”

Within the black energy, he began to change. It was a transformation in the truest sense. His body grew larger, and his skin turned black. From his long, slit eyes flowed a pure white radiance, and his ears rose like those of an evil spirit. Within his chest, a large red heart could be seen pounding.

Was that an illusion, or was it real?

Transformed, he exuded a completely different presence from before. It was cold and solid. I grew tense. This demonic art was a lethal move prepared against the Nine Flames Demonic Art. That meant it was correspondingly powerful.

“I will tear your precious Nine Flames Demonic Art to shreds!”

The demonic energy he released swept through the surroundings. It was not mere demonic energy, but Dark Demonic Energy.

Chapter 56: Live Long Then.
---------------------------
The Dark Demon Art is a forbidden demonic art.

Compared to ordinary demonic arts, it is far more tyrannical and ferocious, but its side effects are just as great as its power. Most who master the Dark Demon Art are consumed by the art, fall into qi deviation, commit endless slaughter, and meet their end. Thus, the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult strictly forbids learning the Dark Demon Art.

Dark Demonic Qi is the demonic energy produced when one masters the Dark Demon Art. Because that aura is so overwhelmingly powerful, I tried to break his momentum first.

“Why did you learn such trash?”

“I suppose you’ll still be saying that when you’re buried in trash and dying.”

“Then I came here for nothing. If I had left you alone, you would have died from the side effects of the Dark Demon Art anyway.”

“I won’t die from side effects. I succeeded in blocking them with the internal energy I obtained through the Heart-Soul Great Method.”

Anticipating that this would not be an easy fight, I drew my Black Demon Sword.

When Seophon Majon stretched out his hand, black energy coiled around it.

As the energy faded, a black sword rested in his hand. Even at a glance, it was no ordinary blade.

“I will kill you, and someday kill your father with my own hands as well, taking his place.”

“You are too much of a coward to ever do such a thing. You would spend your whole life hiding your ambition just to preserve that life of yours.”

In truth, even in my previous life before the regression, he had never once revealed that he had mastered the Dark Demon Art. He had kept it until the end as a final trump card to protect himself.

We were enraged at each other, and we imbued our blades with internal energy to match that rage.

Sword clashed against sword in midair.

Boom!

An ear-splitting explosion erupted. Neither I nor Seophon Majon gave an inch. We were evenly matched in internal energy. If he hadn’t expended his internal energy earlier with multiple uses of his Soul-Reaping Technique, his transformed self would have overwhelmed me. Such is the power of the Dark Demon Art.

I faced him with the Soaring Heaven Sword Art from the very beginning. This was an enemy I had to fight using only my highest stances.

The First Form, Equalizing Heaven, was unleashed.

Shiiiiing!

A streak of sword light bisected Seophon Majon. At the moment a perfect line was drawn, when his waist should have been severed, he surged upward and swung his sword. A deep, booming cry burst from Seophon Majon’s throat!

“Human Annihilation!”

It was a sword strike as swift as light.

Six sword lines flashed into existence where I had stood, then vanished.

Had I not used the Flicker Step to evade, I would have died there, sliced into six pieces.

“Ghost Annihilation!”

Seophon Majon likewise made no mistakes that would give me an opening with a sloppy attack. A second stance, stronger and faster than before, flew toward me.

This time, the space where I stood was torn into twelve pieces. The sight of twelve sword lines appearing and vanishing simultaneously was truly a spectacle.

I dodged with the Flicker Step again, but it was even closer than before.

Before he could launch his third stance, the Soaring Heaven Sword Art’s Second Form, Changing Heaven, was unleashed.

My sword transformed twelve times before Seophon Majon.

Seophon Majon did not evade; he blocked each attack one by one with his blade. Sparks flew every time sword met sword.

As the transformations of Changing Heaven grew greater, the white light radiating from Seophon Majon’s eyes intensified. After fending off the final, twelfth transformation, he immediately launched a counterattack.

“Extermination!”

His third stance, growing ever stronger, and the Soaring Heaven Sword Art’s Third Form, Profound Heaven, were unleashed simultaneously.

Fortunately, I was not in the space that split into twenty-four fragments.

I had evaded, but Seophon Majon could not.

Shhhhraaaack!

Profound Heaven tore through Seophon Majon’s shoulder. But no blood flowed from his body. His blackened skin was as tough as armor. Relying on that resilience, Seophon Majon’s attacks continued. His roar, half-devoured by a metallic screech!

“Ruination!”

Forty-eight sword lines embroidered the air in splendor. The space occupied by his sword light grew ever larger.

It was powerful, but he had one weakness. Lacking practical combat experience with this demonic art, there was a monotonous aspect to his attacks. Had I been the one using that martial art, I would never have been able to evade.
