Astonishingly, he had realized the fact that Geom Mugeuk had killed the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

Yet for some reason, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had not spoken honestly to the Demon Buddha. It was a choice that clearly held another intention, one beyond merely thinking that if they had dragged along someone who did not want to come, they ought to endure at least this much.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader turned and walked in the opposite direction from where the Demon Buddha had disappeared.

* * *

That night, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon came to see me. He had sought me out directly after finishing the meeting of the Seven Demon Venerables.

“For now, you have escaped suspicion of killing the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.”

He looked at me with a surprised expression.

“How on earth did you avoid the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s eyes?”

“Did I not drink with you all night that day, sir? It was not something I did.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon shook his head.

“Truly, you… you must have been born with the fate of a dragon ascending to the heavens. Otherwise, such things could not be possible.”

“A dragon is still far off. I have only gone from a loach to about a snake now.”

“Then what kind of dragon are you to become, if the snake is already this much?”

When I gave a faint laugh, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon spoke as if warning me.

“But it is too early to relax. Among the Seven Demon Venerables, there are some who do not believe the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s words. In particular, the Demon Buddha, who is said to be his closest friend, seemed the least convinced. He should not even call him a friend.”

“Sir.”

“What is it?”

“If, by any chance, you ever develop other thoughts like the Demon Buddha, please tell me.”

At that, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s expression stiffened slightly.

“Why do you say such a thing?”

“I was just thinking how painful it would be if we kept smiling with such good feelings, only for the moment to come when we point blades at each other. So if there is anything you dislike, or anything that seems wrong, please tell me right away. Do not endure it for no reason and let anger build up. From what you have seen so far, have I not handled the areas where I am lacking rather wisely? Please do not ever assume you know how I will respond. Be sure to tell me.”

After staring intently at me for a while, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon opened his mouth.

“Second Young Master.”

“Yes.”

Words I had never expected flowed from the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s mouth.

“What will you do if the opposite case arises? If, in your eyes, I seem too unsatisfactory and lacking? If I am not someone suited to that new demonic path of yours? If I seem fine and pleasing now, but the more you see me, the more I feel like someone old, obsolete, and tiresome? If, when that time comes, I have no charm left at all, what will you do then?”

With a burning gaze, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon added one more question.

“Will you remove me as well?”

Chapter 62: Please Give Me What You Don’t Want to Give.
-------------------------------------------------------
It was unexpected.

I had not thought the Blood Heaven Saber Demon would ask if I intended to kill him too.

How should I answer? After pondering for a moment, this was my reply.

“My demonic path neither breaks tables in inns nor kills friends.”

For an instant, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon could say nothing.

After staring at me for a while with a complicated expression, he asked quietly,

“Am I your friend?”

“Yes.”

Even without elaborating, he would know I did not mean it in the ordinary sense of the word.

“You asked what I would do then? I’ll tell you plainly too. Without reading the room, without being considerate of you, Elder, I’ll just say it. This and that is why I don’t like it. You’re far too old-fashioned! It’s far too tedious. Where did all your old charm go? Find it! Then we’ll put our heads together and find a way for us to suit each other.”

“What if we can’t find a way? What if I still don’t change?”

“If you try and still can’t change, then there’s nothing to be done. We’ll have to live off our old memories. But if you don’t even try? Then we’ll have to have it out. No matter who’s in the wrong or what the complaints are, won’t we feel a little better after beating each other up?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon stared at me for a moment, then burst into loud laughter.

“Hahahaha.”

It was the most hearty laugh I had heard from him since meeting him.

After laughing like that for a long while, he suddenly stopped and said to me,

“There is something I once said to you that I must amend.”

“What is it?”

“That my life has been filled only with misfortune… It seems that may not be entirely true.”

In that moment, I clearly heard it.

The sound of the door to his heart opening a little wider.

I will continue to listen for that sound. Hearing the sound of that door opening often, from many people, will be the moment I grow, and it will become a weapon as important to me as the Nine Flames Demonic Art. Hwa Mugi, what sound are you hearing now?

* * *

Late that night, after even the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had left, an unexpected visitor came.

Surprisingly, it was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“What brings you here at this late hour?”

“I have something to say to you.”

“Please come in.”

“It is something private. Let us go this way.”

The surroundings darkened and then brightened, and we were in another space. It was the very same demonic art the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable had used several times. This demonic art of the Blood Cult lineage was known to him as well.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at me and asked meaningfully,

“What do you think? Does this martial art not look familiar?”

His eyes told me that he knew everything, so I should speak honestly.

“This is my first time encountering such a martial art.”

“That cannot be. Did you not see this space when you fought the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable?”

“What do you mean?”

At that, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression turned cold.

“You killed the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable in this space.”

“I do not know what you are talking about.”

I flatly denied it, but he continued making his claim with certainty.

“Because he died in the space where the Soul-Seizing Art had been deployed, it looked as though he had died of qi deviation. You cannot deceive my eyes.”

“Do you have proof?”

“I have no proof. I cannot even guess how you did it. But it is certain that you killed him.”

“Why do you think so?”

“Because I cannot read you.”

Inwardly, I was surprised. I had never looked down on the Pungcheon Cult Leader, but I had not thought he would believe I had killed the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable for a reason like this. This belief came from the confidence he had in his own instincts.

“You killed the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. I cannot read you either, so I, too, could die.”

“I did not expect someone wiser than anyone to make such a misjudgment.”

He truly had me figured out with precision.

“I am not trying to provoke you, so relax. I do not want to die in a place like this, as the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable did.”

Moreover, he did not overestimate his own ability.

“I will say it again. You misunderstand.”

Ignoring my words, he continued speaking.

“There can only be one reason why the Soul-Seizing sorcery did not work. You must have received the Nine Flames Demonic Art.”

“If you are so certain, why did you not tell the Demon Venerables? If you announced that my father secretly passed the Nine Flames Demonic Art down to me without the Demon Venerables’ knowledge, there would have been an uproar. Especially since I hear you are close to the Demon Buddha.”

“Where are there friends in the murim? People meet each other because they need one another at the time. The Demon Buddha is likely the same.”

“Then what do you need from me?”

“This matter is my goodwill. I came today to make sure you do not forget my goodwill.”

The moment I heard that, I understood. Something had arisen between the Pungcheon Cult Leader and the Seven Demon Venerables. Or perhaps, from the beginning, their relationship had not been as close as I had thought.

“Let us make one thing clear. As of now, Cult Leader, you have not shown me any goodwill.”

“What?”

“It is not that you will not tell the Seven Demon Venerables, but that you cannot. Because you have no proof. Will you go to the Seven Demon Venerables and say this? ‘Because I cannot read the Second Young Master, the culprit is the Second Young Master!’ Your pride alone would stop you from saying that.”

“At the very least, I could convey my suspicion that it might be you.”

“Then that would actually work in my favor. ‘His skill is enough to kill the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable? The Cult Leader may already have passed the Nine Flames Demonic Art on to him? There is no proof, so we cannot be certain, but somehow we cannot treat him recklessly.’ It will help me seize control of the situation going forward. You have, in fact, hindered me.”

“What?”

As if he had not expected such a situation, the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not refute me.

“If you show me goodwill, I will gladly accept it. But show me real goodwill.”

He had been pushed on the defensive for a moment, but even so, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was not flustered. He knew well the lesson life had taught him: never make any decision when the situation is unfavorable.

“Let us meet once more before I leave.”

The moment he finished speaking, the surroundings changed, and he left at once.

Watching his back, I smiled. Because rather than the scent of conspiracy, this thought came to me first.

“Our Cult Leader came to me of his own accord to offer me goodwill like this.”

* * *

In the large, splendidly decorated room provided by the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, there were only the Pungcheon Cult Leader, the sacred objects, and the shackled man.

“What do you think?”

The shackled man, who had been sitting before the Yin Thunder Bell like a stone statue, raised his head toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader. Words no one else could even imagine came out of his mouth.

“You fucking idiot, why are you asking me that?”

Insulting the Pungcheon Cult Leader like this was a crime worthy of having one’s limbs torn off.

But the Pungcheon Cult Leader smiled as if it were nothing out of the ordinary and raised one finger.

“You are the only one in this room. Who else would I ask?”

“Ask the reaper standing behind you when you’re going to drop dead.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader raised another finger.

“When I asked, the reaper answered thus: I will die after you die, so regrettably, you will not see my death.”

“Must be nice to live long, you pig bastard.”

At the man’s curse, the Pungcheon Cult Leader raised a third finger and laughed pleasantly.

“Now you have two left!”

The number of curses or instances of speaking down the man could direct at the Pungcheon Cult Leader was set at five per day.

Of course, it was not because the Pungcheon Cult Leader had some perverse taste that made him rejoice at being cursed at. Nor was it because this shackled man possessed a keener sense of smell than anyone else.

The reason the Pungcheon Cult Leader kept the man alive despite his excessive rudeness was that he possessed a mind more brilliant than anyone’s. At first, he had not known. He had not known this man would be someone so intelligent.

But after spending a long time in the same room with him, he came to know. The man’s true value was not his ability to smell scents, but his outstanding judgment and brilliance.

From the day he learned that the man was smarter than the Pungcheon Cult’s strategist, the shackled man became the true strategist of the Pungcheon Cult.

He received advice while being cursed. Most of the man’s advice was accurate, and in time, it returned as profit.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader did not try to make the man obedient by inflicting pain on him. That was because the shackled man had little attachment to life, and he knew that if pressured in that way, the man would gladly choose death.

He was a man with no attachment to life, but he did not commit suicide. Once, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had asked him the reason, but he never said a single word about it to the end.

“Why are you in such a bad mood?”

“Would you feel good if you were me?”

Watching the Pungcheon Cult Leader raise a fourth finger, the man grabbed his shackles and shook them. The clanking sound rang throughout the room.

When the Pungcheon Cult Leader flicked a finger, the surroundings darkened and then brightened, and the place changed. It was a green field.

There, the Pungcheon Cult Leader unlocked the man’s ten-thousand-year cold iron shackles. The key that could open the shackles hung around the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s neck.

Once the shackles were removed, the man’s expression eased as well.

Freed, the man ran about here and there in a frenzy like a puppy on a snowy day, then flopped down on his back and looked up at the sky.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader came over and looked down at him.

“You big-headed cult leader bastard, get out of the way! You’re blocking the sky!”

“It is a fake sky.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader raised the final fifth finger.

Having used all five chances, the man’s tone became respectful.

“Cult Leader, I miss that fake sky very much. Please move aside.”

His attitude changed in an instant, as if he were acting.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stepped aside and asked,

“I expressed goodwill to the Second Young Master, as you advised. But he demanded more concrete goodwill.”

The man was looking up at the sky. Through the long, disheveled strands of hair hanging down, his eyes were infinitely clear.

“Will the Second Young Master truly become the successor?”

“You said the Second Young Master killed the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable?”

“I am certain.”

“If such a person does not become the successor, then who would? A young man barely around twenty cannot kill the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.”

“It is remarkable skill, but…”

“Are you afraid? That the Second Young Master might kill you too, Cult Leader?”

If anyone else had said this, his head would have flown from his shoulders on the spot. But the Pungcheon Cult Leader nodded obediently.

“It would be a lie to say I am not afraid. If a man can kill a Demon Venerable, whom could he not kill?”

At some point, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had begun to speak frankly with the shackled man.

“There is another reason you must secure the Second Young Master.”

“What is it?”

“Do you truly think the Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult does not know that the Second Young Master killed the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable?”

“He knows?”

“Of course he knows. The Heavenly Demon of this generation is an extraordinary man. A man with grand ambitions too. Why do you think such a man has remained quiet until now?”

“Why?”

“Think! Do not merely waste food!”

“Was that not an insult just now?”

“It was not. My voice was merely loud.”

“Be careful.”

“I will. I was telling you to think.”

“I refuse. You do the thinking. If I think as well, what reason would there be for you to remain alive?”

The man looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader with an openly irritated expression. Even so, the words that came out were polite.

“When an extraordinary man remains silent, it means he is preparing a decisive blow.”

“Unifying the Central Plains?”

“That much, I do not know. In any case, this matter must either have been ordered by the Heavenly Demon, or it was something he knew about and pretended not to. Among the Eight Demon Venerables, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was the one the Murim Alliance considered the most troublesome. Just as the people of the Central Plains fear you, Cult Leader. And yet he allowed his son to kill the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable? This means the Heavenly Demon has made up his mind to replace the Eight Demon Venerables one by one. After replacing the Eight Demon Venerables with his own limbs, where do you think the Heavenly Demon will strike?”

“Us?”

“The Heavenly Demon of this generation will not wage war against the Murim Alliance while leaving us at his back. He will either drive us to the vanguard, or eliminate us and then begin the war.”

“Damn it!”

“A wind of change is blowing through the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. If we do not feel this wind, we die. The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable died because he was holed up in another world and failed to feel this wind. We must decide whether to hide from the wind behind the wall called the Eight Demon Venerables, or hide behind the wall called the Heavenly Demon.”

“You think it should be the Heavenly Demon?”

“The Heavenly Demon’s side will win.”

“Why?”

“Because there is no way a remarkable man like Geom Ujin would have begun a fight he had no chance of winning. So you must side with the Second Young Master no matter what. First, win his favor by giving him a gift.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader nodded.

“What should I give him?”

“Give him the thing you least want to give. Only then will we live. Now, no more advice!”

The man lying down shouted,

“Stop disturbing me and withdraw!”

Because this space was the only place where the man could feel freedom, he shouted while gazing at the fabricated sky.

“Aaaaaaaah!”

Unlike the man trying to release his stifled feelings even in this way, the Pungcheon Cult Leader stood with his hands clasped behind his back, lost in thought.

A short while later, having decided what to give, the Pungcheon Cult Leader said to the man,

“That is enough!”

The created space began to disappear.

The startled man sprang to his feet and shouted,

“No! Please! Just a little longer! You goddamn bastard! No!”

Despite that desperate cry, which even broke the rules, the green field vanished, and along with it, the man’s freedom.

Chapter 63: After Properly Giving What Was Due.
-----------------------------------------------
The Pungcheon Cult Leader came looking for me again.

He was waiting in the courtyard, just as he had when he first came.

"Will you come inside?"

"No need. Come to my place."

The surroundings darkened, then brightened again.

The created space was a desolate wasteland. My eyes saw the method to break out of this space. A bluish light hovered around the rock visible far in the distance.

"Desolate, isn't it? This is my homeland."

"The silence is pleasant. Please invite me here sometime."

"I shall."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been looking around at the scenery for a moment, spoke in a calm tone.

"I thought it over after returning, and you were right. I haven't yet done you any favors."

He took something from his bosom and showed it to me.

It was a secret manual.

Space-Time Shift Technique.

"It is a martial art that can create such a space. In this murim, it is a martial art that only those who have mastered the authentic demonic arts of the Blood Cult can execute."

"!"

"Since the Soul-Devouring Demon Lord is dead, I am likely the only one who can properly execute this demonic art."

Something truly unexpected had come out.

I personally disliked soul-devouring techniques or sorcery. But this martial art alone, I thought, was incredibly appealing.

"This space will save your life in an emergency. Because the moment you enter this space, you vanish from the original world."

As he said, the Space-Time Shift Technique could be used for various purposes. It could become a personal space, or serve as an evasion technique to escape a crisis at a decisive moment. If so, it would be like learning another Heavenly Demon Guardian Art.

From the moment I obtained the Yin Thunder Bell, the first ingredient for the Regression Grand Technique, followed by the Blood Essence, and now this Space-Time Shift Technique. I felt that I was fatefully bound to the Pungcheon Cult Leader by a deep connection.

"What do you think? Is this enough as a favor?"

"It is more than enough."

I reached out without hesitation. But the Pungcheon Cult Leader did not hand over the secret manual.

"If you want something in return, then it is not a favor, is it?"

When I showed my disappointment, he answered.

"Is showing my hand first not a favor in itself?"

Without weighing matters, the very act of presenting this excellent martial art was what he meant by a favor.

"What do you wish for?"

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader's gaze turned back to the desolate land. Watching the wind kick up sand into the sky, he spoke.

"I no longer wish to breathe this dust."

That remark meant only one thing. As my father had said, his wish was indeed to advance into the Central Plains.

"If you help with that first step, the Space-Time Shift Technique is yours."

I was tempted for a moment, but I boldly withdrew my outstretched hand. Because the moment greed is detected, any negotiation becomes unfavorable.

"Unfortunately, that is not something I can decide."

"I know. It is something for the Cult Leader to decide."

"It is a matter that not only Father but also the Demon Lords will step into."

Because if it concerns the Pungcheon Cult, the Demon Lords will definitely intervene.

"That is why. That is why I am trying to give you this secret manual."

"What specifically do you wish?"

"In two days, I have an audience with the Cult Leader. I intend to settle the matter of our cult's advance into the Central Plains that day. Step in with me then and persuade the Cult Leader. If you promise to do that, I will give you this secret manual."

He was saying he would give the secret manual regardless of the outcome. It was truly an unconventional condition. It was closer to a gift than a condition.

Even so, I did not accept it at once, instead showing a thoughtful demeanor.

"The method is wrong."

"What do you mean?"

"No matter how much the Cult Leader and I try to persuade him, Father will never permit the Pungcheon Cult's advance into the Central Plains."

"Then what must be done?"

The Pungcheon Cult's advance into the Central Plains.

Unlike the main cult's policy, which had absolutely forbidden it until now, I did not think it was a bad idea.

Unless we were going to strike and annihilate the Pungcheon Cult, there was no need to keep at our backs an existence that could become an enemy at any moment. As the ancients advised, keep your enemies closer than your friends, so I judged it better to keep the Pungcheon Cult close and manage it.

I said nothing.

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader extended the secret manual he was holding toward me.

"Can I hear the answer with this?"

"It is sufficient."

I accepted the secret manual.

I felt that the Pungcheon Cult Leader was no ordinary bold man. There was a clear difference between handing it over after hearing the content and expressing gratitude, and handing it over in advance like this.

'This man came determined to win my favor.'

Instead of pressing me for an answer, he explained about the secret manual. Since he was going to give me the secret manual anyway, he might as well give it when I was most agreeable; he knew how to make a deal.

"Of course, I created this space so quickly because I have trained for a long time. At first, even if you spent the whole day memorizing the incantation, you will not be able to create it. Even someone with outstanding talent in martial arts would take at least a few days, or more than ten days at most. Those with dull senses might take months, and some may never create it even once in their lifetime."

"How long did it take you to create it the first time, Cult Leader?"

"It took me three days."

Three days meant he had considerable talent.

"The time taken on the first attempt is important. The shorter that time, the more drastically it will decrease afterward. Unlike other martial arts, everything in this Space-Time Shift Technique depends entirely on the talent and comprehension of the one who executes the art."

Indeed, how long would it take me? With the body of the Heavenly Martial Body and my current level of martial attainment?

"How long can this space be maintained?"

"It depends on internal energy, but if forced while having reached grand mastery, about half a shichen? Even now, as I speak with you, my internal energy is rapidly depleting."

"Can this space be used permanently?"

At the word "permanent," the Pungcheon Cult Leader snorted. That hollow laugh carried the meaning that such a thing was impossible.

"If the amount of internal energy recovered here exceeds the amount required to maintain it, couldn't the space be kept open permanently?"

"That is indeed the dream of all who have mastered the Space-Time Shift Technique. Among the former Cult Leaders of the Blood Cult, not a single one has succeeded yet."

"So it is possible."

"If one possessed tremendous internal energy and was a heaven-sent martial arts prodigy, perhaps it would be possible."

That heaven-sent martial arts prodigy with the Heavenly Martial Body—that was me.

How long would it take me to create it for the first time? Could I truly maintain this space permanently someday?

"What if I take this secret manual and don't tell you the proper method?"

"Then you would become the Cult Leader with the worst relationship to the main cult and the Eight Demon Lords in history. Ah, you wouldn't become Cult Leader at all."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader handed the secret manual to me.

"Destroy it after you've learned it."

He had been trying hard to hide his reluctance, but he was clearly pained to part with it.

"I will do so. Instead, please interpret the formula for me just once."

"Interpret it?"

"This is not the original secret manual, is it?"

"Of course not."

"If a single character was copied incorrectly, there would be a problem."

"Surely you aren't doubting me? That would never happen!"

"If you interpret it, I will be able to grasp the true meaning."

For a moment, a displeased look flashed across the Pungcheon Cult Leader's face. But he soon readily accepted my request.

"Very well. I shall interpret the formula, so listen well. It is such a difficult martial art that you must listen with focus."

From his attitude, I could guess.

'This man thinks I won't be able to properly learn this martial art.'

Indeed, it was that difficult.

But as I listened to the Pungcheon Cult Leader's explanation of the formula, I came to perfectly comprehend the principles of the Space-Time Shift Technique. He could never imagine how deeply I understood it from a single explanation.

And so I received the transmission of the Space-Time Shift Technique. It was also the moment we became temporary allies.

"Now tell me the answer."

"If the Pungcheon Cult wishes to advance into the Central Plains..."

I looked at him and calmly added.

"Intervene in the succession struggle of Seohwanjin."

* * *

"Coming back after having my thing snatched away is exhausting."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader collapsed into his seat upon entering his quarters. He was mentally exhausted. Because he had to maintain the dignity of a Cult Leader while giving away something he did not want to give.

The shackled man was blankly staring at the Yin Thunder Bell alone, without even greeting him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader glanced at him, gauging his mood.

"As you instructed, I passed down the Space-Time Shift Technique to the Second Young Lord. I don't know if this was the right thing to do."

The shackled man answered without turning around.

"You did well."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader flinched at the polite words. He usually started with curses; when he began this politely, it meant he was in a bad mood.

'I must be careful.'

On such days, curses always burst out all at once at the end. Since he was human too, listening to the torrent of abuse sometimes made him want to beat the man to death in a single strike.

Such a thing must never happen. He couldn't imagine himself without the shackled man. His role as a strategist was important, but more than that, if the conversation partner he was with every day disappeared, he wouldn't be able to endure the emptiness.

"Are you saying that knowing what proposal he made?"

He had thought the man obviously wouldn't know, but the words Geom Mugeuk had spoken were reproduced from the shackled man's mouth.

"He told you to intervene in the Seohwanjin succession struggle, didn't he?"

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled, as if the man had been present.

"How did you know?"

"Because right now, that is the best method."

"You knew? What, you knew. Damn it! If you knew the method, why did you give the Space-Time Shift Technique to the Second Young Lord for nothing?"

The Pungcheon Cult Leader recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said.

"If the disciples of the deceased Soul-Devouring Demon Lord had all inherited their master's true legacy, that would be one thing, but if not, they will surely need the Cult Leader's help. You must make someone who trusts the Cult Leader and is willing to learn martial arts from him into your successor. If you can become the master of the next Soul-Devouring Demon Lord, that can be considered the first step toward advancing into the Central Plains."

It had sounded plausible. He had marveled, 'Ah, so there was such a method.'

But if the shackled man had known this as well?

"Then why give away that precious secret manual!"

He had acted magnanimous in front of Geom Mugeuk, but he had been dying of regret.

"Speak! Hurry up and tell me!"

He went beside the shackled man and shouted.

Then the shackled man looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader with an annoyed expression.

"What? What are you going to do about it? I'm the Cult Leader, the Cult Leader. You want to die today?"

Even as he shouted, the Pungcheon Cult Leader steeled himself internally. It was the moment for the curses to burst forth.

But the shackled man was calm.

"We didn't buy that method. And the Second Young Lord didn't sell it either."

"What?"

"What you bought was an opportunity."

"What opportunity?"

"The opportunity to bring the Second Young Lord to your side. Though I don't know how many more times we'll need to pay to become firm allies."

"Do you truly believe the Second Young Lord is such an amazing person? Do you truly believe he will become the next Heavenly Demon!"

"I don't know."

"What?"

"How can one know the future?"

"What kind of irresponsible talk is that?"

"You gave what needed to be given; what regret is there?"

"Because it's a waste! It's a waste, that's why!"

To the shackled man, he cast aside his dignity as a Cult Leader and laid bare his innermost thoughts. There were many times he wanted to kill him, but it also meant he had laid bare his heart to him just as much.

"What should I do now?"

"Become close with the Second Young Lord. Do as he says."

"I should do as you say. We don't know what sinister intentions the Second Young Lord harbors..."

"Enough!"

As the Pungcheon Cult Leader had feared, the shackled man finally exploded, and curses burst out all at once.

"You pathetic bastard! If you've clasped hands with someone riding high and even gave him a gift, you should think about riding that momentum. Why are you doubting and acting like a lunatic? It's because you harbor such idiotic thoughts that you end up dead. Do you know what those who die like dogs in the martial world have in common? They don't use their heads when they should, and use them when they shouldn't! They refuse to trust even if it kills them, and trust out of nowhere when they should be suspicious. That's why they die. Aren't you going to fold your fingers? Before I cut them off! Don't act tough today! No more questions! Unless you're really going to kill me, don't open that mouth!"

The Pungcheon Cult Leader pouted and folded all his fingers, and a new space opened.

Looking at the green field spread before his eyes and the clear sky without a single cloud, the shackled man spoke politely.

"Cult Leader, what do you wish to know? Please, just speak."

Chapter 64: Come When Told, Go When Told
----------------------------------------
After the Pungcheon Cult Leader departed, I tried the Spacetime Transference Technique for the first time. I was curious to see how quickly I could create a space.

I had no intention of refining this martial art for decades, so I merely hoped that the space I first created would be made as quickly as possible.

Fortunately, having listened to the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s explanation of the mnemonic formula, I had thoroughly grasped the profound secret contained within the Spacetime Transference Technique, so I circulated my true qi and unfolded the formula with the same skill as someone who had trained in this martial art for a very long time.

How much time had passed?

A clear, crisp sound rang in my ears, and I was standing in a new space.

It was a success!

The time taken was exactly two shichen. Even a talented person was said to take several days, so I had created a new space in an incomparably short time.

I was certain. Not even my father could create a space this quickly.

Two shichen.

If I kept reducing that time, the day would surely come when I could use this martial art as freely as the Soul-Seizing Demon Lord or the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Learning a new martial art always granted great excitement, but the thrill the Spacetime Transference Technique gave me was entirely different in nature.

I slowly walked around and examined the space. There was nothing in this place that had just been created.

Just as the Soul-Seizing Demon Lord had created cliffs and the Pungcheon Cult Leader had created barren lands, I too would later be able to create the space I desired.

But even now, it was not bad.

Perhaps because it was a space where I alone could exist, completely cut off from the world? Surprisingly, I felt a comfort in this empty place that I had never felt before.

A place where I could be apart from the responsibilities I shouldered, the struggles, the succession battles, the weaponry, human relationships, and everything else in the world—a place where I could have time alone without a single thought. That was why I liked it. Because a space of my own had been born.

But unfortunately, I could not enjoy this freedom for long. Perhaps because it was my first time, the drain on my internal energy was enormous.

Later, as my martial level rose, the internal energy required would gradually decrease, but at my current level, it drained away like water poured into a bottomless pot.

Dispelling the space was easy. As soon as I recited the mnemonic formula, the same crisp sound rang out and the space slowly vanished.

Having returned to the original world, I sat down and began circulating my energy in meditation.

To obtain that brief freedom, I had to fill my internal energy using a cultivation method and invest a period of two shichen, but I did not hesitate.

After filling my internal energy, I unfolded the Spacetime Transference Technique again. This time it also took nearly two shichen, but it felt subjectively a little faster than before. Yes, keep decreasing. So that I too could create this space in an instant.

So I unfolded the Spacetime Transference Technique, created a space and dispelled it again, created it again and dispelled it.

Until dawn broke, I was completely absorbed in creating my own space. The creation time was decreasing, ever so slightly.

Heavens! To think there was such an interesting martial art! That was my honest feeling.

* * *

At the moment Geom Mugeuk was completely absorbed in the Spacetime Transference Technique, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and Mabul were creating a rather heavy atmosphere.

Mabul had come to the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s residence late at night without any word.

Mabul, who had been about to sit facing him across the tea table, hurried over and stood before the shackled man. Because Mabul was short, his eye level was almost the same as the seated man.

The shackled man hung his head, so his face could not be seen.

Mabul bent down to try to see the shackled man’s face, but because of the hair hanging down in tangled strands, this too was not easy.

Mabul contemplated whether to lift the man’s hair with his tiny hand.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader watched this with a displeased expression. He was unhappy that Mabul was treating the shackled man like a spectacle.

But he did not voice any protest about ignoring his subordinate. Because if he did, that Mabul would surely start with something like, “Who is the one who bound him in shackles and didn’t treat him like a human in the first place?” and turn his stomach inside out.

Then Mabul turned around and abruptly asked.

“I heard you met the Second Young Master.”

A jaundiced, probing gaze flew toward him, but the Pungcheon Cult Leader was not shaken in the slightest.

“He may become the cult leader someday, so I had to meet him.”

“The cult leader will be the Eldest Young Master.”

Even without such a firm answer, the Pungcheon Cult Leader already knew. That Mabul was actively supporting the Eldest Young Master. If the Second Young Master had the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, the Eldest Young Master had Mabul.

“The situation does not seem to be going entirely that way.”

“What do you mean?”

“Hasn’t the Second Young Master ascended to the position of Hwangcheon Pavilion Master? No matter who looks at it, it seems the Heavenly Demon is grooming a successor, does it not?”

“Hehe, my friend. Spending all your time beyond the pass has dulled your insight.”

Mabul deliberately said something unnecessary to offend the other before continuing with his own piece.

“I see it the opposite way. He will make the Eldest Young Master his successor, and has simply given the Second Young Master another position. You should be content with that.”

“That may well be. It is a shame to return from this long-awaited trip to the Central Plains without seeing the Eldest Young Master.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader never revealed his true feelings to Mabul.

“Why do you support the Eldest Young Master?”

“Because the Eldest Young Master will become the successor. Must you ask something so obvious?”

“What gives you such certainty?”

“Just my intuition.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader knew. That Mabul was not someone who decided such weighty matters on intuition alone. He simply did not want to tell him matters related to the Eldest Young Master.

‘How can we possibly call each other friends like this?’

He was the same, of course. Because he would never tell him anything important regarding the Second Young Master.

“Look here, Cult Leader.”

“What is it?”

“Do not think of doing anything unnecessary. There are many eyes watching you on this side as well.”

“I do not know what you mean.”

“Why are you not returning?”

“Are you telling me to get lost now that business is finished?”

“That is not what I meant.”

Though the content was sharp, the tones of both men were soft and calm.

“Then leave me be. I am enjoying a rare trip to the Central Plains. Why? Do you mean to charge me for the room?”

“If you come to another’s house, you should stay quietly and leave. How can you defile the inner chamber?”

“Hehe. Whose mutt’s feces am I being mistaken for?”

“Advice from a friend. Do not cause needless trouble, and just return to the Pungcheon Cult.”

Honestly, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was inwardly taken aback. He had not expected Mabul to come and tell him to leave so brazenly. Setting aside their true feelings, they had outwardly maintained a relationship of courtesy and friendly pretense.

“Am I some existence that must come when called and go when dismissed?”

Still a calm tone, but the words themselves were a gleaming cold blade.

“Were you not?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression hardened, and the surrounding air grew cold in an instant.

Then Mabul burst into a broad smile.

“A jest, a jest. My friend, going all serious because of a little joke. You have really lost your touch, spending all your time beyond the pass. Enjoy yourself to your heart’s content. Let us stay here and play forever. Hahaha.”

As if he had never been angry, the Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed along.

“I suppose so. The desolate sandstorms have made me desolate as well. Such is the nature of the lands beyond the pass.”

“Let me buy you a drink somewhere with a nice atmosphere. Somewhere with beauties who could melt our Cult Leader’s desolation like snow.”

“Is it proper for a monk to frequent such places? Will your Buddha not scold you?”

“The Buddha I serve sometimes manifests as a Lustful Buddha.”

“Splendid.”

The two men smiled at each other.

“Well then, let us have a seat for a while!”

“Very well.”

Mabul laughed heartily and left the room.

Once Mabul departed, all trace of laughter vanished from the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face, and he asked the shackled man.

“What do you think?”

“He is anxious. He seems to have caught on that you intend to join hands with the Second Young Master.”

“Is that something to be so anxious about?”

“It means he is wary of the Second Young Master to that extent. He is keeping a close watch.”

“What should I do now?”

“Think for yourself. Do not get into the habit of always asking.”

“My life and the life of our cult are at stake right now. So do not drive such a hard bargain.”

The man turned to face him and rattled his shackles.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader made a reluctant expression, but ultimately opened a new space.

Gazing at the blue field, the man wore a bright expression as if he might take flight.

As he undid the myriad-year cold-iron shackles with the key hanging from his neck, the Pungcheon Cult Leader asked.

“Why do you like this place so much?”

For an instant, a certain tender affection flickered across the man’s eyes. But the emotion disappeared so quickly, and the Pungcheon Cult Leader did not see it because he was bent over unlocking the shackles.

“Cult Leader, try being bound in shackles all day long. Let us switch roles for just one day.”

“I must decline.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader gave the man a moment to enjoy his freedom.

Today, the man’s emotions were unusually calm. Perhaps that was why. Gazing at the field, he immediately offered the necessary advice.

“Mabul will find a way to make you return. He will probably use his own cult leader.”

But the Pungcheon Cult Leader had no intention of returning obediently. He intended to somehow establish a foothold for advancing into the Central Plains with this opportunity.

“What should I do?”

“Go find the Second Young Master and ask how to remain here. He will surely tell you.”

“Might you not also know the way?”

“Even if I knew, you must present a gift to the Second Young Master to receive it.”

“I cannot understand. Why must I cling to the Second Young Master like this?”

“Then who else would you cling to?”

For a moment, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was speechless. The Heavenly Demon disliked him, and the Eldest Young Master was supported by Mabul and the other Demon Lords.

Only the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had officially joined hands with the Second Young Master; the rest of the Demon Lords were either neutral or believed the Eldest Young Master would become the successor. He truly had nowhere to set foot.

“If you do not wish to cling to anyone, just hole up beyond the pass, coming when called and going when told. Then you can live comfortably in luxury for the rest of your life.”

It would have been less infuriating if he had spoken while cursing, but because he spoke politely, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was even angrier.

“Do not provoke my pride with such words!”

But the man paid no heed to the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s mood.

“The way to win this fight is simple. Choose well and believe until the end. Simple, is it not? So simple that it is actually difficult to put into practice. Losers never know, do they? That victory depends on whether one follows these simple principles.”

“Enough nagging! Enough lecturing!”

“If you do not think you can keep to this principle, then Mabul is right—we should go back.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been about to explode in anger and tell him to shut up, calmed his mind.

He had been several times more infuriated than with Mabul, but everything the man said was correct. So instead of getting angry, the Pungcheon Cult Leader revealed his innermost thoughts.

“I do not want to keep giving things to the Second Young Master. Honestly, it is a waste. An unbearable waste. I keep regretting giving him the Spacetime Transference Technique too. I could not sleep a wink last night, thinking I had given it away for nothing.”

No one would know that he, the supreme ruler of the martial world beyond the pass, had such a personality. And that he lived hearing such nagging.

“It is because you are narrow-minded and greedy.”

When he heard such words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader felt fury rising to the tip of his hair, yet at the same time felt a strange pleasure. He had lived hiding his true nature better than anyone, but the sense of kinship at having at least one person who knew exactly what kind of person he was.

“What can I do about feeling it is a waste? And yet you tell me to give more?”

“If you have chosen, you must believe. Give. Give everything.”

“It is a waste! I do not want to give a single thing!”

Normally, this would be the moment curses burst from the shackled man’s mouth. But today the man remained calm until the end.

“Let us just go back, wipe the dust from the sacred objects, and busy ourselves with paperwork. That perfectly suits a Cult Leader’s vessel.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader bit his lip firmly.

“I will break free. I am doing this to break out of this skull the size of a soy sauce dish! I will definitely establish the Pungcheon Cult’s main headquarters in the Central Plains. I will accomplish with my own hands what previous generations could not.”

He would rather have been cursed at, but the shackled man only let out a sigh.

“…I do not think it will work.”

After that, the Pungcheon Cult Leader fell silent for a long time, lost in thought. It was a day when he had been terribly furious, but the illusory space lasted until his internal energy ran out.

Chapter 65: If You Refuse, You’ll Be Dragged Beyond the Frontier.
-----------------------------------------------------------------
I went to the Maga Village sub-branch on business and stopped by Pungryu Tavern.

As always, Jo Chunbae greeted me warmly.

“Welcome, Pavilion Master.”

“I dropped in because I was feeling a bit hungry.”

“I’ll prepare your favorites and bring them up nice and tasty.”

Jo Chunbae, who had been about to hurry to the kitchen, suddenly turned back and asked,

“Pavilion Master. May I ask you something?”

“Ask anything you like.”

“Someone of your standing could go to a much finer tavern, so why do you always come to ours?”

“You still don’t know?”

“Please tell me.”

“The liquor is good, the food is good, the atmosphere is good, you bring me what I like without my having to say a word, and the owner is handsome. Do I need more reasons?”

“That is more than enough. I’ll make it delicious and bring it right up.”

He smiled broadly and hurried off to the kitchen, his steps light.

He would never know how important I considered this place. This Maga Village was the very foundation of the demonic path I intended to build.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader who was here now, and the Eight Demon Venerables as well, were not as important as Jo Chunbae and the villagers here. The moment I forgot this place, no matter how well I dressed it up, it would not be easy to find how I was any different from the Eight Demon Venerables.

A short while later, just as Jo Chunbae brought out the food,

a commotion broke out outside.

I went to the window and looked down. A man had fallen to the ground, and in front of him a large, burly man was raging. One glance was enough to tell he was heavily drunk.

“Why the hell are you glaring at me, you bastard?”

“I wasn’t glaring.”

“You were glaring.”

The brute did not hesitate to strike him.

“You lying bastard!”

The man being beaten had been walking with his family. His wife stepped forward to stop it.

“Please stop!”

“Get out of the way! A wench like you, bringing bad luck!”

The bastard did not go easy on her just because she was a woman. The woman, shoved roughly aside, fell to the ground, and a child of about six or seven ran to her.

“Mother!”

Seeing his father and mother beaten, the child was utterly shocked.

Even with the child there, the hulking man did not stop. Rather, when the child cried, he grew irritated.

“Shut up, you little bastard!”

The moment he raised his hand high to strike even the child—

Thud!

The brute was sent flying backward.

The man who had been lying on the ground had leaped to his feet and landed a blow on him.

His wife and child ran to him.

The woman was startled that her husband had knocked the brute down.

“How did you do that?”

“My fist moved before I knew it.”

The man who had thrown the punch looked bewildered as well.

“Let’s hurry and go.”

“Let’s.”

The family left the place, avoiding the brute.

After they had gone, the brute belatedly came to his senses and got up.

“That bastard! Where did he go? I’ll find him and kill him, his bitch, and his kid, all of them. Anyone know who that guy was? Talk!”

He grabbed a spectator by the collar and began causing a scene.

Then a voice came from the second floor.

“Is that really something worth going that far over?”

At those words, the brute looked up toward the source of the voice.

“Who was the bastard who just mouthed off?”

The person waving from the second-floor window was none other than me.

The drunken man did not recognize me. Among the lower-ranked martial artists, there were countless who did not know my face.

Behind me, Jo Chunbae whispered that the man was a bastard who usually caused all sorts of trouble in the marketplace. Even without his telling me, I could tell what the man’s usual conduct was like. Among the spectators, there were several who recognized me, yet none of them were warning him.

“I’m asking whether meeting someone’s eyes is a reason to beat a father in front of his child.”

“Where does a green little bastard like you get off lecturing me? And you bastard, I got hit too!”

That had been my doing.

If I had stepped forward and punished him myself, that child would have had to live the rest of his life remembering the sight of his father and mother being beaten.

So, as if moving an object through the air, I moved the man’s body and made him throw that punch. I wanted the child to remember his father as brave.

“You got hit once, so you’ll slaughter his whole family? Is that really something that should go that far?”

“Come down here! I’ll kill you instead.”

“I was planning to come down anyway.”

When I lightly leaped down, the brute seized that moment and threw a punch at me.

After slightly dodging the incoming fist, I grabbed his wrist and twisted it. Crack, crack, crack! In an instant, his arm twisted like wet laundry being wrung out.

“Aaaaargh! It hurts! You bastard. You!”

As he charged at me like a madman, I did not hesitate to twist and crush his other arm as well.

“Aaaaaaargh!”

He screamed that the pain was killing him. If he had not been dead drunk, he would already have fainted, but he was enduring it on the strength of the alcohol.

“Right? This wasn’t something worth going this far over, was it? Meeting someone’s eyes wasn’t a reason to hit him, getting hit once wasn’t a reason to massacre his family, and getting drunk and acting like a lunatic wasn’t a reason to end up with crippled arms. So why the hell did you do it? It was something that could have passed without anything happening.”

Instead of begging for forgiveness, the man glared at me as if he would kill me. A life spent beating and tormenting others could not display even a single moment of wisdom, right up to the very end. Later, he would regret it and say it had been because of the liquor.

“Die!”

When the bastard threw himself at me to headbutt me, I shattered even his dantian, and only then did he lose consciousness.

I had the martial artists of our cult who were on patrol transport the man to Hwangcheon Pavilion. I intended to uncover every crime he had committed so far and imprison him in the underground prison with the heaviest possible sentence.

Despite his miserable state, the people who knew him wore expressions of satisfaction.

I spoke to the spectators.

“There is no law anywhere in our cult that says a demon may commit evil deeds. If anyone like this appears among our cult’s martial artists in the future, report them over there……”

I pointed to the Hwangcheon Pavilion sub-branch across from the tavern.

“Report them to that place!”

Starting with Jo Chunbae’s applause, everyone cheered and clapped. Without exaggeration, in Maga Village, my popularity surpassed even Father’s.

Jo Chunbae came over with a delighted face and said,

“Today’s meal is on me.”

Just then, someone behind me said,

“May I be treated as well?”

When I turned around, to my surprise, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was standing there.

I looked at Jo Chunbae and said,

“If you refuse him, you’ll be dragged off to the Outer Lands. He is the Pungcheon Cult Leader, after all.”

Jo Chunbae’s eyes widened as if he might faint. Thanks to me, he had received several great figures as guests lately, and now even the Pungcheon Cult Leader had appeared as a customer.

On the second floor, at the seat where I always sat, I faced the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“What you said earlier—did you mean it? That demons should not take evil deeds for granted.”

“Yes.”

“That does not sound like something that should come from the mouth of the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“I dislike being bound by convention. Young people these days are like this.”

“So I was old-fashioned after all.”

“Keep young people by your side. With an open mind. Thanks to that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon has been getting younger lately.”

“Is that so? Hahaha.”

As the Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed heartily, I filled his cup and said,

“By the way, it seems the rumors were wrong.”

“What rumors?”

“I heard that you never take even a single step away from your sacred relics, Cult Leader, but I seem to see you outside quite often.”

It had been that way when he visited last time, and it was so now as well. The Pungcheon Cult Leader was breaking his own principles.

“Perhaps I trust the cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult more than the cult leader of the Pungcheon Cult?”

He meant that he trusted Father. Though he said it like a joke, it was likely sincere. At the very least, it was because he believed Father would not stand by and allow his sacred relics to be stolen within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

I smiled and picked up my chopsticks.

“Now then, please eat.”

I had expected the food here not to suit the palate of the Pungcheon Cult Leader, who ate only high-class cuisine, but he emptied his bowls cleanly. I thought he might be less concerned with formality than I had expected.

“The taste is quite good.”

“I’m glad you enjoyed it.”

After finishing our meal, we drank tea instead of liquor. The Pungcheon Cult Leader not only disliked drinking with meals, but always drank tea immediately after eating.

“Have you tried using the Space-Time Transposition Art?”

“Yes.”

“Everyone fails at first, so do not take it too much to heart.”

He believed I had failed to create the space. Since he had said the first attempt would take several days no matter how fast one was, the fact that I was here now naturally led him to think I had failed.

“I succeeded.”

“What?”

“The space was splendid. I think I’ll become deeply absorbed in this martial art from now on.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled out of his wits.

“You have already manifested it?”

“Yes.”

“How long did it take you to manifest it?”

“About two shichen.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was shocked. He was so surprised that he even let out a short gasp.

“Is that true?”

His face said he could not believe it at all.

“It seems to be a martial art well suited to me.”

“To be honest, I cannot believe it.”

“Why would I lie?”

“If one is not a genius of martial arts, that is…… No, even for a martial arts genius, two shichen is impossible!”

I no longer insisted that it had taken two shichen and simply sipped my tea.

“Is it truly so?”

I nodded.

“Can you show me later?”

As a matter of courtesy, one should not disbelieve someone so openly and dig into it this persistently. But because I understood his suspicion, I readily nodded.

“Very well.”

“Thank you.”

Perhaps his insides were burning, for the Pungcheon Cult Leader drank his cooled tea in one gulp.

I waited a moment for him to calm down, then asked quietly,

“By the way, why did you come to see me today?”

“I have something to ask of you.”

“Please say it.”

“Mabul is trying to send me back to the Outer Lands.”

“What is the reason?”

“You.”

“Because of me?”

“He is wary that you and I might join hands. So give me a pretext to remain here.”

I had a feeling that was not the only reason he had come to see me. How could he come looking for me without knowing whether I could create such a pretext or not? This was an excuse to grow closer to me. There was a way to confirm whether that was truly the case.

“What will you give me in return?”

“That is rather cold between us.”

“There is no ‘between us’ yet, Cult Leader. There was one transaction, though.”

I left him no opening. I had to show him I was thorough to lead any deal with him in my favor. That was also why I had told him I opened the Space-Time Transposition Art in two shichen. To give him boundless confidence that he could trust me.

“What do you want?”

“May I choose one of the sacred relics you brought?”

In that instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression hardened.

“Are you insane?”

I answered calmly, as if asking in return.

“Surely not as much as you, Cult Leader, who seeks to advance into the Central Plains amid this chaos after a Demon Venerable has died.”

* * *

I entered his residence together with the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

He had accepted my proposal. As I expected, he intended to join hands with me. Even by giving me one of his precious sacred relics. This was not payment for a request; it was a gift.

Why was he going this far, straining himself like this, to join hands with me? There had definitely been some change in his state of mind.

In the room, a man wearing shackles sat with his back turned, looking at the Yin Thunder Bell.

When I had taken the Blood Eye Essence, I had briefly thought about how I had been unable to free him. I had believed I would not meet him again in this life, but now here we were, meeting once more.

“Who is that man?”

When I asked the Pungcheon Cult Leader while pretending not to know, I saw it. The way the Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at him. It was not the gaze one used to look at a subordinate or a slave.

The man, too, did not offer any greeting even though the Pungcheon Cult Leader had entered. Normally, one would turn and greet him.

I became certain that the relationship between these two was not that of an ordinary master and servant.

“He is a subordinate who guards the Yin Thunder Bell.”

“I see.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked over the sacred relics lined up there and said in a low voice,

“Among the sacred relics, you must not ask for the Yin Thunder Bell or the Blood Buddha.”

“Yes, I understand. So that bell is the famous Yin Thunder Bell of the Pungcheon Cult.”

I walked toward the Yin Thunder Bell as if seeing it for the first time.

The moment I had brought this Yin Thunder Bell back in my life before the regression flashed past my eyes like a revolving lantern. It felt as if the demon engraved on the bell were looking at me and saying, “You again?”

“How is it, seeing it in person?”

“It is magnificent.”

As I said so, I glanced down at the shackled man. In truth, I was more interested in this man than in the Yin Thunder Bell. I had approached this way as an excuse because I wanted to see him up close.

Just then, the man who had been lowering his head slightly lifted it and looked at me. Through the long hair hanging over his face, our eyes met. He was much younger than I had imagined, and his gaze was clear.

The man lowered his head again. It had been a very brief meeting of eyes, but somehow, it was a gaze not easily forgotten.

“Now, choose.”

“I shall.”

I slowly examined the display stands on which the sacred relics had been placed. Of course, I knew very well what all the sacred relics here were.

I chose one of them.

“Please give me this.”

In that instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression went rigid.

And then I saw it.

At the very moment when the Pungcheon Cult Leader was about to say no despite himself, he flinched and closed his mouth.

I could feel it instinctively. A fact as important as the sacred relic I had chosen.

‘The shackled man sent a voice transmission to the Pungcheon Cult Leader!’

Chapter 66: Win It All, or Lose It All.
---------------------------------------
The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face was filled with regret.

Given the circumstances, the shackled man had undoubtedly sent him a sound transmission telling him to simply hand over the divine relic I had chosen.

‘That man interfered with the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s decision at such an important moment?’

Something truly unexpected had happened.

“Do you know what it is you’ve chosen?”

“I’m not sure, but I was strongly drawn to it.”

What I had chosen was a small, egg-shaped sphere made of iron.

Of course, I knew exactly what it was. It was neither a weapon nor a jewel. What mattered was what lay inside this egg-like object.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader lifted the sphere and touched its underside. With a metallic click, the egg opened.

In an instant, an intense yet heavy medicinal fragrance spread out. Inside was a crimson spirit pill.

The Blood God Pill.

It was a spiritual medicine known as the finest not only in the Pungcheon Cult, but throughout the lands beyond the borders.

“It is the Blood God Pill. Have you heard of it?”

“Yes, I know it to be an extremely precious spiritual medicine.”

“Precious is hardly enough to describe it.”

“Why did you not take something so precious yourself?”

“I was saving it for the future.”

“It seems that future was me.”

He surely had never imagined that, out of all these divine relics, I would choose this one of all things.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at the shackled man again. At that moment, I saw the shoulders of the shackled man, whose head was lowered, tremble faintly. Because my eyesight had improved through the Divine Eye Art, I could confirm it with my own eyes.

‘He’s definitely sending sound transmissions.’

In other words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was undoubtedly following that man’s instructions. No, at the very least, he was consulting him.

‘Just what kind of person is he?’

Curiosity about the man arose in me, but I pretended not to notice. For now, the Blood God Pill was what mattered.

To give them time, I turned away as if unaware and looked over the other divine relics. I saw several things I wanted after the Blood God Pill. Whether I could obtain them or not would depend on the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s choices and actions from here on.

After a while, having finished their discussion, the Pungcheon Cult Leader made a difficult decision with a face that seemed to have aged ten years.

“Very well. I will give you the Blood God Pill.”

“Thank you.”

Still holding the Blood God Pill in his hand, the Pungcheon Cult Leader asked me,

“This time, I cannot give it to you first. Tell me the method by which I can remain in the Divine Cult.”

It seemed that he could not readily hand over the Blood God Pill, at least.

“The method is precisely this.”

Before the Pungcheon Cult Leader could stop me, I put the Blood God Pill he was holding into my mouth. The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s eyes bulged, as he had never imagined I would actually swallow it.

“You! What do you think you’re doing?”

He belatedly roared, but I immediately sat down cross-legged.

“Please stand guard for me while I dissolve the medicine for a moment.”

Then, with my eyes closed, I began dissolving the medicinal efficacy of the Blood God Pill. As a result, I did not see just how dumbfounded the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression had become.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk’s qi circulation and breathing had continued for a full two hours.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader and the shackled man were conversing through sound transmissions.

The shackled man calmed the Pungcheon Cult Leader, who was jumping in rage and saying he would kill him on the spot. It was also the shackled man who insisted that they must not disturb his qi circulation, and that even their conversation had to be conducted only through sound transmissions.

—Hahahahaha.

—Damn it! Curse it all!

The shackled man kept laughing through sound transmission. The Pungcheon Cult Leader, on the other hand, vented his fury without pause.

—Damn it! Should I kill this bastard right now?

—Kill him. Kill him and have a nice, satisfying war with the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. I would very much like to see you torn to pieces by the Heavenly Demon.

—Damn it! Curse it! What the hell is this?

He had truly never imagined that the man would eat it right in front of him.

—The method he intends to propose must be absurd. That must be why he took it first, is it not?

—I told you to kill him! Kill him! Smash his skull with one palm strike.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader approached Geom Mugeuk and raised his hand high. But he could not bring himself to strike down on his head.

—Ah, damn it!

After lowering his hand, the Pungcheon Cult Leader came over beside the Sound Thunder Bell and plopped down. The shackled man seemed to be enjoying the sight with amusement.

—Damn it!

—I do not think the Cult Leader will end up dead.

—Why? Because I’m a coward? Is that it?

—No. The Second Young Master is far more intelligent than I thought.

—What makes you say that?

—He is certain that the Cult Leader cannot kill him. That means he has already finished grasping what kind of person you are. Someone like that will give a proper answer.

—Do you have an idea what it is?

—I did not at first, but seeing him take the Blood God Pill, I think I know what method he will propose.

—What is it?

—Hear it from him directly when he finishes his qi circulation.

—Tell me!

—You cannot know, Cult Leader. It will show on your face.

—In my younger days, I never once lost at gambling!

—Because no gambler would dare display his true skill in front of the heir to the Pungcheon Cult.

—Hmph!

Only then did the Pungcheon Cult Leader feel somewhat relieved.

The shackled man called to the Pungcheon Cult Leader casually, as if speaking to a friend.

—Cult Leader.

—Good grief, here you go again. Am I your friend?

—Just give him everything. It would be fine to give him all of it.

—Are you serious?

—Until now, I judged the Second Young Master only by the information that came flying in. But seeing him in person today, I realize all the information up to now underestimated him.

—You can see that with your eyes?

—I can.

—Then why can’t I see it?

—Because you are too greedy.

—Damn it! Fine, let’s say that’s true. Let’s say my eyes are completely covered by greed. So? If I give him everything, what happens to me?

—You can live in the Central Plains. Was that not your dream?

—Did he say he would let me live in the Central Plains?

—He must have the intention to let you live there, which is why he keeps accepting everything so readily.

—What if he doesn’t keep his promise? We’ll have to carry around a broken beggar’s bowl and live off alms like paupers.

—Do not worry. I will feed and support you.

—What?

—I will drag these shackles around, clanking and clattering, and sell medicine if I must.

—……

The Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a sigh.

—What if the Second Young Master loses the succession struggle and dies?

—Is that not what it means to invest in someone with your fate on the line? You either gain everything, or lose everything.

—You are doing this to ruin me, aren’t you?

—You only realized that now?

The shackled man wore a strange smile. It was hard to tell whether he was serious or joking.

—Cult Leader.

—Do not speak informally to me. You passed five times long ago today.

—I understand you.

—……

—You must discard in order to obtain. Neither you nor I are blessed enough to have everything without throwing anything away. People like that are people like the Second Young Master sitting over there.

—……Damn it.

At last, Geom Mugeuk finished his qi circulation and breathing.

Geom Mugeuk’s expression was bright as he opened his eyes. His already clear eyes had grown even deeper, making it difficult to guess just how much internal energy he possessed.

But soon, those deep eyes changed to become like those of an ordinary person. The sight of him altering his gaze made the Pungcheon Cult Leader flinch.

‘Just how deep is his internal energy?’

Geom Mugeuk hid nothing in front of him. It was as if he were saying this:

I am this strong, so trust me and follow me.

Despite the power he revealed, Geom Mugeuk was courteous. He bowed his head and cupped his fist in thanks.

“Thank you.”

“You truly have guts. To steal a spiritual medicine and then circulate your qi right in front of me!”

“Steal a spiritual medicine? I received it as payment.”

“As payment? What do you mean?”

“I told you before I took the Blood God Pill, did I not? The method by which you can remain here is for me to eat this.”

“What does that mean?”

“Tomorrow, please come to the Yellow Springs Pavilion and report it. Say that your spiritual medicine has been stolen.”

“!”

“You should be able to stay here until the spiritual medicine is found.”

Only then did the Pungcheon Cult Leader realize Geom Mugeuk’s intention.

“But if an investigation is conducted, it may be revealed that no thief broke in.”

“The place conducting that investigation is none other than the Yellow Springs Pavilion, where I am the head. Until you achieve your purpose, I will proceed with the investigation as slowly as possible.”

“Ah!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression brightened radiantly.

Since a thief had broken into the inner court of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, he now had a pretext not to leave this place.

Furthermore, since Geom Mugeuk would be in charge of investigating the case, there would be no risk of the staged act being exposed, nor would there be unnecessary friction in the process of searching for the culprit.

“You and I will also be the ones to decide the date of your return.”

“Ah! So that was your meaning.”

“Then I will be going now. Thank you once again for the Blood God Pill.”

After Geom Mugeuk left, the Pungcheon Cult Leader asked the shackled man,

“Did you know?”

“I knew the moment the Second Young Master ate the Blood God Pill.”

The shackled man’s attitude had returned to being respectful.

Looking down at the empty shell that had held the Blood God Pill, the Pungcheon Cult Leader said,

“But did the Second Young Master know in advance that this was the Blood God Pill?”

“Of course.”

“He knew?”

“Do you think he picked it up because the container looked pretty? Did you truly believe that?”

“No.”

But for a moment, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had believed just that. He had thought there was no way the Second Young Master could know the details of his divine relics.

“How did he know?”

“It is precisely because he is a figure of that caliber that you are giving him gifts, Cult Leader.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader nodded. Even so, he thought it was fortunate that the shackled man was here. On the other hand, because all of this was happening due to the shackled man, he also felt resentful.

“……Still, the Blood God Pill was far too wasteful.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader began lamenting its loss again. There would be no sleep for him tonight.

“I am telling you for the hundredth time that we did not give it to him because he provided a method.”

“If things do not go well after we have poured everything out like this, you die and I die too.”

Amid his long, disheveled hair, the man grinned.

“You promised to die only after killing me.”

“Shut up!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader sank into his empty container, and the shackled man into the Sound Thunder Bell. In that way, the two fell into their respective worries.

* * *

The next day, shocking news struck the cult.

A thief had broken into the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s quarters and stolen a divine relic.

Everyone who met spoke of it. It was shocking enough that a thief had entered the inner court, but what everyone was curious about was exactly what divine relic had been stolen. However, the identity of the stolen divine relic was not revealed.

The cult was in an uproar over the matter, but the atmosphere inside the Heavenly Demon Hall itself was calm.

That was because Chief Strategist Sima Myeong had seen through the situation.

“It appears that the Pungcheon Cult Leader has staged this himself. According to our pavilion’s surveillance network, no one infiltrated the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s residence. Moreover, to steal a divine relic, one would need to be at least at the level of a Demon Venerable, and as far as I have determined, there is currently no expert of that caliber who would have reason to target the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s divine relics.”

Heavenly Demon Geom Woojin believed everything in Sima Myeong’s report.

“What is his reason for staging this?”

“He most likely wishes to remain in our cult longer. He will stubbornly insist that he cannot return unless the divine relic is found.”

“Why exactly does he refuse to leave? Is it perhaps because of that matter?”

“That is correct. It seems he wishes to settle the matter of the Pungcheon Cult’s advance into the Central Plains during this opportunity.”

“The second was involved in this, wasn’t he?”

“Yes. It appears the Pungcheon Cult Leader asked the Second Young Master for a method to remain. Since the Yellow Springs Pavilion will handle the investigation of this incident, the Pungcheon Cult Leader will be able to stay in our cult for the time being.”

“He used his head rather well.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Geom Woojin’s mouth.

“What is the relationship between the Pungcheon Cult Leader and the Demon Venerables?”

“Not very good. Since the first meeting with the Demon Venerables after his arrival, they have not met even once. The only person he met was the Demon Buddha, with whom he is somewhat close.”

“What do you think should be done about this matter?”

Sima Myeong answered calmly.

“I believe it would be best to pretend not to know and watch for the time being.”

Geom Woojin pondered for a moment, and Sima Myeong waited for him to make a decision.

Sima Myeong could guess the cult leader’s thoughts.

The reason a decision did not come immediately was, after all, because this concerned the Pungcheon Cult. The cult leader did not look favorably upon the Pungcheon Cult’s advance into the Central Plains. Even so, the reason he was contemplating it like this was probably because the Second Young Master was involved.

At last, Geom Woojin made his decision.

“Do so.”

“Yes.”

Sima Myeong bowed respectfully and was about to turn and leave when he spoke to Geom Woojin.

“If the Pungcheon Cult Leader and the Second Young Master join hands, the Demon Buddha will not sit still. He will likely begin drawing in the First Young Master in earnest.”

In Sima Myeong’s judgment, the reason the First Young Master had been holding back despite the Second Young Master’s recent rise was partly because he was watching the cult leader’s mood, but also because he was still certain that he would become the successor.

However, if Geom Mugeuk joined hands with the Pungcheon Cult Leader after the Blood Heaven Saber Demon and the One Flower Sword Venerable, he would no longer be able to simply watch.

Geom Woojin nodded silently, his expression saying that he understood.

Leaving Geom Woojin behind as he sank into thought alone, Sima Myeong slowly walked out along the Path of Blood.

He was curious.

Just what decision would the cult leader make? Would he truly permit the First Young Master to return and leap into the battlefield? Would he somehow stop the fight between brothers?

Everything depended on the will of the Heavenly Demon.

Chapter 67: At That Age.
------------------------
The next day, I went to find my father.

It was to report on what had transpired with the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Since it was an internal matter of the cult, Father would already have a rough grasp of the situation, but my coming to tell him personally was an entirely different matter from him receiving a report from Sima Ming.

As it happened, Father was in the middle of training.

While I waited outside, a message came telling me to enter.

“Please come inside the training hall.”

The voice that came from the air was Hwi, Father’s personal bodyguard. It had been a while since I last heard his voice.

“It’s been a while, Uncle.”

“Yes, young master.”

A brief answer came. I had memories of holding Hwi’s hand and walking the streets of Maga Village when I was young. I think I pestered him to buy me street food. In any case, that day remained a pleasant memory for me.

“Uncle, let me see your face sometime. I’ll treat you to a meal.”

“Yes.”

“Be at ease, Uncle.”

I couldn’t know exactly how he would receive my goodwill, or what he currently felt toward me. Because while it was a fond memory for me, to him it might have been nothing more than an unremarkable day.

Even so, I had to get along well with him. He was the person Father trusted most.

I steeled myself and entered the training hall.

Sure enough.

Today as well, Father attacked with the Myeongwangbo.

I dodged with the Jeommyeolbo and batted aside Father’s Heavenly Demon Sword with my Black Demon Sword. It was the first clash between the Black Demon Sword and the Heavenly Demon Sword, and in fact, it was a far more dangerous attempt than simply evading. Naturally, Father recognized the change in my level at a glance.

“You’ve grown stronger!”

Father had attacked with his mastery of the Myeongwangbo at a much higher level, yet I had blocked it in a more dangerous manner.

“Of course I had to improve. I probably train the most in the Main Cult. No, I suppose I’m second. Ian trains the most.”

“Why does it not occur to you that you might be third?”

From this remark, I could guess how diligently Father was training the Pungsinsabo.

*Father, you are doing well. You must reach great accomplishment. You must!*

I walked out of the training hall with Father. We walked side by side as we conversed.

“What brings you here?”

“The Pungcheon Cult Leader wishes to join hands with me. It feels as though a rift has formed with the Eight Demon Lords. Did you know?”

I deliberately did not mention that he had transmitted the Space-Time Displacement Technique to me. This was something the Pungcheon Cult Leader likewise would not reveal to outsiders.

This was how Father viewed the crux of the matter.

“It was an arrogant act for the Demon Lords to drag the Pungcheon Cult Leader all the way here. They should have gone to him to resolve it, and if they meant to bring him, everyone should have gone to request his presence.”

“They offended the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s pride.”

There were likely several reasons he was pushing for an advance into the Central Plains, but as I peeled them back one by one, I felt that what lay at the very bottom might truly be his wounded pride.
