Even my own resolve to kill Hwa Mugi without fail had invisible shackles bound to it.

Between the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the One Flower Sword Venerable, too, there was a shackle of hatred difficult to sever.

Then what about Father? Like the shackled man bound before the Yin Thunder Bell, perhaps Father was bound to the throne as well. With the heavy responsibility of protecting the cult resting on his shoulders.

“I’m saying you may choose your own life. You don’t have to be the leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit. Leave the cult and travel the Central Plains. Live as a hidden master in some peaceful village if you wish. I taught you martial arts, but there is no need to repay that grace. You have already done more than enough for me, so choose with an easy heart.”

Ian listened quietly, then smiled and said,

“I suppose I have no choice but to reveal one more tail.”

“What do you mean?”

“Being by your side makes me happier, and I’m living a better life. Traveling is fine for a day or two, and being a hidden master is fine for a day or two. Every day inside your fence makes me happy. I’m not stupid, Young Master. If I truly hated it, I’d have risked my life and run away in the middle of the night. I really don’t know why you insist on seeing me as so good.”

I looked at her and let out a faint laugh.

“How many hidden tails do you have left?”

“About seven, perhaps?”

“That’s enough. Then keep training.”

I was about to turn away, but needless worry stirred in me, and I added one more thing.

“Still, make sure you keep at least one tail hidden.”

Having lived my life, I learned that was as important as a secret ultimate technique.

I left while watching her expression, which said, I know that much even without you telling me. Yes. As long as she knew.

* * *

The Demon Buddha came to visit the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

He had come with resolve, and even the look in his eyes as he entered was different.

“Did you come after killing someone? A man who follows Buddhist teachings is reeking with killing intent.”

“The Buddha I serve occasionally breaks the precept against killing.”

“First a lecherous Buddha, then a murderous Buddha. Truly, yours is a busy deity.”

The Demon Buddha did not sit down, but stood in place and stared at the Pungcheon Cult Leader. He was nearly at eye level with the Pungcheon Cult Leader, who sat with his back ramrod straight. For someone as sensitive about his height as he was, matching eye level in such a way was a clear sign that he was extremely angry.

“See here, Pungcheon Cult Leader!”

“What is it?”

“How long have we known each other?”

“I don’t quite remember.”

“Exactly. It is a relationship so long that we can’t even remember it properly, and yet you can do this to me?”

“Sit down. Let us talk seated.”

But he did not sit. He glared at the Pungcheon Cult Leader. The golden light radiating from his body grew even more intense.

“I never imagined you would play such an obvious trick!”

“A trick, you say?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression hardened, but the Demon Buddha had come determined from the start.

The One Flower Sword Venerable and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had begun moving, persuading the other Demon Venerables. The Demon Venerables’ reactions differed when they met the Demon Buddha and when they met the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. They were all people who had lived their lives in their own way, never minding others’ opinions, so no one could guarantee whose words they would listen to at the final moment.

That was why the Demon Buddha had come to attack the Pungcheon Cult Leader directly.

“You seduced the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s young disciple and took her as your own? Just what kind of scheme is this!”

“I did not seduce her. The child came to me on her own two feet.”

“Then you should have sent her back! As the adult, you should have sent her back!”

“Why should I have?”

“Why are you trying to become the master of a disciple of our cult?”

“Because the martial arts practiced by ghost arts practitioners are the demonic arts of the Blood Cult!”

“Then are you now saying you belong to the Blood Cult? Are you serious?”

Everyone knew that the Pungcheon Cult was the successor of the Blood Cult, but that did not mean one could mention or connect it to the Blood Cult. The Blood Cult had a history of waging war against the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult several times. He had been invited only because he was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Are you splitting hairs now?”

“When you staged that self-made play about your sacred relic being stolen, I could understand it in a way. But this matter I cannot tolerate.”

“A self-made play? You come to a friend whose sacred relic was stolen and call it a self-made play? I came here trusting only you, and even had my sacred relic stolen. Instead of taking responsibility, you spout such absurd speculation!”

“Did you think we would believe that? The Demon Venerables are not fools.”

“Looking at you, I would not say you seem all that clever.”

“What did you say?”

“In your life, how many times have you ever been told you were clever? Be honest.”

For a moment, the Demon Buddha’s expression stiffened. He knew very well that he was not a particularly clever man.

Even his habit of snapping at others with harsh words was a means of suppressing the other party’s momentum and steering the conversation to his advantage.

When the Pungcheon Cult Leader struck straight at his inferiority complex, the Demon Buddha chose a forceful approach.

“Listen, my friend. The place you are in now is not beyond the borders.”

“Are you threatening me?”

“This is not a threat. I am telling you the reality. I have long been on friendly terms with you, but the other Demon Venerables think differently. I cannot know how they will act.”

“With that poor imagination of yours, what else could there be? You would put yourself at the front and try to kill me.”

“If only that were the case, we would at least be able to say farewell.”

A taut tension hovered between the two of them.

“Do not look down on the Demon Venerables. They will try to dispose of you in a far more miserable way than that.”

“You tell me not to look down on them, but why do you look down on me so much?”

“I do not look down on you. You simply have a fatal weakness.”

“A weakness?”

“If the Demon Venerables decide to remove you, they will not make the foolish choice of attacking you directly. Instead, they will sow discord between you and the Cult Leader and make him eliminate you. Can you handle that? Our Cult Leader.”

When he threatened him with the Heavenly Demon, the Pungcheon Cult Leader could say nothing. The Heavenly Demon, who disliked the Blood Cult, could certainly be called the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s weakness.

“Pretend to search for the sacred relic for a few days, then leave. Expel the disciple as well. I will speak well to the Demon Venerables.”

“And if I refuse?”

Instead of answering, the Demon Buddha gave a faint laugh and left. Perhaps that made it all the more unpleasant, for the Pungcheon Cult Leader said nothing for a long while.

At last, he opened his mouth.

“I have just made up my mind.”

Looking toward the shackled man before the Yin Thunder Bell, the Pungcheon Cult Leader ground his teeth.

“I will make the Second Young Master the Heavenly Demon without fail.”

* * *

“What brings you here, Second Young Master?”

Today, as always, Chief Strategist Sama Myeong was buried in work.

“I came because I wanted to have some fun with you, Strategist. If you have time, shall we go to Maga Village?”

Sama Myeong was startled by my suggestion.

“To Maga Village?”

“You are the most important person in our cult, but the cult will not collapse if you slack off for a day.”

“The sorrowful secret is that it would not collapse even if I slacked off for a year.”

“Then let us go have fun for just two hours, so that secret does not get out.”

“Very well.”

Without hesitation, Sama Myeong closed his documents and stood up. He must have guessed that I had not come simply to ask him to play.

“If you are absent, the Martial Alliance will invade at once.”

“You say that because you do not know Tongcheon Pavilion. There is a line of clever strategists waiting only for me to step down.”

“They certainly chose a difficult path.”

We laughed as we left his office.

The place I brought him was Pungryu Tavern.

“So it was here after all.”

That meant he knew Pungryu Tavern well. Then again, since he must know everything about me in detail, naturally he would know all about Pungryu Tavern as well.

“Are you disappointed, perhaps? Were you expecting me to take you to the finest pleasure house in Maga Village?”

It was a somewhat excessive joke, but Sama Myeong accepted it with a smile.

“I was hoping I might pretend to lose and follow you there.”

“This is why prejudice is frightening. For our next bout of slacking off, I will make a reservation at once!”

“Hahaha.”

At Pungryu Tavern, I sat across from Sama Myeong in the second-floor seat where I always sat.

I introduced him to Jo Chunbae, who came to take our order.

“This is the Chief Strategist of our cult. Greet him.”

“U-uhheok!”

Jo Chunbae bowed to Sama Myeong with a face as though he might faint. Lately, visits from high-ranking people had left him in a daze. Pavilion Masters, Demon Venerables, and now even the Chief Strategist. In particular, the weight carried by the title of Chief Strategist was different from any other.

“I have always respected you. You are the person I respect most.”

“So the person you respect most is not my father?”

At my teasing, Jo Chunbae jumped in fright.

“Of course not! I respect the Strategist second most.”

“Ah, I thought I was second.”

“No, that is not it. Actually, the Pavilion Master is the one I respect most…”

He played along, pretending to be flustered.

“I was joking. Please bring the liquor first, and I look forward to today’s dishes.”

“Yes!”

After bringing the liquor, Jo Chunbae ran to the kitchen.

I poured Sama Myeong a drink.

“This is the first time I’ve had a drink alone with you, isn’t it?”

“It is.”

“I should have invited you sooner, but I was late.”

“Not at all. You have been very busy all this time, have you not?”

“If anyone is busy, it is you, Strategist. I am always slacking off.”

“If you say that, then what are the true slackers supposed to do?”

He was the person who knew best just how diligently I had been running around lately.

We drank while sharing this and that about ordinary matters. Sama Myeong could hold his liquor better than I had expected.

“You drink well.”

“Of course. In my younger days, I could drink like a fish.”

“That is unexpected.”

It was difficult to picture the younger Sama Myeong drinking heavily.

“But what is this about?”

Sama Myeong knew that I had not come to ask him to play. I, too, did not beat around the bush and went straight to the point.

“There is something I would like to ask you.”

“Please do.”

“What makes a good strategist?”

As if the question was unexpected, Sama Myeong widened his eyes and looked at me. Soon, his gaze turned toward his cup.

“When I was young, I asked myself that question often, but I have forgotten it for some time.”

Questions like this were difficult by nature. If someone asked me what made a good martial artist, what should I say?

But Sama Myeong’s answer came right away.

“The good strategist I envision is a strategist smarter than the strategist on the opposing side. There is no profound meaning to it. Just as the words say, a strategist smarter than the enemy strategist. That is my answer. There may be people in our cult smarter than I am. There may even be one in this tavern. It is fine if you are smarter than I am, Second Young Master. However…”

After a brief pause, Sama Myeong said,

“I must be smarter than the Martial Alliance’s strategist. I must also be smarter than the Evil Dao Alliance’s strategist. That is enough.”

It was truly an answer I had not expected. As if he had anticipated my reaction, Sama Myeong smiled and asked,

“A bland answer, is it not?”

“That is why it struck me so clearly.”

“When I was young, I had too many thoughts, so a different answer would likely have come out. Perhaps one a bit more pedantic.”

I could guess what he meant. The older one grew, and the greater the master, the simpler the answer tended to become.

“Martial artists moving in the field can act according to circumstance. They can change the operation depending on the situation. But a strategist cannot do that. If he devises one operation incorrectly, it is annihilation. At least if one dies by one’s own choice, there will be less regret. But how wronged would one feel if one died because of a mistaken judgment from above? That is why I always tell the junior strategists: become smarter with everything you have. Everything depends on that.”

This was the first time I learned that Sama Myeong held such thoughts. Just as it was the first time I learned he could drink quite well.

Sama Myeong filled my empty cup and said,

“Now then, it is my turn to ask a question.”

In truth, I had not come to hear the answer to what made a good strategist. My meeting with him today had been for the sake of hearing this question.

“Why did you ask me that?”

Chapter 74: Trust Your Instincts.
---------------------------------
“Lately, I’ve been looking for a strategist.”

Today’s meeting with Sama Myeong was for the sake of saying those words.

“Am I not enough for you?”

At Sama Myeong’s joke, I smiled and replied.

“Father and I are both rather greedy men, aren’t we? Father would never share Strategist Sama with me. You know my father well enough, don’t you?”

Sama Myeong deflected with a smile.

“So I came to seek your advice, Strategist. I wanted to ask what sort of strategist I ought to look for.”

For a moment, Sama Myeong toyed with his wine cup in silence. He looked calm, but his mind was surely turning at great speed.

From the intent behind my visit to how much he should tell me. What effect the aftermath of his words might have on himself, on our cult, or on the succession struggle. All manner of thoughts were likely being sorted inside his head.

I did not know the conditions that made a good strategist. Only this one thing I knew from experience.

A good strategist, in the end, makes good choices.

As far as I knew, Sama Myeong was a better strategist than anyone, so he would surely give me the proper answer.

He drank another cup of wine, and when I filled his cup again, he finished thinking.

Sama Myeong asked me with a meaningful look.

“Could it be that you have already finished deliberating?”

There was an important meaning contained in that question. I could tell. Sama Myeong already knew of the shackled man’s existence. He even knew that the person I had in mind was him.

What he was saying to me now was this.

—Are you considering that man from the Pungcheon Cult as your strategist?

Perhaps it was only natural. There was no way Chief Strategist Sama Myeong would be unaware of the shackled man, who served as the Pungcheon Cult’s strategist.

Lately, using the theft case as an excuse, I had been going in and out of the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s residence, and now I had suddenly come to meet him and said I wanted to find a strategist. Of course he would think of him.

“There is someone I’m considering, but I’m not yet certain.”

If I asked him directly, he would never answer, so I asked with my eyes.

—May I take him as my strategist?

Sama Myeong gazed at me and held back his answer. Now was the moment when the most important words of today’s meeting would come.

“No one can be certain when it comes to winning a person over. The result only appears after a long process. So for now, trust your instincts, Second Young Master.”

Trust my instincts.

It was ordinary advice that anyone could give.

But right now, that advice held a different meaning. Knowing that I had the shackled man in mind, he told me to trust my instincts, which in the end was advice to accept him.

If the shackled man were someone who would harm our cult, he would never have said such a thing. It meant he was a man of ability whom Sama Myeong acknowledged.

Sama Myeong was not my man, but at the very least, he was someone filled with loyalty toward our cult. With the possibility that I might become the Heavenly Demon, he would not recommend just anyone.

“Yes. I will trust my instincts.”

When I raised my cup, Sama Myeong nodded once, then clinked his cup against mine with vigor.

Today, all he had told me was to trust my own instincts.

Whether it was Father or a Demon Venerable, if someday there were any questioning over whether he had recommended the shackled man to me, he was free of it. Even with me, it was the same. I could not demand to know why he had recommended him. Sama Myeong had merely told me to trust my instincts; he had not recommended him.

We drank for a full two sijin.

We shared all sorts of stories, but Sama Myeong did not show the slightest disorder, nor did he make a single slip of the tongue.

* * *

Several days passed.

Outwardly, they were days no different from any other, but among the Demon Venerables, all manner of schemes and politics ran rampant.

And today, at last, the day of fate arrived.

“They must be in the middle of voting right now.”

At Seo Daeryong’s words, I nodded.

“Most likely.”

Right now, through the Demon Venerables’ meeting, they were deciding the next Demon Venerable.

I could not know whether the Blood Heaven Blade Demon or the One Flower Sword Venerable had persuaded the other Demon Venerables. I had not gone to ask them separately, and they had not come to tell me either.

“You’re involved in this matter too, aren’t you, Pavilion Master?”

I nodded readily.

“You’re my right arm, yet there are many things you don’t know. Are you disappointed?”

“No. I hope I never know. I absolutely won’t sulk just because I’m excluded from something like this. So please proceed without my knowledge in the future as well. Even if you unify the murim, Pavilion Master, I’m fine finding out from rumors the next day!”

“You were always this funny, weren’t you? When you first met me, you were just pretending to be gloomy, right?”

“Now even I’m confused. About what kind of person I am.”

“Bright is better. She’ll like that more too.”

“She?”

“The junior you like.”

“You mean the one even I had forgotten about?”

As if he had given up denying it now, Seo Daeryong nodded.

“Thanks to you, Pavilion Master, we’ll end up getting married.”

“If you want that, you need to eat with her first. Ask her to have a meal.”

“What for, when she’s busy?”

“What does eating have to do with being busy? Because you start out with that kind of burden, things that could work out end up not working. You should just approach her as someone she can see often and comfortably.”

I sensed Seo Daeryong’s eyes glint slightly.

“Does seeing someone often change anything?”

“It does. But you mustn’t become someone too comfortable. This is the most important part. Don’t let yourself look easy!”

“What should I do so I don’t look easy? Should I put strength into my eyes?”

“Good heavens!”

When I let out a sigh, Seo Daeryong quickly said,

“It was a joke, a joke.”

“It’s sadder because I know it wasn’t.”

“I’m not that lacking in sense.”

There was no way to determine the truth of that. A subordinate rushed in and reported the voting results.

“The results are out.”

“What happened?”

“It was a draw.”

“A draw? There are seven Demon Venerables. How can there be a draw?”

“They say one Demon Venerable abstained.”

“Ah, so there was an abstention.”

Who the Demon Venerables cast their votes for was not made known to outsiders. It seemed that either the Blood Heaven Blade Demon or the One Flower Sword Venerable had only managed to persuade half of them.

“And?”

“They decided that the first disciple and the third disciple will have a duel to determine the outcome. They say it will be held immediately tomorrow.”

“So it comes to this in the end.”

It may not have been intended, but in the end, the decision would be made through a duel. From Seo Hwanjin’s perspective, this was rather a good decision. If the one with superior skill took the Demon Venerable’s seat, there would be less room for complaints afterward.

After the subordinate who delivered the report left, Seo Daeryong asked,

“Who do you think will win?”

“Why do you ask?”

“I’ll go to the marketplace and place a wager. This is when a life of meager wages turns around.”

“Bet everything you have on Cheongseon!”

“Why the third disciple?”

“You’re not betting on the third disciple. You’re betting on the Pungcheon Cult Leader. No, to be exact, you’re betting on the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s desperation. He won’t let his disciple lose. Where are you going?”

“To mortgage my house!”

Seo Daeryong opened the door and was about to leave, but stopped.

“You’re not going to stop me?”

“You weren’t going to gamble anyway.”

“How do you know?”

“Gamblers only aim for a sudden fortune. They don’t think about changing reality like you do.”

Seo Daeryong grinned and closed the door.

“I really am going. I’m going to put strength in my eyes and ask her to eat.”

I walked to the window.

In the training ground where dusk had settled dimly, martial artists were coming and going busily. Each organization that heard the voting results would be watching tomorrow’s outcome closely.

It was important for Cheongseon to win, but more important to me were the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the One Flower Sword Venerable. If not for them, we could not have come this far.

‘Thank you, elders.’

* * *

The next day, the duel took place.

Around the dueling platform, the Seven Demon Venerables and their subordinates, as well as Seo Hwanjin’s ghost arts practitioners, all gathered. Since this match was open to spectators beyond the Demon Venerables’ direct subordinates, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and I also found seats and sat down. Father, however, did not attend.

Onto the dueling platform climbed the first disciple, Yang Do, and the third disciple, Cheongseon.

At their appearance, cheers erupted from Seo Hwanjin’s ghost arts practitioners.

Compared to the deceased Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, they still had a long way to go, but one of them was the next Demon Venerable who would lead Seo Hwanjin from now on. Depending on what sort of person that was, Seo Hwanjin’s fate would be divided.

Would Yang Do, the existing powerhouse, win? Or would Cheongseon, who had learned martial arts from the Pungcheon Cult Leader, win?

The expectation was that Yang Do would win at a ratio of about seven to three. Although Cheongseon had become the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s disciple, she had had far too little time to learn martial arts.

Since neither Yang Do nor Cheongseon had received the Space-Time Transposition Art, we could watch them fight. If not, it would have been the blandest fight in the world. The two of them would have vanished before our eyes at the same time, only reappearing after victory and defeat had been decided.

There were no polite greetings between the two.

“Cowardly bitch. Bitch who sold out her master.”

“Bringing up Master now is exactly what selling out Master looks like, you stupid senior brother.”

With cold curses, the duel began. The two knew that their lives and futures were at stake, so they showed each other no mercy.

Pitch-black smoke raised by Yang Do engulfed Cheongseon, and her Soul-Seizing Art invaded Yang Do’s mind. The wailing of ghosts rang out, and a ghost-possessed wind blew.

A black monster sprang from the floor and tried to drag its opponent down, while a red hand that shot out from midair tried to tear off the opponent’s head.

Though it was a fight not between Demon Venerables, but their disciples, it was fiercer and more powerful than expected. Since no one had expected much, everyone was startled.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwang!

With a roar, the earth trembled, and at times thunder sounded from a clear sky.

The demonic practitioners watching fell into despair as they thought:

How in the world is one supposed to block those techniques?

Just by watching this fight between disciples, one could understand why the orthodox sects had considered the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable the most troublesome. Techniques that left one at a loss for how to respond appeared one after another.

Everyone held their breath.

Even when they threw hidden weapons, even when they rolled across the ground, no one looked down on them.

A black evil spirit sprang out and took the incoming hidden weapon in their stead, and even in the moment they rolled across the ground, ghostly energy was being released from their hands. Blood that melted the floor surged over the opponent, and a shimmering haze tried to tear off the opponent’s arms.

They employed every method they could, and the battle was desperate and gruesome. It would not have seemed strange if they tore out each other’s throats with their teeth. The watching demonic practitioners were swept up in fervor.

And then, at some moment!

The ghostly wailing that had sounded throughout the fight ceased.

When the gray smoke that had been coiling around the two of them like a mad whirlwind vanished, the sight of them standing face-to-face came into view.

Everyone could tell. At last, the fight was over. They still could not tell who had won.

The first to speak was Yang Do.

“That last technique?”

“A lethal demonic art I learned from my new master.”

“I knew since we were young… that you were this kind of bitch.”

“Then you must know well. That I will become a Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable greater than our late master.”

Leaving him standing there, Cheongseon walked toward the place where the ghost arts practitioners were gathered. In a ringing voice, she said to them,

“That is why our Seo Hwanjin will become the greatest of the Eight Demons!”

The moment her words ended—

Papapapapak!

Blood spurted like fountains from the mouth, nose, and ears of the first disciple, Yang Do. With every meridian in his body severed strand by strand, he collapsed to the floor.

No one had imagined that Cheongseon would cruelly kill the first disciple, so silence flowed for an instant. Only for a moment. Then cheers erupted, shaking the place.

Waaaaaaaah!

Cheongseon slowly walked toward where the Seven Demon Venerables sat.

She cupped her fist respectfully.

“I greet you. I am Cheongseon.”

Those who had chosen her were smiling, and those who had not were stiff-faced.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon rose from his seat and shouted as their representative.

“A new master of Seo Hwanjin has been born!”

At his cry, everyone cheered.

It was the moment a new Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was born.

Cheongseon looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader and respectfully greeted him with cupped fists.

Next, she looked at me and greeted me.

But she did not cast even a single glance toward Sa Ujong.

Perhaps he had expected even a secretly passing look, for Sa Ujong seemed deeply disappointed. At some point, his life had ceased to flow as he wished.

Chapter 75: I’ll Accept a Drink.
--------------------------------
I crossed the final boundary of the inner court together with Jo Chunbae.

They once again checked our identities and searched us, as they had already done twice before, and inspected the various ingredients Jo Chunbae had brought.

Jo Chunbae was frozen stiff. The deeper we went into the inner court, the stronger and more terrifying the aura of the warriors guarding it became, and he could hardly even breathe.

Only after we had passed beyond the boundary did Jo Chunbae finally relax.

“Phew, I’ve never trembled this much in my life.”

“There’s no need to be so nervous. I’m here, am I not?”

“Of course. If not for you, Pavilion Lord, how could I ever have come this far? This is my first time in the inner court.”

The place we were heading was the residence where the Pungcheon Cult Leader stayed.

“What is your first impression of the inner court?”

“I was too scared to look properly.”

“There is nothing to be afraid of. In this vast place, there are only a few people who live as diligently as you do, proprietor.”

“Goodness, please don’t say such things.”

Jo Chunbae waved his hands as if I were talking nonsense, but the pleased corners of his mouth rose higher with every wave.

“I must have been blessed beyond measure. The Second Young Master came to my tavern, and thanks to that I was able to serve dishes to people I could never have dreamed of meeting. I never imagined I would even come all the way to the inner court like this.”

The reason I had brought Jo Chunbae today was ostensibly to have him cook when I drank with the Pungcheon Cult Leader, but in truth it was to show him around the inner court.

At some point, in passing, he had said he wanted to see the inner court of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and today I was going to grant that wish.

These days, my personal cook, Cook Im, was waiting in the outer court with the other cooks to provide meals for the demonic practitioners of the Pungcheon Cult, so I had ample pretext to bring Jo Chunbae along.

“I must have accumulated a great deal of merit in my previous life.”

“You must have saved the murim in your previous life, proprietor.”

“Even if I didn’t save the whole murim, I’m certain I must have beaten down at least one elder of the Murim Alliance who invaded our cult with an iron ladle.”

I laughed pleasantly at his joke. Just then, warriors of the inner court passing by saw me and greeted me respectfully. Jo Chunbae could not even meet the eyes of those burly, frightening warriors.

“Now that I’m actually here, I’m happy, but also afraid.”

“That is why I established the sub-branch in Maga Village. This is not a place everyone can come to easily. So, proprietor, please keep a good eye on the people of Maga Village and the sub-branch, and if there is anything you need to tell me, make sure to pass it on.”

“Yes, I will.”

In that way, we arrived at the residence where the Pungcheon Cult Leader stayed.

“You’ve come?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader welcomed us warmly. I had promised to treat him to drinks once the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable was decided. Since Cheongseon had been chosen and he was in good spirits, and since I had even refused the sacred relic, the Pungcheon Cult Leader looked to be in an excellent mood.

“This is the proprietor of Pungnyu Guesthouse, whom you saw last time. Rather than bringing cold dishes to serve you, I wanted to eat warm dishes made by the proprietor himself, so I brought him along. Proprietor, I leave it to you.”

“I will pour my soul into the dishes I serve.”

“I do not particularly wish to eat your soul, proprietor, so please make them as you normally would.”

“Yes!”

While Jo Chunbae prepared the food, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and I went into the room.

“Congratulations on taking the Demon Venerable as your disciple.”

“It was something I could never have accomplished without you, Second Young Master. Thank you.”

“It is because you taught your disciple well, Cult Leader.”

“Let us call it our victory.”

We exchanged such pleasantries and enjoyed our victory. I had known Cheongseon would win. Once an expert of the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s level had intervened, he would have obtained victory no matter what method he used. The opponent was far too great a figure for First Disciple Yang Do to create a variable.

A short while later, the dishes Jo Chunbae had made and the wine were brought in.

After tasting the food, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was satisfied.

“The cook’s skills are quite good.”

“I will pass that on exactly. The proprietor will be pleased.”

We enjoyed the wine and dishes. As if he had steeled himself for the occasion, Jo Chunbae’s cooking today was excellent.

“Have you met my father?”

“I am to see him in a few days.”

“When you meet him, emphasize this. That you will take Cheongseon as your disciple and raise him into a Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable stronger than the previous one. To my father, that point is the most important.”

“I understand what you mean. Thank you for telling me.”

“Not at all.”

When the drinking party had grown mellow, I spoke to the Pungcheon Cult Leader as if it were nothing important.

“May I offer a cup to the man wearing shackles over there?”

For an instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader flinched.

“Why do you want to give that man wine?”

“There is no special meaning to it. I have seen him several times whenever I came to visit you, Cult Leader, and on such a pleasant day, I simply wanted to give him a cup.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said with a smile,

“That man cannot put alcohol to his lips at all.”

“Is that so?”

It was then. The shackled man opened his mouth.

“I will accept a cup.”

I was startled as well, but the Pungcheon Cult Leader was even more surprised. His expression slowly hardened. From the change in his expression that he could not hide, I could guess.

The shackled man had never once accepted wine from the Pungcheon Cult Leader until now.

“Then I shall give you a cup.”

I rose from my seat, pretending not to see the Pungcheon Cult Leader, who looked as if he might explode at any moment.

Then I took a wine cup and bottle and went over to the shackled man.

I sat before him and held out the cup.

Then the shackled man, who had been lowering his head, lifted it and accepted my cup.

I slowly filled his cup.

I said nothing else. I did not use sound transmission either. I merely poured him a cup of wine.

The shackled man kept his head bowed as he received the wine, then drank it just as he was.

Then he returned the cup to me, and this time he poured for me.

I accepted the wine and emptied it refreshingly.

We, who had only exchanged glances, finally shared wine. Though we had drunk only wine without exchanging a single word, for me, that was enough.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been watching, seemed truly angry. Otherwise, he would not have said such a thing.

“Do you know what that fellow’s specialty is? He has a nose like a dog. He can smell those who come to steal the sacred relic with uncanny precision. What do you think? Do those shackles not look like a dog’s leash? Hahahaha.”

After uttering words closer to contempt than a joke, the Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed loudly.

Only his laughter could be heard in the room.

And then came the silence.

What he had just said was a clear slip of the tongue that belittled the shackled man. I did not cleverly respond and give him a chance to recover from that mistake. This was, in a way, a gift his rashness had given me.

I returned to my original seat and poured wine into his cup, which had already become empty.

“Now, please have a cup.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader silently downed the wine.

The shackled man lowered his head as usual and became as still as a fossil.

Looking at him, the Pungcheon Cult Leader bit his lip quietly. At least when it came to the shackled man, the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not hide or control his emotions. Just as the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was with matters related to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

Earlier than the time I had originally planned, I bid farewell and left the place.

I had given wine to the shackled man, and I had drunk his wine, so I had achieved the purpose of today’s visit.

* * *

After Geom Mugeuk returned, the atmosphere was chilly.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader acknowledged that he had done wrong, yet strangely, he felt displeased. He did not want to apologize, but forced himself to do it.

“I am sorry about earlier.”

Even at the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s apology, the shackled man said nothing.

“I said I’m sorry.”

When there was still no response after his second apology, the Pungcheon Cult Leader finally exploded.

“Damn it! What do you think you’re doing right now? I am your master. You are my subordinate. I can’t even say something like that? I let you do as you pleased all this time, and now you’ve grown arrogant, have you? Do you want to die? Is that it?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader shouted. But the voice in his heart was rebuking himself.

‘What are you doing right now? What did you do so well that you’re even shouting?’

The greater his self-reproach, the louder his voice became.

“How much have I endured until now? Five times speaking informally? What lunatic gives such an opportunity to a subordinate? Me! It was because it was me, was it not? And yet you don’t even know gratitude. You said you couldn’t drink, didn’t you? You said you would hate it even unto death, didn’t you? But you accepted the Second Young Master’s wine? And that, after I had said you could not drink?”

No matter how angry he was, he should have stopped there.

But the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not hold back the rage surging within him and crossed a line he should not have crossed.

The next instant, the space around them changed.

It was not the place with the blue sky and fields that he always opened.

It was the middle of a battlefield where blood flowed and corpses lay scattered. The stench of blood stabbed into the nose, making it difficult to remain there even for a moment.

“This is a place perfectly suited to you. From now on, it will be the only place you can see!”

The decisive reason the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not stop his anger was that, from beginning to end, he had been beating the drum and janggu all by himself. He had made the mistake, and he was the one getting angry.

The shackled man had not said a single word. He had merely accepted a cup of wine.

‘No, that bastard was the one who did wrong first. He should not have accepted that wine. You said it to me, didn’t you? That I am petty and greedy? If you knew that, why are you doing this?’

At that moment, as if he had read his innermost thoughts, the shackled man spoke politely.

“My thoughts were short-sighted. I apologize.”

He was so polite that the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s heart sank.

At the anxiety that he might never again hear the shackled man speak frankly, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s heart began to pound.

‘This is not right. Hurry and apologize. Hurry!’

He shouted inwardly.

But the words “I am sorry” would not come out. He, too, had much accumulated within him.

‘Why should I apologize? I am that man’s master. I am the Pungcheon Cult Leader!’

Though he had quarreled with him several times, they had never experienced such a major conflict. That was why he was flustered, not knowing what to do.

If he had been dealing with someone else, he would have handled it far more coolly. If Demon Buddha had been the opponent, even while shouting outwardly, he would have been calculating inwardly.

But with the shackled man, he could not do that.

The mountain of corpses and sea of blood unfolding before his eyes was the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s own heart. He agonized and agonized, but in the end, the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not apologize.

Unaware of that heart, only the howls of wolves drawn by the smell of blood rang out cruelly.

* * *

I watched Seo Daeryong’s training from a corner of the yard at the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s residence.

Having passed beyond basic training, he was now practicing forms, and the skill with which he swung his saber was different from before.

In the past, I had told Seo Daeryong at Pungnyu Guesthouse that although it was late, he would learn with greater sincerity. That because of that, it would be all right. My words had become reality.

He did not neglect even a single movement. On top of that, just as an intelligent person learned martial arts well, Seo Daeryong was improving by leaps and bounds.

Just then, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon came to my side and stood beside me.

“Did you come to watch your subordinate suffer?”

“He is a friend who suffers plenty during the day as well. I do not have such a nasty hobby.”

“Then what brings you here at this hour?”

“I came to consult you about something.”

“Come in.”

Leaving Seo Daeryong to his training, I entered the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s residence.

I admired the books that filled the wall cabinet.

“You have so many books.”

“Add what you want to say after that.”

“Unfittingly so.”

“They are not books to read, but books for decoration.”

“Even the decoration does not suit you.”

“You brat! If you added up all the books I read in my youth, they would number as many as the people the Cult Leader has killed.”

“Then you would have become a great scholar.”

After exchanging jokes like that, we discussed the main matter.

“What is it you want to consult me about?”

“There is someone whose shackles I want to undo.”

At the unexpected words, the startled Blood Heaven Saber Demon misunderstood.

“Is there someone in the underground prison you want to release?”

“No. There is one person around me.”

Then this time, he took it in a symbolic sense.

“Yes, everyone lives wearing at least one shackle.”

“What are your shackles, Elder?”

“As for me…”

Something seemed about to come out, but it slipped back in, and the Blood Heaven Saber Demon asked,

“Who is this person?”

“A subordinate of the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

“Oh dear.”

His expression already showed he sensed difficulty.

“Since he is someone you wish to obtain, he must be a decent person. And that means there is a high possibility the Pungcheon Cult Leader also cherishes him.”

“Precisely.”

“Does the Pungcheon Cult Leader know that you want that person?”

“He does not.”

“Have you spoken with that subordinate?”

“No, not a word yet. Still, I believe our hearts have connected.”

“Hearts? You believe? This truly is one mountain range after another.”

When I thought of whom I should consult about the shackled man, the first person who came to mind was the Blood Heaven Saber Demon. Even if he could not give me an answer, I felt that while speaking with him, I might be able to find the answer myself. To me, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was that kind of person.

“That is why I came to see you, Elder. Please tell me a method.”

“Did you leave a method in my keeping?”

I smiled brightly and spoke boldly.

“Yes! Please give me what I left with you. Tell me the path out of this endless mountain range.”

Chapter 76: If I Set My Mind to Live.
-------------------------------------
I clearly have a part of me that relies on the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

If there is a part of me that relies on my father, then there is another part that relies on the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Settle it with what you’re best at.”

“What is that?”

“Kill the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

I took it as a joke and burst out laughing, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was serious.

“Why are you laughing? It would be killing two birds with one stone. If you kill the Pungcheon Cult Leader, no one would dare oppose you becoming the successor. As a bonus, you’d gain the subordinate you want.”

“I won’t kill the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

“Why not?”

“He isn’t a good man, but he isn’t so evil that he deserves to die, either.”

“That’s not the real reason, is it? Isn’t it because he’s your treasure vault?”

“Well, that is part of it.”

“You’re the villain here.”

“I admit it.”

“You admit it that easily?”

“How could I catch villains if I were softhearted to the core? I’ll become a proper villain. So I’ll bear the karma and go to hell.”

“Hell is where people like me should go. You should walk a path strewn with flowers.”

Because I could feel he meant it sincerely, I was inwardly moved.

“You’ve already made your decision, haven’t you?”

“Yes.”

After my conversation with Chief Strategist Sama Myeong, I had decided to make the shackled man one of my people.

“He belongs to the Pungcheon Cult Leader, so you can’t just take him by force.”

“That’s right.”

“Then leave it to him. Make him come to you on his own.”

“He’s a man bound in shackles.”

“He has to break those shackles himself. If he can’t show at least that much ability, would he even be worth bringing over?”

“You’re right, but I’m not confident I’ve given him that much certainty. We haven’t even exchanged a single word yet.”

That was why I had come to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Others might not understand, but I believed that he, at least, would understand how I felt.

And indeed, instead of cursing me as a madman, he gave me an answer I had never considered.

“Then give him that certainty.”

“How?”

“Show him your people.”

“!”

“If you show him your people, he’ll be able to tell what kind of person you are. If the person you’re trying to recruit is intelligent, then just by looking at your people, he’ll become certain about you. He probably won’t be able to endure not coming.”

I stared at him in silence. To think he would give me advice like this. Because his own experience was imbued in those words, the advice was all the more valuable.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“That is the best advice.”

“Honestly, it isn’t that good, is it?”

“Among my people, you are number one, Elder. So it is the best advice.”

“Then it’s the worst advice, isn’t it?”

This time, I laughed first, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed after me.

“If he sees me, even someone who was going to come will run away, so show him the others. I didn’t say that with myself in mind.”

I spoke firmly to him.

“You must come too, Elder.”

“No.”

“It’s not because I want to show him you.”

“Then?”

“I want to show you as well, Elder. Whom I have chosen.”

“Why?”

“Wouldn’t that let you know better what kind of person I am?”

“So why?”

“Because I want to give you certainty about me. Because I want to grow closer to you, Elder. This is what truly counts as killing two birds with one stone.”

The corners of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s eyes twitched. It was usually the expression he made when he was angry, but now it was a reaction born of awkwardness.

“You really do say such embarrassing things to a man’s face.”

“Let’s go.”

I sprang up from my seat.

“Now?”

“You’ll be going anyway, so why put it off? Let’s take everyone who has time. I’ll show all of you to one another. Whom I’ve chosen. And by that, what kind of person I am.”

“You really are… a madman.”

“Hahaha.”

Exactly. Making even one person mine is this difficult.

That applied to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and to the shackled man as well.

* * *

The air in the room was cold.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was looking out the window, and the shackled man was looking at the Yin Thunder Bell. For a long while, neither of them said a single word.

As for who would speak first, the winner of this contest had been decided from the start. The shackled man could probably go an entire year without uttering a word.

In the end, the Pungcheon Cult Leader opened his mouth first.

“I’m sorry.”

It was an apology he had struggled to make. For that reason, he hoped the answer would not come easily, but the shackled man spoke as if he had been waiting.

“It’s all right.”

He had agonized and agonized before apologizing, yet it was accepted so lightly that the Pungcheon Cult Leader flared up again.

But earlier, and now as well, this was not a situation in which he had any right to be angry. Whatever else might be said, opening the space piled with corpses had truly been a great mistake. Especially when he knew so well how much the shackled man loved the space with the blue sky and open fields.

“Lately, after giving the divine relic to the Second Young Master, it seems anger built up in me without my realizing it. I’m sorry.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader apologized again.

“It’s all right.”

At the answer, spoken in the same unwavering tone, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face hardened. Not confident that he could hide that expression, he continued looking out the window. He bit his lip until it bled.

All sorts of distracting thoughts tormented him.

‘Is all of this because I’m narrow-minded and greedy? Because I’m a petty man? Damn it! I can’t even win the heart of a single subordinate, so how am I supposed to march into the Central Plains? The others must be even worse, right? They swear loyalty outwardly, but inside, they might be laughing at me.’

If he tried circulating his qi at a time like this, he really would suffer qi deviation.

Just then, a single sentence came from behind him.

“Cult Leader.”

The moment he heard those words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was overcome with emotion. He had been angry and blaming himself, but deep down, he had been anxiously afraid that he might never hear those words again.

“What?”

“Cult Leader, while keeping me bound with shackles, you’re trying to bind my heart as well. I told you before, didn’t I? Neither you nor I are people blessed enough to possess everything without giving anything up.”

“I am the man who rules the martial world beyond the borders! If I’m not blessed, then who is?”

“Did you seize the martial world beyond the borders with your own power? Cult Leader, you merely inherited it. Well, if you call that a blessing, then it is certainly a great one. But the blessing I’m speaking of isn’t something handed down through an umbilical cord.”

“You insolent little—! You’re making me furious again!”

At that, the shackled man closed his mouth.

An awkward silence passed for a moment.

Whether he spoke or remained silent, it infuriated him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader knew exactly where his anger today had begun.

“Why did you accept the Second Young Master’s wine?”

“Just because.”

“What do you mean, just because? There must be a reason. Tell me. I have the right to hear it.”

The shackled man did not answer. The Pungcheon Cult Leader abruptly asked something he truly had not wanted to ask.

“Don’t tell me you want to go to the Second Young Master?”

The shackled man neither affirmed nor denied it.

In that instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader felt the strength leave his legs.

“You’re really going to go? Truly?”

“I haven’t decided anything yet.”

“That means the same thing!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader shouted, his eyes narrowing into slits.

“When did you conspire together? Did you exchange sound transmissions behind my back?”

He would almost have preferred to hear that they had.

“We have never exchanged sound transmissions.”

“Then how?”

“I read his heart. The Second Young Master wants me.”

“Cut the bullshit! I said cut the bullshit! What are you, imitating the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable? Read his heart? What hearts do you think you can read?”

Only now did the Pungcheon Cult Leader understand why he had been so irritated and angry when Geom Mugeuk poured wine for the shackled man earlier. It had not been because the shackled man accepted the Second Young Master’s wine while refusing his own.

He had sensed this very thing. His fate, his instinct, his premonition had read this damned situation.

“You think I’ll let you go? I’d kill you before I ever let you leave!”

“Then you should kill me now.”

“What?”

“Kill me while you still can. Later, you won’t be able to.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s entire body trembled with rage.

The shackled man was the only person who understood his heart. Kill such a person? Have him taken away by someone else?

“You’re doing this because you think I can’t kill you, aren’t you? Because you look down on me!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader strode toward the shackled man.

He raised his hand high.

If he struck down with one palm, he would be freed from every torment.

If the shackled man had looked up at his face, perhaps he really might have struck.

But the shackled man remained still with his head lowered, so that he would not feel the sting of conscience.

Seeing him meekly accept death, the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not bring his palm down.

“If you can’t kill me now, you’ll never be able to kill me. If I decide to live, no one will be able to kill me. So if you’re going to kill me, you must do it now.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s hand, raised in midair, trembled.

But in the end, he could not strike.

Slowly, the Pungcheon Cult Leader lowered his hand. The rage that had erupted like lava had, before he knew it, calmly settled.

“You think you can run away alone from this chaotic world? This easily? Not a chance! I won’t let it happen.”

The shackled man slowly raised his head. With complicated emotions, the two men’s gazes tangled in the air.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had not yet abandoned hope.

“You said you haven’t decided to leave yet, didn’t you?”

“That’s right. Not yet.”

“Then all I have to do is not lose you, isn’t it? I just have to become a better man than the Second Young Master, don’t I?”

A smile appeared at the corners of the shackled man’s mouth. It was not a forced smile, but a natural one.

“Yes. Keep living like that from now on.”

“I told you not to talk as if we’re parting!”

“Don’t obsess over me. I only look greater because someone else is trying to have me.”

“Shut up! Give me a chance to persuade you before deciding whom you’ll serve. They say even hateful ties are still ties, and since we’ve spent years in the same room, I should at least be able to struggle. I’ll cling to you with everything I have.”

“Even then, it won’t work. He is not someone you can contend with.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a sigh. The divine relic, now emptied, had already told him that this was beyond him.

“I can’t let you go for nothing. If you’re going, take me with you.”

The shackled man gave a faint laugh. It was the first time he had laughed today since their quarrel.

“Fine. Let’s go to the Second Young Master together.”

“What?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stared blankly, never having expected him to say they should go together.

“Are you insane?”

But the shackled man’s next words were even more astonishing.

“You said you resolved to make the Second Young Master the Heavenly Demon, didn’t you? Come with me, and let’s make him the Heavenly Demon. Then your dream will naturally come true as well. If it is the Second Young Master as he is now, he is the kind of person who would even build the Pungcheon Cult’s main headquarters beside the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“You lunatic! How can you call that an argument?”

At that moment, a subordinate spoke from outside.

“The Second Young Master and his guests have arrived.”

“Guests?”

“Yes. There are as many as five people with him.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader sighed and shook his head.

“Ah! He really does push forward frighteningly hard.”

Ordinarily, he would have snapped in irritation, but the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face was comfortable, as if he had let go of everything.

“You’re right. We talk about the Second Young Master far too much. That’s why he always comes looking for me like a tiger that heard us speaking of it.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said to his subordinate,

“Show them in at once. And prepare plenty of wine and food.”

“Yes!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at the shackled man. His gaze was different from usual.

“It seems fate wants us to settle this over you. Fine. Let’s decide today. Whether the Second Young Master dies, or I die. Whether you go to the Second Young Master, or remain with me. Let us decide everything today. But judge fairly.”

“In that regard, the Second Young Master is actually at a disadvantage. As you said, Cult Leader, I’ve grown more attached to you.”

“Good. Then that’s enough.”

Soon after, Geom Mugeuk and his party stepped inside.

As if nothing had happened, the Pungcheon Cult Leader greeted his guests with a hearty laugh.

“Hahaha, welcome, all of you!”

And so began the decisive drinking gathering that would change their fates.

Chapter 77: What If Someone Tries to Take That Seat?
----------------------------------------------------
Today, I brought all of my people with me.

From the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had proposed this method, to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, Ian and Seo Daeryong, and even the Demon Army Lord Jang Ho.

I did not tell the others why we were visiting the Pungcheon Cult Leader. I did not want them to try to do anything deliberately. Today’s gathering needed no persuasion, nor any need to move anyone’s heart. They only had to show themselves as they were.

“Such honored guests have come together. Welcome, all of you!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, greeting us with both arms spread wide, looked happier than he had ever looked since I had met him.

He had made a grave slip of the tongue with the shackled man. Had they already reconciled?

“I suddenly felt inclined to pay you a visit, and so I came with the companions who were with me. Please forgive the discourtesy.”

“It is quite all right. Is this not an opportunity to mingle with the heroes of the Divine Cult?”

“Your heart is as vast as the sea.”

The first among those who came with me to offer greetings was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Good to see you.”

“Welcome.”

Naturally, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was well acquainted with the Demon Venerables.

“I heard that you have grown quite close with the Second Young Master recently.”

“You heard wrong. It is not that we have merely grown close. I am the Second Young Master’s left wing.”

Among ourselves, we had exchanged jokes about wings easily enough, but I had not expected the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to say it to someone else.

Perhaps he meant to show the shackled man that this was how much he trusted Geom Mugeuk. Even I had not expected him to show such a side, so how shocked must the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who had come with us, have been?

I knew she would not be eager to attend this gathering, but I had asked her to come so that she might see the Blood Heaven Blade Demon even one more time. There was also my own will to make her one of my people.

After staring at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon with a startled expression for a moment, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable said to the Pungcheon Cult Leader,

“That does not mean I am the right wing.”

It was her determination not to be entangled with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon in any way, but the Pungcheon Cult Leader took it as a witty joke and expressed his gratitude to her.

“I heard you gave great help in Cheongseon becoming a Demon Venerable this time.”

“I did it because I trusted the Second Young Master.”

She gave the credit to me. And then added one more thing.

“And the one who gave truly great help was the Blade Demon. I obtained an abstention, but Senior Blade Demon obtained a full vote.”

Though she did not mention a specific name, she made it clear that we had gained one vote thanks to the Blade Demon. No matter how much she disliked the Blade Demon, her nature was such that she could not simply sit by and take credit for another’s merit.

It was the first I had heard of it as well. Naturally, I had assumed that the Ilhwa Sword Venerable had secured one vote, while the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had created an abstention.

But the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had secured a vote? This eccentric old man had done that and yet had not come to me even once to boast about it.

I sent a sound transmission to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

—You said you were being ostracized, did you not?

—Perhaps someone helped me out of pity.

—If you are ostracized just twice more, you will have all the Demon Venerables kneeling.

—I will never again go around asking for favors.

—Thank you.

—Enough.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader cupped his fist to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and expressed his gratitude.

“You are better than my friend.”

“Was it not strange to seek friends in the jianghu to begin with? People meet and part according to necessity at the time. Living that way keeps life clean in many respects.”

“I will not forget your good teaching.”

With that, the two Demon Venerables finished exchanging greetings, and this time Jang Ho greeted him.

“I am Demon Army Lord Jang Ho. It is an honor to meet you.”

“To meet the valiant Demon Army Lord like this—rather, the honor is mine.”

“I pestered the Second Young Master to let me meet the Supreme Beyond the Passes.”

“It is nothing but an empty name. I only hope you will not be disappointed.”

Jang Ho had heard my request and followed me without even asking the reason. This dependable quality, the sense that he would silently carry out any task entrusted to him, was something unique to the man called Jang Ho.

Next, Seo Daeryong offered his greeting.

“I do not know whether I belong in such a gathering, but I am Seo Daeryong, an investigator of the Hwangcheon Pavilion.”

“I know well of you. They say you are our Second Young Master’s right arm?”

At that, Seo Daeryong’s eyes widened as he looked at me. His expression seemed to say, Did you really say even that? I smiled and said,

“Seeing that he knows even without my saying it, I suppose everyone truly does think so.”

The last to greet him was Ian.

“I am Ian, who assists the Second Young Master. It is an honor to meet you.”

“Eyes brimming with living spirit. It has truly been a long time.”

“Thank you for looking kindly upon me.”

Her aura, immersed in the ecstasy of training these days, was like a single blade.

A sharp aura that seemed ready to cut anyone.

How to lessen that keen edge would be her task from now on.

“If Investigator Seo over there is my right arm, then Ian here is someone like my heart.”

When I said that in front of everyone, Ian was flustered. Her face flushed red, but I spoke proudly, as though making a proclamation.

“All of you, know this. Ian is my heart.”

What one says when alone together and what one says in front of others are as different as heaven and earth. If they are words one can say when it is just the two of you, then one should be able to say them in front of everyone. That is a true relationship.

But in ordinary cases, it is not easy to encounter such a relationship. Truly, it is not easy.

In that sense, meeting Ian could be called a great fortune for me. Not merely because of the life debt from before my regression. As I had told my father, it was because she was an excellent person.

A person who, while saying she hid as many as nine tails, could sacrifice her own life for someone else.

Because she had grown close to me, she had revealed those words. Otherwise, she would have hidden the fact that she was a fox for the rest of her life and fulfilled her duties like a bear.

“That said, you do not need to throw yourself away for me. Just because someone values me highly does not mean you need to sacrifice yourself for that person. You must not forget. Those two things are clearly separate.”

That was my view on superiors and subordinates.

Though the words were spoken to Ian, at the same time, they were also spoken to the shackled man.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader exclaimed in admiration.

“I envy you for having someone you can praise like this.”

Beyond his shoulder, I could see the shackled man. What was he thinking?

“Since the Second Young Master is boasting of his subordinate like this, I cannot simply sit still either. Now, I shall introduce one person as well. The one wearing those shackles over there is this man’s right arm.”

Inwardly, I was surprised. Until now, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had never once introduced the shackled man, yet today he had. Moreover, he had even called him his right arm. Something must have changed between the two of them after that slip of the tongue.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked openly,

“How is it that you keep your right arm bound in shackles?”

“He is someone who would ascend to the heavens if I took my eyes off him even for a moment, so I have held him here by force.”

“Should you not let one who is meant to ascend ascend?”

“That is not as easy as it sounds.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded with an expression that said he understood everything.

“Indeed. These damned imugi are so willful that humans like us cannot handle them easily.”

As he said that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at me. While saying that one should release those who must be sent away, he was also praising me by saying I was a dragon who would ascend.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon truly was quick-witted and good at reading the atmosphere.

If only he were ten years younger—no, twenty years younger—I might have made the Blood Heaven Blade Demon my strategist. Of course, he would leap up and insist he would never do it.

In the meantime, a table of drinks was set.

We sat around it and poured drinks for one another.

I do not think fate controls everything about human beings. Fate merely provides us with opportunities. Judging and seizing those opportunities is up to people.

I had investigated and prepared a great deal for my regression, but the shackled man had been left out of that. Even though he might become someone who would play a truly important role in my life after regression.

But that was all right.

Rather, because I had not known and prepared in advance, a gathering like this had come about. A gathering where I could show who I was, and in the end, make him look back on himself.

The one to open today’s gathering was Ian.

After several rounds of drinks had gone around, Ian stepped forward cautiously.

“Would it be all right if I offered that person a cup of wine?”

The person she meant was the shackled man. It was not something I had told her to do. Previously, because I had given him wine, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had made one great verbal mistake.

Ian, who did not know that, was making the same suggestion.

Unlike the reaction he had shown me earlier, the Pungcheon Cult Leader smiled and said,

“He will like it as well.”

He had certainly changed. What on earth had happened?

Ian took a wine bottle and cup over to him, poured him wine, and asked politely,

“Martial artist, are you guarding this bell?”

I thought he would not answer, but unexpectedly, the shackled man opened his mouth.

“At first, I was… but now, the bell is protecting me.”

Whether Ian knew how precious that answer was or not, she continued the conversation.

“That is similar to me.”

The shackled man lifted his head slightly and looked at her.

“At first, I protected the young master, but now the young master protects me.”

I saw it. The faint smile that formed at the corners of the shackled man’s mouth. Perhaps that was why Ian could continue speaking so naturally.

“That bell does not tell you to leave, does it? Our young master keeps telling me to leave. He says he wants me to live my own life. Is that really why? Or is he trying to send me away because he dislikes me?”

At that, the shackled man answered her question again.

“He would not call someone his heart in front of everyone if he meant to send her away because he disliked her.”

It was a new side of the shackled man. He had already answered Ian’s questions twice.

Just as Ian’s question had been from the start, I wondered if the shackled man’s answer was also not directed at Ian, but at me.

“Ah, I thought so.”

Ian looked toward me. I felt that she was unfurling yet another of her tails. Her intuition must have told her that the reason I had brought them all here had something to do with this man. Otherwise, she would not have held such a conversation with the shackled man.

I tapped my heart twice, then pointed at her with my finger.

Ian smiled brightly.

“It felt like a joke because you keep doing that too much.”

At that moment, Seo Daeryong, who had been sitting down, picked up his cup and went over to join them. His movement was so natural that there was no question of stopping him or not.

Seo Daeryong sat beside Ian and said,

“I think it is because he worries too much about this martial artist.”

“About me?”

“Yes. From what I have observed of the Pavilion Master so far, his desire for his subordinates not to die is great. It is like the heart of a parent who wants them to leave behind the perilous mountain of blades and forest of swords and live well. I also think he had me learn martial arts from Master because of such worries.”

“Ah! Perhaps that may be true.”

Ian looked at me again. Since she had received the Bicheon Sword Art, of course those words must have sounded plausible to her.

I said to her,

“That is a misunderstanding. I am teaching you so I can work you properly from now on. If you are that innocent, you will be used for the rest of your life!”

Then Seo Daeryong answered in her stead.

“When one works, there are times when being under someone who works you properly is more comfortable. It is harder under a clumsy and indecisive leader.”

Ian extended her cup as though she agreed, and Seo Daeryong clinked his with hers.

“Investigator Seo, why do you like our young master so much?”

“Does it look as though I like him?”

“If not, you would not have come over here, would you?”

Ian had seen through Seo Daeryong’s personality at a glance.

She was right. For him, who hated standing out and hated danger, voluntarily going to sit beside Ian was an act of courage in its own way.

“Because I am his right arm.”

He said it like a joke, but that one sentence contained much of Seo Daeryong’s feelings.

It was then. The shackled man raised his head and asked quietly,

“What will you do if someone tries to take that place as the right arm?”

It was not an answer to a question, but something he had brought up first. Those first words were extremely provocative.

At the unexpected question, Seo Daeryong was startled. The shackled man’s eyes, visible between the locks of hair hanging down, were intense.

A complicated state of mind showed on Seo Daeryong’s face as he wondered how to answer, but soon he replied calmly,

“I am timid and fearful, so I do not think I would be able to stand and fight. Tell them to take it. In preparation for such a day, I am working hard and sweating to become someone else’s right arm. Fortunately, every person has a right arm.”

At that answer, the provocative light in the shackled man’s eyes settled calmly.

Meanwhile, because he knew that he was the subject of that glib remark, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon wore an expression of disbelief. I looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and said,

“Raise him well.”

“I refuse.”

At that moment, Demon Army Lord Jang Ho looked toward them and said something unexpected.

“I am next in line as a candidate for the right arm. Investigator Seo may be good-natured enough to hand that place over, but I will not hand the position of right arm to just anyone.”

It was the moment Jang Ho revealed a new side of himself.

Chapter 78: They're All Crazy Bastards.
---------------------------------------
Jang Ho was not the type to step forward by nature.

But he did not like the attitude of the shackled man, who dared to speak so arrogantly about the position of right-hand man.

He was the first to realize that today’s gathering was because of the shackled man.

-Perhaps the reason we came today is because of that shackled man.

So he had even sent a voice transmission to Ian and Seo Daeryong.

Ian bravely took action, and Seo Daeryong moved as well.

Jang Ho, who had abundant real combat experience, had instinctively realized that this place was a battlefield where an unseen fight would continue.

At Jang Ho’s words, the shackled man raised his head and stared at him. The large scar on Jang Ho’s face twitched.

The shackled man said to Jang Ho,

“Why are you so fixated on being the right-hand man? If it were me, I would become the left hand and watch the right hands fight.”

“I have no hobby of enjoying other people’s fights. Since I set becoming the right-hand man as my goal, I will strive to become the right-hand man.”

“How manly of you.”

“Thank you.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had only been watching until then, sent a voice transmission to the shackled man.

-Manly? Bullshit. Since when did you say things like that?

-You just don’t know me well. I am a man who can say all sorts of things. I make jokes and banter too.

-Damn it! Why say something like that in this situation?

-The Cult Leader started it first.

-Let’s say that’s true. But what are these people trying to do, flocking to you like this?

-Can’t you tell by watching? They’re all working hard for their leader.

-Cowardly! I’m alone.

-What have you been doing all this time? The Cult Leader has so many subordinates.

-So? The Second Young Master is the kind of leader who cherishes his subordinates more?

-Even if I go to the Second Young Master, it will be for reasons that have nothing to do with them. It would be the same even if a hundred people came rushing in to praise him. So you don’t need to worry about them.

-Then for what reason would you go?

But the shackled man did not tell him the reason.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said to Geom Mugeuk,

“The Second Young Master has good subordinates.”

“I’m sure you have far more good subordinates, Cult Leader.”

“No. I am a lonely man. Other than that man over there, no one understands me.”

“To have someone who understands you is one of life’s great blessings.”

“I hope that blessing stays by my side for a long time.”

Geom Mugeuk did not say that, if that was the case, he ought to remove the shackles. He knew very well that pressing him by bringing up the shackles would instead be a disadvantageous choice for himself.

“Now, accept a cup from me.”

As the Pungcheon Cult Leader poured the wine, he imbued it with internal energy. Imbuing internal energy into the wine without breaking the cup was a technique only true masters could perform; unless one possessed internal energy on par with his, they would never be able to receive the wine to the end.

Gurgle.

But until the cup was filled to the brim, Geom Mugeuk’s expression did not change in the slightest.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was inwardly greatly shocked.

‘Could it be? He surpasses my internal energy? Impossible!’

Compared to the stunned Cult Leader, Geom Mugeuk’s expression was gentle. He accepted the wine, drank it, and held out the cup.

“This time, please accept a cup from me.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression hardened. If he failed to receive Geom Mugeuk’s cup, he would be humiliated before all these people.

But in terms of momentum, he could not refuse.

“Good. Let me receive the wine of our ascending hidden dragon.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader received the wine.

This time as well, internal energy was carried in the falling wine. The Pungcheon Cult Leader accepted it while making an effort to remain calm. It was internal energy he could endure well enough.

“The wine tastes excellent.”

“Let’s drink until we’re drunk today.”

“Good.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader triumphantly sent a voice transmission to the shackled man.

-Did you see? Receiving the wine imbued with the Second Young Master’s internal energy? It was nothing.

-The Second Young Master saved the Cult Leader’s face. He probably didn’t want to, but he went out of his way to imbue it with internal energy because the Cult Leader had done so first, and if he poured normally, the Cult Leader would have been embarrassed. That’s why he didn’t put all his internal energy into it either.

-…….

In truth, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had felt it before anyone else. They had exchanged internal energy through their bodies, so how could he not know? He had only exposed his own limits by trying to show off to the shackled man for no reason.

-Don’t be discouraged. I know very well that the Cult Leader is this kind of person.

-Because of you, I feel even more diminished. I’m normally a better person than this, but I keep making mistakes.

-I understand that too.

Geom Mugeuk had noticed that the Pungcheon Cult Leader was exchanging voice transmissions with the shackled man.

But he pretended not to know and drank his wine. At that moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s voice transmission flew in.

-Why didn’t you humiliate him?

-If his pride is wounded, the situation will become worse.

-When you had the chance to show him, you should have shown him. You should have trampled him properly. People’s hearts move in simpler places than you think.

-He must have seen something more important than that.

-Aren’t you overestimating him too much?

-Perhaps I am.

The drinking continued again.

Geom Mugeuk drank with the Pungcheon Cult Leader and exchanged calm conversation with him, but refrained from words or actions that attacked him. His strategy was not to go after the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Geom Mugeuk focused on why he had come here today. On showing his people to the shackled man.

“Investigator Seo. How is your martial arts training going these days?”

“It’s difficult, but I’m working hard.”

Then the Blood Heaven Blade Demon cut in.

“Difficult?”

“No, it isn’t difficult.”

“Is that so? I’ll have to increase the amount of training.”

“No, it’s difficult enough to kill me.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and joined their conversation.

“Catering to the elder’s mood must be as hard as striking my father’s vital point.”

“Master is not that difficult.”

“You’re calling me Master again.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon bluntly rebuked Seo Daeryong. As if it had not been a mistake, Seo Daeryong said confidently,

“Though you have not accepted me as a formal disciple, I think of you as my teacher. Even if you drive me away tomorrow, I will remember you as my master forever.”

“Who gave you permission to remember me?”

“Are you saying I can’t even remember as I please?”

The one who added strength to Geom Mugeuk’s scolding was the One Flower Sword Venerable.

“That’s the sort of person he is.”

At her curt remark, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not flare up.

“Do you even know me properly before saying that?”

“Clearly. I’m the one who doesn’t want to remember.”

The two carried on a war of nerves without raising their voices.

Geom Mugeuk did not stop them. Showing them as they were was the purpose of today’s visit. The problem was that they were showing themselves too much as they were.

Geom Mugeuk silently poured wine for them.

The One Flower Sword Venerable toyed with her wine cup, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon immediately emptied his.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at that sight with a somewhat unfamiliar emotion. It seemed he had never imagined that the Demon Venerables would have an emotional fight in front of him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader sent a voice transmission to the shackled man and asked,

-What on earth are they trying to do? What is their intention?

-You knew those two were on bad terms, didn’t you?

-But they’re openly showing their emotions and fighting in front of me? Those sly old foxes? That can’t be. Do you know what kind of people they are?

-It’s probably not because they’re in front of the Cult Leader.

-!

The Pungcheon Cult Leader gently bit his lip and looked at Geom Mugeuk and the two Demon Venerables.

As though they were wings on either side, they sat with Geom Mugeuk between them and freely revealed their emotions without caring about him.

Even that sight came to the Pungcheon Cult Leader as a burden. Loneliness rushed over him, as if he were standing alone on a remote rocky island.

‘It can’t go on like this!’

When he first began this gathering, he truly thought he could face Geom Mugeuk with great momentum. He felt he might be able to bring about some impressive reversal, but…….

But reality was that he was losing in momentum, losing in internal energy, losing in magnanimity, and on top of that, the people who had come with him were moving differently from what he had expected.

The woman suddenly offered wine to the shackled man, that small and gloomy fellow stepped forward and chimed in, and the Demon Army Lord, unfittingly, coveted the position of right-hand man. And now the two Demon Venerables were having an emotional fight with each other.

‘These people are a pack of madmen.’

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was plunged into all sorts of worries. In his heart, he wanted to overturn the table and shout.
