-You bastards! Won’t all of you get lost? Do you know who I am, coming here in a pack and making this fuss? Do you want to go to war with the martial world beyond the passes? If you want to come at me, then all of you come!

But he could not do that. If he had possessed even that much boldness, perhaps such a moment would never have come.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader did not give up until the end.

He poured wine for Geom Mugeuk and said in a pleasant tone,

“Our cult will spare no support to ensure that you become the next Heavenly Demon.”

“Thank you. I, too, will actively assist the Pungcheon Cult’s advance into the Central Plains.”

“This time, thanks to your help, my disciple became a Demon Venerable. You wanted no reward for it, but I must reward you.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader showed that he no longer begrudged even a divine relic. He was trying to show the shackled man that he had changed. To show his resolve to change. To show that he was not forever a narrow-minded and greedy person.

“Choose.”

“Will you truly give me what I want?”

“I will. If it is in this room, I will give you anything.”

Everyone looked at Geom Mugeuk, waiting to see what he would choose.

At last, Geom Mugeuk spoke to the Pungcheon Cult Leader. Words no one present had imagined flowed from his mouth.

“Please give me those shackles.”

At the statement that he wanted the shackles, everyone was startled.

Among them, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s surprise was the expected sort of surprise that said, “What was bound to come has finally come.”

That was why he became even angrier.

‘How dare he make such a demand when I said he was my right-hand man?’

The Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke firmly with a cold gaze.

“I can never give you that man.”

But he had misunderstood Geom Mugeuk’s request.

“I am not asking for the person. I said I want the shackles made of Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled. It was truly something he had never expected.

“What do you plan to use the shackles for?”

“To tie up someone who won’t listen.”

He said it like a joke while looking at Seo Daeryong.

Seo Daeryong stuck out his lower lip and said,

“Shackles are for tying up people who listen well. You mustn’t tie up someone who doesn’t listen, like me! That’s when something terrible happens.”

He had said it jokingly, but the Pungcheon Cult Leader flinched, shaken. It was a problem he had never once thought about.

Then Geom Mugeuk revealed to the Pungcheon Cult Leader the true purpose for which he wanted the shackles.

“I want to melt that Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron and make something more valuable. A treasure of that caliber would be hard to find even among divine relics.”

In any case, the man could be bound with another set of shackles. So this request was a symbolic one. It was an expression of his intention that he wanted to set him free.

“So please give me those shackles.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader fell into contemplation.

‘What should I do?’

At a time like this, it would have been good if the shackled man sent him some voice transmission.

‘But this is about removing his own shackles, so there’s no way he would send one.’

At that moment, the shackled man sent a voice transmission.

-Refuse.

-What?

-For the Cult Leader’s sake, it is a request you must not accept.

-Why are you telling me?

-Didn’t you ask me to be fair?

The Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a sigh without realizing it. He had not known that Geom Mugeuk would ask for the shackles, nor that the shackled man would send a voice transmission.

‘Everyone is smarter than me, so I cannot keep up with my own head.’

He had really thought he could do well…… but the gap between ideal and reality was so vast that it drove him to despair.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was conscious of the gazes directed at him.

“A promise must be kept.”

It was not because of pride or face. It was because he judged that only by releasing him now would the shackled man see him in a better light.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader took out the key hanging from his necklace. Then he slowly walked over and unlocked the man’s shackles. During the process, they said nothing. They did not exchange voice transmissions either. He simply unlocked the shackles, and the man merely watched.

Clank.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader brought the shackles and key before me and set them down.

“Take them.”

“Thank you.”

The shackled man remained seated there, head lowered in the same posture as before.

Geom Mugeuk asked the shackled man,

“Is there perhaps something you would like made from what these are melted into?”

Everyone’s startled gazes focused on Geom Mugeuk.

They had never imagined that he would return the melted Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron to the shackled man.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader felt dizzy and squeezed his eyes shut.

He thought that the only thing he could do against these people was overturn the drinking table.

Chapter 79: So Do I.
--------------------
Just as he was about to overturn the table, a stream of internal energy flowed forth and pressed it down.

He raised his head to see Hyeolcheon Doma.

He sent a telepathic message to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Restrain yourself. If the table is flipped, what will be revealed is not the underside of the table, but the underside of the Cult Leader.”

Sensing the dissuasion came from goodwill, the Pungcheon Cult Leader removed his hand from the table. Hyeolcheon Doma's eyes said it: I understand you, to some degree.

Geom Mugeuk spoke again to the shackled man.

“You have been bound here for a long time, so I thought you might want to keep something made from these shackles.”

The shackled man did not refuse and requested a single item.

“Then please make me a fan.”

“Will that be enough?”

“It is sufficient.”

I entrusted the shackles to Jangho.

“Take these to Artisan Gwak at our cult's forge and have him make a fan with Myriad Year Cold Iron ribs, and from the remainder, have him forge eight daggers—one for each person gathered here today. Since our gathering today is also worth commemorating, let us each take one.”

The eight included both the Pungcheon Cult Leader and the shackled man.

At that, Seo Daeryong spoke.

“I cannot speak for the others, but I am not sure I am worthy of receiving such a precious dagger.”

“Nor I.”

“I feel the same.”

Ian and Jangho stepped forward as well.

“Instead of acting humble, work yourselves to the bone to repay me!”

At my jest, everyone lowered their heads in gratitude.

“Thank you truly.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader's reaction, by contrast, was cold.

“Why do you bother preparing one for me as well?”

“Because I wish to commemorate today.”

“But I do not wish to commemorate it.”

At that moment, the shackled man sent a telepathic message.

“Accept it graciously. He is agitated because of me, but the Cult Leader must still accomplish the grand endeavor of advancing into the Central Plains, must he not?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was immensely grateful to the shackled man. Until the very end, the man was trying. It was only he himself who could not meet expectations.

“I was merely jesting. Give me one as well. Those shackles hold meaning for me too.”

“Yes, of course.”

“By the way, you are truly remarkable. Daggers forged of Myriad Year Cold Iron would be difficult to acquire even in the Central Plains. To think you would distribute them to everyone.”

“That is how precious these people are.”

“Is there any guarantee that these precious people will remain precious later on?”

“This gift is not given with an eye toward what comes later.”

“Then why?”

“It is a gift given in gratitude for all they have done for me until now.”

At Geom Mugeuk's words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader could not continue. As if understanding his state of mind, Hyeolcheon Doma poured him a drink and spoke.

“Did we not say so earlier? That ascending dragons are beyond our handling. Simply letting them go with a light heart is the wisest course.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader emptied his cup in silence.

The drinking gathering continued, precarious and ready to erupt at any moment.

After passing cups back and forth for a round or two, everyone grew drunk.

“Inspector Seo, please stop. At this rate, you'll be grabbing your master's pant legs again today.”

At Ian's dissuasion, Seo Daeryong shouted loudly.

“I am perfectly fine!”

“But you're already slurring your words.”

“I told you not to worry! I am not drunk.”

Whether because he had much weighing on him lately, Seo Daeryong began drinking heavily, and Ian, who had been trying to stop him, was also taking small sips of the liquor he had refrained from throughout his training.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jangho. Jangho sent a look saying he would take good care of the two, so there was no need to worry.

“Shouldn't it be the other way around? You getting drunk and those two stopping you.”

“So it would seem.”

“If they show signs of throwing a drunken fit, knock them out with those big fists of yours.”

“That will not be easy either. Both of them are in extraordinary form these days.”

Of course, Seo Daeryong still had a long way to go, but in Ian's case, one could not say he would unconditionally lose to Jangho. The Bicheon Sword Technique was a martial art that could kill even Jangho if his guard were down.

Watching the scene, the Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke to the Demon Lords.

“The world has turned upside down. It has become a world where we must cater to the whims of our inferiors.”

It was a lament directed not at Ian and Seo Daeryong, but at the shackled man. Then he asked Geom Mugeuk.

“What does the Second Young Master think? Even if you become Cult Leader, do you intend to run the cult while watching your subordinates' moods?”

Instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk asked the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Do you know the common trait of incompetent people?”

“Why, is it not because they are incapable and lazy?”

“That may be so. However, there is a more common trait.”

“What is it?”

“They have no tact.”

In that instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader's expression hardened. Clearly, Geom Mugeuk was about to say something that contradicted his own words.

“You mean to say I should watch my subordinates' moods? Will the cult function properly then? Will we not be devoured by the orthodox bastards instead?”

“Only by watching them can you know what kind of person a subordinate is. Only by watching them can you know what is in their hearts. I refuse forced loyalty.”

“Enough!”

In the end, the Pungcheon Cult Leader overturned the table.

Everyone looked at him with startled faces as the table crashed over. Wine and food spilled on their clothes, but no one paid it any mind.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader shouted.

“It is pretense. All pretense to look good before you. If any subordinate disobeys, I would drag them away in secret and kill them. I am not trying to watch my subordinates' moods—I am watching yours. I am putting on airs because I want to take you with me.”

His words echoed thunderously. To others, it seemed a sudden outburst, but for him, it was an explosion of accumulated grievances.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had held back from overturning the table countless times. But the moment he heard the words “forced loyalty” among Geom Mugeuk's final remarks, the dam he had been holding back collapsed.

For lately, he had been tormented by self-blame and even a sense of victimization, believing that both the shackled man and his other subordinates were forcing their loyalty upon him.

Only silence flowed through that place.

Staring at the overturned underside of the table, the Pungcheon Cult Leader clenched his teeth.

‘Damn it! What do you want me to do? This is who I am! What am I supposed to do about not wanting to hear those words! Damn it!’

The overturned table signaled the end of today's long, drawn-out battle.

“I believe I have been rude. Please forgive me. It is late today, so I will call upon you again tomorrow to apologize.”

Along with Geom Mugeuk's apology, Hyeolcheon Doma and Ilhwa Geumjon said their farewells.

It was conduct unbecoming before the Demon Lords. The two were in a situation where they could have raged, but for Geom Mugeuk's sake, they showed no sign of it.

Jangho gathered the shackles, Seo Daeryong, and Ian, then followed them out.

Geom Mugeuk conveyed his gratitude to everyone with a smile. Had it not been for them, this gathering would never have come to pass in the first place.

They parted at the entrance. No one spoke of the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Jangho, Seo Daeryong, and Ian went to a tavern to have another round together. Hyeolcheon Doma and Ilhwa Geumjon returned to their lodgings, saying they were tired, and Geom Mugeuk likewise headed to his own quarters.

* * *

The shackled man stood up.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader found the sight of the shackled man walking toward him terribly unfamiliar.

The shackled man picked up one of the fallen wine bottles from the floor and drank straight from it. Then he extended the bottle to the Pungcheon Cult Leader. The Pungcheon Cult Leader took the bottle and drank.

Perhaps they could have sat face to face like this, drinking together, from the very beginning. What had he been so afraid of?

“I kept the shackles on because I feared you would kill me. No, to be more honest, I feared that I would kill you.”

“That is not true.”

“It is not?”

“The Cult Leader simply had no thoughts at all.”

“What?”

“The reasons that came to mind just now are thoughts I've only had recently. Before that, you lived without thinking at all, did you not? You simply kept me bound because you had bound me at first. It became a habit.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader could not refute him. He had indeed lived without thought, just as the man said. The realization that these shackles were such a grave matter had only come since becoming entangled with the Second Young Master recently.

Even so, the shackled man comforted the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“It is all right. Everyone lives this way.”

“What do you mean, all right? It is because of this that you are leaving.”

“I am not leaving because of that. Not because you kept me in shackles.”

“Then why? Do you think you can change the world alongside the Second Young Master?”

“I hold no such dream of changing the world.”

“Then why? Why in the world!”

The reason was something the Pungcheon Cult Leader had never anticipated.

“I am going because I could not predict anything.”

“What?”

“I handled everything related to the martial world situation on the frontier together with the Cult Leader.”

“You did.”

“And in that process, regarding matters related to the Second Young Master, I could not predict a single thing.”

“But you did predict things. You said he would come, and he came. You predicted he would demand the sacred artifact.”

The shackled man shook his head.

“Those were meaningless things. Many reports related to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult flew in from the frontier. A considerable number of them concerned the Second Young Master. Each time, the Second Young Master defied all my expectations. I never thought he would kill the Demon Lord, nor that he would kill the Soul-Devouring Demon Lord. I never imagined he would draw in Hyeolcheon Doma, nor that he would bring everyone under his command here today. I did not even know he would demand the shackles. How is this possible? Yes, that's right. Perhaps the Second Young Master is smarter than I, and if I go, I may be slighted. ‘What? I brought him a strategist, but he is more mediocre than he looks?’ I might be disappointed. But even so, I must go and see. I must see why I could not predict him, what kind of human being he is to walk such a path. I have become truly curious.”

It was the first time the shackled man had spoken so much.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader understood. He could no longer hold this man back. He asked one last time.

“Can you truly not stay with me?”

“I can. Let us go together.”

“You madman, where do you keep saying we should go together! What will my subordinates think? Would they follow me if I went under the command of some greenhorn Second Young Master!”

“Then let us throw everything away and go.”

“What?”

“Pass the position of Cult Leader to your successor and come with me. We shall live a new life.”

Every curse under the sun came to the Pungcheon Cult Leader's mind. But preceding his rage was a sense of defeat.

“Without the title of Pungcheon Cult Leader, where would I be of use?”

“Without that title, you will become quite an impressive person. You have lived an ordinary life bound to that title your whole life. I am not the only one who has lived in shackles.”

“What are you saying.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader threw the bottle he was holding, shattering it.

“Step down from the position of Cult Leader of my own accord? This position where I can kill whomever I wish with a single word, eat whatever I desire, and summon any beauty I please?”

“And does that make you happy?”

“Of course I am happy! No, let us say I am not entirely happy. So what? If I step down from this position, will I become happy? Are you saying happiness that eluded me even as Cult Leader will come walking to me on its own?”

“I do not know that either. You must find it.”

“You are trying to destroy me. You are raving to make me miserable.”

“Perhaps I am.”

“Damn it! I should just kill you and be done with it.”

“It is too late for that.”

“What?”

“If you wished to kill me, you should have done so before the Second Young Master came. If you kill me after the Second Young Master freed me from the shackles, how do you think he would feel toward you? He would blame himself for my death, and that would spread into hatred for the Cult Leader. If that happens, the Cult Leader will never be able to leave the frontier. For your own sake as well, you must not kill me.”

“Damn it!”

“Accept it as fate.”

“Do not speak to me of fate! Where is there a fate that discriminates between people in this manner?”

“Cult Leader, you are the Pungcheon Cult Leader. You who reigned over all, discriminating against every person on the frontier. How does the word ‘discrimination’ come from your lips?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been silent for a long while, spat out abruptly.

“I have lost. Yes, I have lost to you silver-tongued, clever lot. Go.”

He spoke from the heart.

It was the moment he gave up on the shackled man.

“Go and suffer till you die. Regret the day you left me until your dying breath. Go, go. You sicken me, so I am sending you away.”

Finally, the Pungcheon Cult Leader truly released the shackles.

At that moment, the shackled man placed something before the Pungcheon Cult Leader. Surprisingly, it was a key shaped exactly like the one that could unlock the shackles.

“What is this? Could it be? The key to the shackles?”

The shackled man nodded. The Pungcheon Cult Leader was aghast. Earlier, the key that had been hanging from the necklace had been taken away along with the shackles by Geom Mugeuk's subordinate, yet here was another identical key.

“I forged it years ago.”

“How in the world? No, then why did you not run away?”

The shackled man did not answer him regarding that.

“If you could unlock the shackles, you must have had opportunities to kill me. Why did you not?”

“The reason is likely similar to why you did not kill me despite my curses. Did not the Cult Leader say so? That I am the person who understands and knows you best. I feel the same.”

The moment he heard those words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader's heart surged and his eyes grew hot.

This was why. This was why the parting was so difficult and regretful.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked down at the key and spoke.

“To produce this at this moment. I am a loser until the very end.”

At that, the shackled man performed a deep bow right there. And with the most respectful expression and words he had ever shown, he spoke.

“That is not so. That you did not kill me, and that you let me go so willingly. To me, at least, the Cult Leader was an admirable person. Though I could have fled, though I wished to die many times, the fact that I lived on like this was perhaps because I had grown attached to the Cult Leader. I shall never forget the grace of freeing me today. I shall look forward to the day we meet again, Cult Leader.”

The shackled man left the room like so.

Left alone, the Pungcheon Cult Leader pounded the floor with his fists.

He felt relieved, yet regretful; empty, yet glad. And he was sad.

Chapter 80: Even If I Become Predictable Someday.
-------------------------------------------------
The hastily formed trio headed for Pungryu Tavern.

Ian and Seo Daeryong had been fairly drunk, but the instant the Pungcheon Cult Leader overturned the drinking table, they sobered up as if it had all been a lie.

How many times in one’s life would one ever see the Pungcheon Cult Leader overturn a drinking table in front of the Demon Venerables? They had gained a story so absurd it would begin, with grandchildren seated on their knees, “This old grandfather of yours, back in his younger days…”

All three of them felt the same: they could not simply go home on a day like this.

Before leaving for Maga Village, Jang Ho entrusted the Ten-Thousand-Year Cold Iron to Hall Master Gwak, the man in charge of the cult’s iron workshop and known as the Divine Hand.

Hall Master Gwak was delighted, saying it had been a long time since he could properly stretch his hands, and because he was a man who almost never showed emotion, the three of them once again realized just how extraordinary the gift they were receiving truly was.

The three entered Pungryu Tavern in Maga Village.

“Only the three of you came today.”

Jo Chunbae greeted them warmly.

“As of today, the trio has been formed.”

At Seo Daeryong’s joke, Ian added,

“It may become a quartet before long.”

Ian smiled at Jo Chunbae, who clearly had no idea what they meant.

“Please bring us drinks first.”

The three sat in their usual seats on the second floor.

The drinking party began with a remark from Jang Ho that was half joke, half truth.

“I sometimes feel a little hurt.”

“Why?”

“You two are the heart and the right arm, but I’m still nothing, am I?”

He said it like a joke, but there was hurt mixed in.

Then Ian smiled and said,

“Isn’t that what makes it even more impressive?”

“What do you mean?”

“As you said, you’re neither the heart nor the right arm, but the Young Master always calls for you, doesn’t he, my lord?”

Jang Ho opened his eyes wide, as if he had never thought of it from that perspective.

“All that talk of right arms and hearts may be in jest, but the Young Master’s trust in the Demon Army Lord is real. I can tell just by looking at his eyes.”

“Is that so?”

“Yes!”

“Our warrior Ian truly knows how to make a person feel good.”

This time, Ian asked, “Do I?” and Jang Ho answered, loudly like Ian had, “Yes.” The three of them laughed together.

Then, all at once, Ian thought of the shackled man. He was someone who could not be left out of tonight’s drinking.

“Who do you think that man was earlier?”

“He seemed like no ordinary person.”

At Seo Daeryong’s answer, Jang Ho emphasized once more,

“No matter how important or exceptional he may be, I will not allow him to act as he pleases.”

He made it clear that he intended to keep the shackled man in check.

“Thank you for stepping forward for me earlier.”

Seo Daeryong expressed his gratitude. When the shackled man had pressured him by asking what he would do if someone tried to take the position of right arm, Jang Ho had stepped in.

“I’m sorry to say this, but I did not step forward for Investigator Seo’s sake. I was sincere. About standing in line as a candidate for the right arm.”

Ian and Seo Daeryong stared at him in surprise.

“Lord Jang…”

Seo Daeryong was about to say something when Jang Ho cut him off.

“Please do not say you can yield the position. I do not think the position of the Second Young Master’s right arm is something to be yielded so easily.”

“No, I was going to say that even if it is you, my lord, I will not yield it.”

“What? Then why did you say earlier that you would yield?”

“It was a lie. The mood was flowing in a way that made it seem like it wasn’t my place to step forward. To be honest, I can’t yield it. I absolutely won’t.”

Seo Daeryong revealed his true feelings. It was not merely because of the alcohol. There was something he had wanted to say at least once to Ian and Jang Ho.

“My senior at Hwangcheon Pavilion was killed by the previous Demon Army Lord, and I was consumed only by thoughts of revenge. I memorized all the personal details of the Demon Army members. But that was all. I couldn’t step forward and say we should reinvestigate it. I simply burned with vengeance in my heart.”

Seo Daeryong drank. Without a word, Jang Ho filled his cup.

“Pathetic, isn’t it? But I had to do even that just to endure. If the Pavilion Master had not come as a temporary investigator back then, I would probably have spent my whole life blaming myself, living darkly and gloomily. Do you know what I hear most often from people these days? They ask why I’ve become so bright. So do you think I would easily give up the position of that man’s right arm? Even if it is you, my lord, I will not yield. And my martial arts are improving day by day.”

Having poured out his words as if releasing pent-up resentment, Seo Daeryong let out a long breath.

Jang Ho, who seemed as though he might say something, simply smiled in good spirits. Seo Daeryong found that smile this rough man occasionally gave—this broad grin—both terribly unfamiliar and strangely magnificent. Every time he saw it, he thought he wanted to be able to smile with that same feeling.

Seo Daeryong said to the two of them,

“Let’s keep having this gathering from now on. Please!”

That day, the three of them talked until late.

They talked about Geom Mugeuk, and they talked about the shackled man. They talked about the Demon Venerables, and they also spoke of their own work.

It was a pleasant drinking party where no one crossed the line, yet everyone said what needed to be said; where one somehow felt a vague belief that anything could be spoken in this place. Because Geom Mugeuk was not there, they could reveal their true feelings more freely.

“I’m really glad we gathered today.”

When Ian raised her cup high, the two men clinked their cups vigorously against it.

After the drinking party ended and the three came outside, they stood side by side in front of the tavern.

Looking toward the main compound buildings visible in the distance, Seo Daeryong said,

“Tonight, many people’s fates must be changing. Or perhaps they have already changed.”

Ian nodded and answered him.

“All we have to do is believe in the fate that leads those fates.”

The Second Young Master, to the very end? People sincere to the very end were here as well.

* * *

Around the time dawn began to brighten, the shackled man came to see me.

I had always seen him shackled beside the Eumroe Bell, so seeing him in my residence felt entirely new.

Before even greeting him, I examined his ankles first.

“Are your ankles all right?”

“Second Young Master, you defy my expectations to the very end.”

His voice was low and calm.

I looked up at him with an expression asking what he meant.

“I mean I did not expect you to look at my ankles as your first greeting.”

“They are more important than greetings.”

“My ankles are fine. Though I may not possess extraordinary martial arts, I am still a martial artist.”

“Can you tell me what martial art you practice? I would like to know exactly what level your skill is at.”

“At one time, I trained in Mingren Fist Technique up to the seventh level.”

“Ah, that is a fine fist technique.”

“You know Mingren Fist Technique?”

“Is it not the exclusive martial art of Fist Master Yang Cheng, one of the Nineteen Powers Beyond the Frontier?”

“How do you even know that?”

“I have an interest in all sorts of miscellaneous things.”

Fortunately, the man’s ankles were all right. As I straightened and rose, the man said,

“My name is Gu Yue.”

It was the moment I finally learned the shackled man’s name.

Gu Yue. The meaning—a desolate, lonely moon hanging in the sky—suited his atmosphere well.

“Gu Yue. A good name.”

“Thank you.”

Gu Yue’s gaze and mine intertwined in the air. Through the long hair hanging down, I could see the clarity of his eyes. As it had begun when I first met those eyes, no other words were needed between him and me.

“From now on, become my strategist.”

At my proposal, which could have seemed sudden, Gu Yue calmly asked in return,

“If you become the Heavenly Demon later, Second Young Master, I will become the chief strategist of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Can you entrust such a heavy responsibility to me?”

“You will take it on when you have played a great role in helping me become the Heavenly Demon. It will not be something I entrust to you. You will have to rise to that position yourself.”

After pointing that out precisely, I added a joke.

“Even so, becoming the chief strategist of the Demon Cult will probably be easier than becoming my right arm.”

Gu Yue gave a faint smile.

“It seemed that way earlier.”

Just as I had made my proposal without hesitation, Gu Yue did not hesitate either. He knelt and paid formal respects.

“I am lacking, but if you accept me as your strategist, I will serve you with all my loyalty. I swear it to heaven and earth, upon my life and honor.”

I, too, formally accepted him.

“Martial artist Gu Yue, from this moment on, I appoint you as my chief strategist. Please guide me well.”

More than words, I believed in the fervor contained in his clear eyes. I believed in the fate of mine that had brought him before me.

“From now on, please speak comfortably to me.”

“I will.”

This moment of welcoming Gu Yue was special. Because it was the moment I was freed from the burden of having to bear everything alone.

I took his hand and raised him to his feet.

“First, let us restore your health.”

“I am fine.”

“There is no need to hurry. I will need you ten years from now, twenty years from now, and thirty years from now as well. During that time, we may have to wage war against the Martial Alliance, or we may have to appease the Sado Alliance. A rebellion may break out, or a powerful enemy may strike us. I did not seek someone to advise me only today. I sought someone who will go through all those things with me in the future.”

For an instant, a strange light passed through Gu Yue’s eyes.

“To think you have thought that far. Second Young Master, you truly are an unpredictable person.”

“The me of now may have unpredictable aspects, but the me of later may become a man far too obvious. Yet even then, the lives of my subordinates will not be any less precious. You must help me now, and you must help me then as well. We have a very long road ahead of us.”

“I will bear that in mind.”

This time, Gu Yue asked me,

“May I ask you one question?”

“Ask as much as you like.”

“If you discover a strategist more outstanding than I in the future, what will you do?”

“Become his deputy strategist and help me. How is it? Does my answer pass?”

“It passes.”

“Would you not be disappointed? If I brought someone else in and said, from today on, this person is my strategist?”

“The reason I came to you, Second Young Master, was not to build a friendship. It means you do not need to become close to me. So if such a situation comes, I must accept it without question. If I cannot even accept such an obvious choice, then I, as your strategist, would either be an idiot or a villain. You must be resolute. For your sake, and for mine as well.”

Gu Yue’s insight was certainly extraordinary. He knew that if one became bound by affection, one would ultimately ruin the relationship in an even greater way.

“In truth, Second Young Master, you are such an intelligent person that I wonder whether you truly need me.”

“A person cannot always be intelligent, can they? I will make mistakes, and so will you. Even so, the possibility of both of us making mistakes at the same time should be small, should it not? So let us discuss things together as we go.”

“Just what kind of life have you lived, Second Young Master?”

How could I explain that life to him? Instead of my life, I relayed to him the words Sima Ming had once said to me.

“A strategist I respect once said this. A good strategist is one who is smarter than the other side’s strategist.”

“Is my opponent the strategist of the Martial Alliance? Or is it that strategist you respect?”

“Both.”

Gu Yue would surely guess it. That the strategist I respected was Sima Ming, the chief strategist of our cult. That I meant for him to become a strategist who surpassed him.

“There is something you must do from now on. Create an intelligence organization and take control of all information in the Central Plains. Our goal is an organization superior to Tongcheon Pavilion.”

“Yes!”

“Is that something you can answer so easily?”

Gu Yue asked back with a confident gaze.

“Is that not why you went through so much trouble to bring me here?”

* * *

A few days passed.

For several days, Gu Yue focused on recovering his body. I did not arrange separate quarters for him. For the time being, he was to stay in the guest room of my residence.

As he had been from the first day, he was awake early today as well.

“You’re up?”

“Did you sleep well through the night?”

“I slept well.”

“What brings you here so early in the morning?”

“Here, take this money first.”

I handed him the bundle of banknotes I had retrieved from the bank.

“You will need money to build an organization, so use this.”

Even after confirming that it was a vast sum of one million nyang, he did not look surprised.

“What will you do if I take this money and run?”

“I will send people to catch you and bring you back.”

“And then?”

“I will melt down the fan and dagger being made right now, make shackles first, and put them on you. Then I will show you to the person I hate most. You must act well then too, with that mysterious air.”

Perhaps this joke alone was difficult to endure, for he let out a small laugh.

“Let’s go. Today, we’ll eat outside.”

We came out to Maga Village like that, and I saw the Pungcheon Cult Leader sitting on the second floor of Pungryu Tavern.

“The owner of this place sent word that the Pungcheon Cult Leader has already come here alone twice. It seems he wants to see you. What do you think? Will you meet him?”

If not, there was no way the Pungcheon Cult Leader would have come to this place twice to eat.

“Yes. I wanted to meet him as well.”

My own issue had been resolved, but these people still had a matter left unresolved. Yes, even if one must fight with a pack on one’s back, one should at least fight without a burden on one’s heart.

We slowly made our way to the second floor.

Chapter 81: Let's Live Another Life.
------------------------------------
The Pungcheon Cult Leader was not surprised to see me and Go Wol.

“May we join you?”

He had been waiting for us, yet he needlessly threw out a barb.

“Are you here to boast that you’ve taken in a new man?”

“Isn’t that why you’re eating here, so I can boast?”

Go Wol and I sat down across from him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader glanced at Go Wol. Perhaps he could not get used to the sight of Go Wol, who had put on some weight and recovered his condition after resting these past few days, for he kept looking at him.

“Your new master must be treating you well.”

“I eat well and sleep well.”

“You don’t know he might be fattening you up to eat you on a feast day, do you?”

“If so, I hope it’s a grand feast.”

I believed time would resolve the relationship between these two as well.

Someday, the Pungcheon Cult Leader would come to think this: Why was I so obsessed back then? Looking back now, it was nothing.

Or would this become a wound he could never forget, a lasting grudge?

Though I had lived a lifetime, when it came to things I had not personally experienced, when it came to the hearts of those people, I still could not be certain.

In any case, before he grew gloomy, I changed the subject.

“Did you meet my father?”

“I did.”

“Did you tell him? That you would raise the new Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable to be stronger.”

“I did. As you said, he was pleased.”

“Did he say anything else?”

“He did. He asked how long Cheongseon’s martial training would continue. He seemed to want me to return quickly.”

“So what did you answer?”

“I told him I still had to find the sacred relics, and that there was much left to teach.”

“Well done.”

“What should we do now?”

“I will look for a way.”

“You won’t demand another sacred relic as the price, will you?”

“I’ll discuss that part with my strategist.”

In that instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression hardened.

“Is that something you should be saying to me right now?”

I spoke calmly to him.

“Cult Leader.”

“I’m listening.”

“You came to our cult and lost one person, but didn’t you gain another? If you live a life of constantly looking back like this, you will lose that person too.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression hardened even more, but he could not get angry. Cheongseon, who had become the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, was not someone he could afford to treat carelessly.

“Miss Cheong, whom you accepted as your disciple, is a person of great ambition. Focus on her.”

Perhaps irritated by my advice, the Pungcheon Cult Leader took it out on Go Wol.

“If, as you say, Second Young Master here is an unparalleled hero, then countless people will gather around him in the future. There will be many who are smarter than you too. You’ll be reduced to a nobody among them. What was he again? Ah, that shackle from back then! That’s exactly the sort of person you’ll become.”

I felt it. Those words were not meant for Go Wol, but for me. They were the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s warning not to treat Go Wol that way. This man truly cared for Go Wol.

Then Go Wol suddenly opened his mouth.

“Second Young Master gave me one million nyang.”

I had never imagined he would boast to the Pungcheon Cult Leader about the money he had received in a situation like this, so inwardly I was surprised.

“You said Second Young Master was putting on an act, didn’t you? If one is going to put on an act, one should do it on this grand a scale.”

Even I was surprised, so the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s surprise was only natural. Of course, that surprise led to anger.

“Save your advice for your impressive new master!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader sprang up from his seat and left.

Because I knew Go Wol was not such a rash person, I asked him calmly.

“Why did you do that?”

As expected, there had been intent behind his action.

“As your strategist, I would like to offer my first proposal.”

“What is it?”

Go Wol gazed at me and said,

“I would like to recruit the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

The words were so unexpected that I was startled.

“Are you saying you want to bring the Pungcheon Cult Leader over to our side?”

At my question, Go Wol nodded.

“Yes.”

“Is that possible?”

“I cannot be certain, but there is a possibility.”

“Then why did you anger him?”

“I have a plan.”

It seemed to be groundwork to draw the Pungcheon Cult Leader in.

“May I ask why you want to bring him over?”

After a brief pause, Go Wol spoke. The reason was one I had not expected.

“Because I want to save the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

“If he does not join hands with me, will he die?”

“Yes. That is what I expect will happen.”

“Why do you think so?”

“Because of the Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“Because of my father? Why?”

Since he was mentioning the Heavenly Demon, Go Wol lowered his voice.

“The difference in the breadth of the two cult leaders’ vessels is too great. Your father is a man counted among the greatest martial talents of all the Heavenly Demons throughout history. He is also a man of great ambition. In the end, the Pungcheon Cult will be devoured by the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

I agreed with Go Wol’s opinion. Now that the Pungcheon Cult no longer even had Go Wol, if they failed to bring in a proper new strategist, there was no telling what disaster might befall them.

“So you want to save the Pungcheon Cult Leader?”

“Yes, that is correct. If he becomes your man, he should be able to avoid that calamity.”

Gain the Pungcheon Cult Leader? Various problems would arise as a result, but it seemed like a choice with as many gains as losses. Above all else—

“It’s my strategist’s first proposal. I ought to accept it. Good. Let’s try bringing the Pungcheon Cult Leader over to our side.”

“Thank you.”

Go Wol bowed his head respectfully.

I intended to treat him properly as my strategist. Just as Father treated Sama Myeong.

“Are you not worried that I might ruin it?”

“Ruin what?”

“You know that the Pungcheon Cult Leader and I are deeply entangled emotionally, don’t you? If I mishandle him even slightly, the Pungcheon Cult could become our enemy.”

“Well, that could happen.”

“Then why did you permit it?”

“Will things get better if you simply turn away from each other? I think hatred and resentment grow even stronger when you don’t see each other. It would be better to struggle and clash with the Pungcheon Cult Leader and settle things. Whether we make him our ally or our enemy, let’s reach some kind of conclusion.”

Just like the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. At my words, Go Wol’s expression brightened.

I held out my hand to him.

“Let us shake hands once.”

Go Wol took my hand and said,

“This hand is firmer than shackles.”

“Do you mean it is a hand that can break ten-thousand-year cold iron? Or do you mean it is like a shackle that will hold you here?”

“Which is it?”

“To me, they are the same.”

“I like both.”

We clasped hands firmly.

At last, I had gained a strategist.

* * *

The next day, I went to see the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“This is good tea from the Central Plains.”

Instead of thanking me, the Pungcheon Cult Leader snorted first.

“Did you come to boast that you gave your strategist one million nyang? Must be nice to have so much money.”

“I came because I simply wanted to see you. And don’t you have a hundred times more money than I do, Cult Leader?”

“A hundred times? A thousand times, more like.”

“Of course. I’m the sort of man who stretches his legs only after looking at the place he’ll lie down. How could I possibly boast of money in front of you, Cult Leader?”

“Then why did you come?”

“I thought we might share a cup of tea and have a chat.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader first sent me a suspicious look. The sense of grievance he had built up because of me was piled even higher than the sacred relics behind him, so it was only natural.

“To tell the truth, I have a concern regarding Go Wol.”

“What concern?”

“You saw it yesterday, didn’t you? How frivolously he boasted about money in front of you.”

“Are you saying you were disappointed?”

I said nothing, but let a slight hint of disappointment show. Of course, this was something Go Wol had instructed me to do. He had boasted about the money yesterday in order to create this situation.

“I do not know Go Wol well.”

“Then why did you show him such devoted sincerity?”

“So I thought about it carefully…”

“And?”

“Perhaps he was merely a stepping stone to form a connection between you and me, Cult Leader.”

“Does that man know? That you’re stabbing him in the back like this?”

“This is neither slander nor betrayal. It is the truth as it is.”

“So what is it you want to say?”

“I am curious whether Go Wol is someone I can trust.”

Displeasure flashed across the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face.

“Listen, Second Young Master.”

“Yes.”

“While I am still speaking nicely, leave for today.”

At his cold order to leave, I merely gave my respects and rose.

“Then I will see you next time.”

Being driven out like this, too, had been within Go Wol’s expectations.

* * *

The next day, Go Wol went to see the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“You bastards! What trick are you trying to pull now?”

Still, he was more pleased to see him than when Geom Mugeuk had come.

“I have come as a guest, so please show proper courtesy.”

“Bullshit.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader turned and sat facing aside.

Go Wol went to the table nearby and brewed the tea with his own hands. Since the Pungcheon Cult Leader did not usually allow maidservants into his residence and the two of them had often been alone, it had mostly been the Pungcheon Cult Leader who brewed the tea.

“Planning to put poison in it?”

“What would I gain by poisoning you to death, Cult Leader?”

“You could live happily with Second Young Master in a world without me, couldn’t you?”

“You need to be alive, Cult Leader, for me to boast to you. Like I did yesterday about the money.”

“You mad bastard.”

Go Wol brought over the tea.

“Please drink. Come to think of it, this is the first tea I have brewed for you with my own hands.”

Though his expression was displeased, the Pungcheon Cult Leader blew on the tea and drank.

“How is it?”

“It tastes awful.”

“I will leave after just one cup.”

“Do as you like.”

The two drank tea in silence. Then, abruptly, the Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke.

“Second Young Master does not fully trust you.”

Originally, he had never intended to tell him. Let Geom Mugeuk taste some bitterness—such had been his honest feeling. But once he faced Go Wol, he could not do it.

“Are you trying to drive a wedge between us?”

After going out of his way to tell him, and this was what he got? The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been about to flare up, snorted.

“Think what you like.”

Only then did Go Wol smile and say,

“I am moved. To think you would even tell me such a thing.”

“You said I was driving a wedge.”

“I know you were not.”

“How?”

“It is part of the plan.”

At the word plan, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled.

“Plan? What plan?”

“A plan to draw you in, Cult Leader. Second Young Master suspects me and drives me into a corner, then uses your soft heart to bring you over to our side.”

“What?”

After sitting blankly for a moment, the Pungcheon Cult Leader raised his voice.

“You think I would fall for such a worthless plan?”

“If I am driven into a corner, will you simply stand by and watch?”

“Of course! You’re already gone. What does that have to do with me?”

“Then why are you so worked up right now?”

“I am not worked up. I said I’m not!”

But the Pungcheon Cult Leader was clearly worked up.

“Then why are you telling me?”

“Because I do not want to deceive you into coming with us.”

“!”

While the Pungcheon Cult Leader hesitated, unsure what to say, Go Wol spoke again.

“Even telling you this is part of the plan. A plan to make you trust me even more.”

“What in the world are you talking about? You ghost of a bastard, even after leaving you torment me! Shoo! Get out!”

“Now you seem like yourself, Cult Leader.”

“You think shouting is like me? Are you mocking me? Is that it? I’ll shout to your heart’s content, so mock me all you want!”

After shouting for a while, the Pungcheon Cult Leader felt his insides clear.

“Why go this far to take me with you?”

“Because if things remain as they are, I believe you will be defeated by the Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

From beginning to end, Go Wol dealt with the Pungcheon Cult Leader honestly.

“I won’t be defeated! As soon as I return, I’ll find a new strategist.”

“You know that I can roughly guess what kind of person within the Pungcheon Cult will sit in my former seat, don’t you? They will not be enough.”

“Then I’ll find one. Just as I found you.”

“Will such fortune come twice?”

“Damn it! Curses! You insolent bastard!”

“I have now shown all my cards. Now it is your turn to show yours, Cult Leader.”

“…When you know all my cards anyway.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a long sigh.

“Second Young Master will strip me of everything.”

“Then each time, obtain everything you want from him in return. If you lose one thing, gain one thing and fill the gap.”

“My head can’t do that, can it?”

“I will help you.”

“Go and help yourself. Don’t end up penniless over there, bound in shackles. People act kind, act impressive, but in the end they are all the same.”

“That is why. Since they are all the same, let us join them.”

“Can I really trust you?”

“Are you trembling?”

“Wouldn’t you tremble if you were me?”

At that moment, Go Wol’s expression changed, and he suddenly altered his tone.

“Cult Leader.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at him with a face both pleased and surprised.

“Don’t lie. In all the life you’ve lived until now, you have never once trembled. Not even once, right? Because nothing ever threatened you. You say the mere thought of advancing into the Central Plains makes you tremble? Don’t lie. You chanted ‘advance into the Central Plains’ over and over, but you never once thought about it seriously, did you? What it meant, what consequences it would bring. To be honest, it was a nuisance to you, wasn’t it?”

“!”

He could not deny it. Because it had been his grandfather’s and his father’s cherished wish, it had become his own cherished wish as well. He had wanted to advance into the Central Plains only because it felt as if refusing would make him a sinner of the Pungcheon Cult.

“A life where you can kill a man with a single word, embrace every beauty, and eat delicacies of land and sea every day is a good one. It’s the life everyone dreams of. But, Cult Leader, you have lived that life to your heart’s content, haven’t you? There is no longer any thrill or happiness in that life, is there? Cult Leader, let us live a different life for once. Let us live a life that makes us tense, exhilarated, and makes our guts drop. Your martial arts are rusting away in the sandstorms; you should show the world what a true storm of blood is. A life where, if fortune is bad, you might croak and die. Fortunately, at least we have someone who will bring a single flower to each other’s grave, don’t we?”

Their gazes intertwined in the air. At last, the Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke softly.

“Call the Second Young Master.”

Chapter 82: Those Shackles from Back Then, That Cult Leader from Back Then.
---------------------------------------------------------------------------
When I arrived at the Pungcheon Cult Leader's residence, his first question was this.

"If I join hands with you, what would change from now?"

In his blunt question, I sensed sincerity rather than haste. He was serious. I could tell that Gowol had nearly convinced him. So while I would have liked to give him words full of conviction, I told him the truth.

"Nothing will change."

"Nothing will change?"

"Of course. What could possibly change? I haven't even received my father's permission—could I open a Pungcheon Cult branch? Would we publicly announce that we've joined hands? It's simply a promise between us."

"Only that?"

The Pungcheon Cult Leader made a bewildered expression. He looked at Gowol. With eyes that said, *What is this? You dragged me all the way here for this?* But Gowol quietly waited for me to continue.

"However, there is this much. If I truly come to think of the Cult Leader as one of my own, the moment you fall into danger, I will cast everything aside and rush to save you."

"I've never been in a crisis. Falling into crisis in one's life—that doesn't happen often, does it?"

"Then you've lived quite a smooth life."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader glanced at Gowol. Much of that smooth life had likely been possible because Gowol was there.

"On the other hand, if you join hands with me, you *will* fall into crisis. *What? The Second Young Master and the Pungcheon Cult Leader have joined hands? Eliminate the Pungcheon Cult Leader first!* There's no guarantee that enemies like that won't appear."

"Then why in my right mind would I join hands with you?"

"Joining hands with the Cult Leader was not my desire. To be honest, I find the Cult Leader burdensome."

"Don't confuse me with difficult words. Speak simply."

"You know that my father does not think well of the Cult Leader or the Pungcheon Cult, yes?"

"Of course."

"Even in such circumstances, I have associated with the Cult Leader. And now this person, Gowol, has become one of my own. Do you think my father truly doesn't know that?"

"He must know."

"It means I chose this relationship even while going against my father's will. Of course, I also coveted the divine artifact, but…"

I extended my left hand before him. A supreme-grade heavenly silkworm silk was wrapped around my wrist.

"No matter how rare this scrap of fabric is, it is not worth risking my father's displeasure."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader could not refute this. He, too, surely knew well that once one became the Heavenly Demon, one would possess everything they desired.

"Then why all this fuss? Why take turns coming to find me and shaking my heart?"

"Did I not tell you? It is not my idea. It is Military Advisor Go's idea."

Gowol was lost in thought, his eyes half-closed.

"The idea of bringing the Cult Leader to a place beside my own people was entirely Military Advisor Go's idea. So I will ask Military Advisor Go."

I asked Gowol seriously.

"Why must I take the Cult Leader into my fold, even at the cost of conflict with my father?"

The Pungcheon Cult Leader waited for his answer with an even more curious expression than mine.

Eventually, Gowol spoke calmly.

"I am ashamed to say this decision was not made for the Young Master. It was a decision made for my former master."

Gowol gazed at the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

"I wish to gift the Cult Leader a new life. I wish to share a new life with you. Leave the position of Cult Leader to your successor and come."

"You want me to abandon the position of Cult Leader?"

"Yes. I do not want the Pungcheon Cult. I want the Cult Leader himself."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader's pupils shook.

In truth, he was someone ill-suited to leading a group. His selfishness and greed were not the vicious kind that deceived everyone. The ruler of the frontier, content with the status quo, now stood upon a tightrope—a precarious tightrope beneath which countless spears and sword blades surged like bamboo poles.

Yet the man himself did not know how dangerous his situation was.

Would he truly abandon the position of Pungcheon Cult Leader and come to me because of his relationship with one person? Could such a relationship truly exist in this world?

"If I am gone, the main cult will…"

"It will run even better. Please come without worry."

"Let me think! Just a bit! Please!"

At that, I told one of the Pungcheon Cult Leader's subordinates outside to go to Hwacheon Pavilion and bring Seo Daeryong. At my actions, both the Pungcheon Cult Leader and Gowol showed puzzled expressions.

Shortly after, Seo Daeryong arrived like the wind.

"Did you call for me?"

Having rushed to the Pungcheon Cult Leader's residence due to the sudden summons, he was extremely tense. Omitting all playful greetings, I asked him seriously.

"Where did you make the recent decision that changed your life?"

Given the atmosphere, Seo Daeryong also answered honestly and seriously.

"At a tavern."

"How long did it take to make the decision?"

"It couldn't have taken even a quarter of an hour."

"Do you regret that decision?"

"No."

"If you had not made that decision there that day?"

"I would never have been able to do it."

"Tell me about that time in detail."

Seo Daeryong was someone who normally hated being in the spotlight. But once he stepped forward, he was a smart man who knew better than anyone what words to say.

"In my experience, life-changing decisions are made in an instant. When I left home, and when I entered Hwacheon Pavilion. I didn't agonize over them for long. I spend days worrying over buying a set of clothes, but I changed my life in just a quarter of an hour. If I delay? I cannot decide. I wake up as a different person the next day."

I looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

"What do you think?"

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said to me with an incredulous expression.

"You planned this, didn't you? You rehearsed all the lines and came here to do this. Practiced all night yesterday, right?"

Then Seo Daeryong cautiously added one last thing.

"If I may dare speak to the Cult Leader, after meeting the Second Young Master, I have had similar experiences countless times. Situations where it feels like everything is staged to make a fool of me. I wonder what this is, what that is, and before I know it the situation is already over. Going this way and that, running around senselessly doing as told, and again the situation is over. It is the same now. Answering like this before the Pungcheon Cult Leader? Why? Even now it feels like everything is staged to make a fool of me."

Seo Daeryong looked at me and asked as if arguing.

"Right? You're doing this to tease me, aren't you? I may seem like this, but I am the top-ranked inductee of Hwacheon Pavilion. I can see through everything at a glance!"

I smiled broadly at him.

"As expected of my right-hand man! I like everything from beginning to end. A right-hand man is someone you don't need to script. That's why he is the right-hand man."

Seo Daeryong laughed along with me.

"The number of people eyeing the right-hand man position has grown sharply these days, so I am relieved to have scored some points. Since I have confirmed that the right-hand man is still securely attached for now, I will take my leave."

"Wait a moment. This may be a historic moment."

"It is not a place where a blood battle is breaking out, is it? I have yet to escape the basics of martial arts training."

"A blood battle may be breaking out in the Cult Leader's heart."

Everyone's gaze focused on the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

"What? You really want me to decide now? Decide to give up the position of Pungcheon Cult Leader in just a quarter of an hour? And it is not just any position—it is the Pungcheon Cult Leader's position?"

At that moment, Seo Daeryong jumped in surprise and let out a gasp. He had explained excitedly, never dreaming that this was the decision the Pungcheon Cult Leader was being asked to make.

"Good heavens! I would have agonized for a hundred years. Please do not decide now!"

His honest words instead became a stimulus to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

"Do you truly not regret it?"

"You will regret it, Cult Leader. Go back and worry for a year! At least a hundred days!"

"Impertinent wretch! Answer only what you are asked."

"Yes, I do not regret it. Though my lifespan may have shortened after meeting my lord, I am extremely happy right now."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader rose from his seat. He slowly walked and looked at the divine artifacts displayed in the room.

And then, for some reason, he began to strike the yin-thunder bell.

*Bong!*

A deep sound spread in all directions.

As the sound faded, he struck the bell again. And again, and again.

Listening to the bell, he was deep in thought. Was the evil ghost engraved upon the yin-thunder bell offering him some advice?

How many times did the bell ring? Suddenly the Pungcheon Cult Leader turned to me and asked.

"If I go, will you give me the right-hand man position?"

Seo Daeryong was so surprised he covered his mouth with his hand. Not because he coveted the right-hand man position, but because the Pungcheon Cult Leader was truly willing to abandon the position of Cult Leader.

I spoke provocatively to the man who was prepared to give up even the position of Pungcheon Cult Leader.

"That will not do. My right-hand man is Examiner Seo here. Please get in line behind Commander Jang and fight to win it."

Seo Daeryong shook his head pathetically, his eyes wide.

* * *

Only the Pungcheon Cult Leader and Gowol remained in the residence after Geom Mugyeok and Seo Daeryong left.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader and Gowol sat side by side before the yin-thunder bell.

"What does the evil ghost say?"

"It calls me a madman."

"I think so too. Are you really coming?"

"Aren't you the madman? You persuaded me all this time, and now you say that?"

"I didn't think you would really come. I didn't think you would say such firm and cool words to the Second Young Master about going together."

"I might change my mind tomorrow. Or the day after. Or next year. I am a spineless man by nature, am I not? Since it has come to this, let me ask one thing."

"Yes."

"Why do you want to be with me? Is it simply out of affection?"

Gowol stared at the yin-thunder bell for a long while before suddenly speaking.

"I suppose I was afraid to go alone."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled by words he had never imagined.

Gowol still kept his gaze on the yin-thunder bell.

"I suppose I am afraid of doing foolish things if I go alone. I am afraid of being looked down upon by his people when I go to the Second Young Master. But if the Cult Leader is with me, I think I would find strength. Was I not always the smartest man alive in front of the Cult Leader?"

Gowol slowly looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

"I am the one who is narrow-minded and selfish. I am the one making you lay down the position of Cult Leader for my sake."

It was the first time Gowol had revealed his true feelings since they met. Because of that, the Pungcheon Cult Leader did not get angry. Rather, a smile formed at his lips.

"This is better than any other reason."

"Why is it good?"

"Because it means I am going to protect you, does it not?"

This time, Gowol let out a chuckle.

"You may think on it more. The Second Young Master is a fate that brings storms; we do not know how our lives will be swept away. You might truly end up as that former Cult Leader, that former title, shoved into a corner. You might end up utterly ruined, selling medicine by the roadside."

"As long as we survive. And if we sell that medicine together? Then it is fine."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader also laid bare his true feelings.

"Do you think I am not selfish? Would I go just because someone tells me to? I have turned things over in my mind as much as needed, finished all my calculations, and that is why I am going. I will establish a new Cult Leader and come saying I will work on advancing the main cult into the Central Plains. I will leave not as someone simply retiring, but in the guise of sacrificing myself for the cult's great cause. So that if I change my mind, I may return. How long will it take to properly establish the Pungcheon Cult in the Central Plains? Quite a long time, will it not? While the next Pungcheon Cult Leader suffers in the frontier with sandstorms, I will be enjoying the fun in the Central Plains. The kind of fun that is nerve-wracking, thrilling, and makes your liver drop."

"You thought all that in such a short time? The Cult Leader is truly clever."

"You may be honest. You can say I am quite selfish as well."

"Cult Leader."

"Yes?"

"Let us do well together."

"I am nervous."

"What is the Ruler of the Frontier nervous about?"

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader made a joke he had never thought he'd make.

"That I will not be able to become the right-hand man."

"What?"

"You know how obsessive I am once I set my sights on something. I will definitely take that spot."

Judging by his eyes, it did not seem to be entirely a joke.

The two looked at each other and laughed wholeheartedly.

"You said it. That if I put down the position of Cult Leader, I would be quite a cool person. Let us see if your words hold true. Ah, I suppose I will not have cause to see this yin-thunder bell anymore."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been fondly stroking the bell, suddenly shouted.

"Shut up, you evil ghost bastard!"

The evil ghost must have said something like this: *A breathtaking showdown against a mere Hwacheon Pavilion examiner over the right-hand man position? I truly look forward to it.*

Chapter 83: Like Two Meteors Meeting.
-------------------------------------
Two days later, the Pungcheon Cult Leader departed from the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, leading all the subordinates he had brought with him.

He had exchanged farewells with the Heavenly Demon, but he did not so much as greet the Eight Demon Venerables.

The cult members murmured among themselves that he had left in anger over the theft of the sacred object.

We were the only ones who knew the truth behind his sudden return.

“The Cult Leader is an impatient man, so he will settle the affairs of the Pungcheon Cult and return as quickly as possible.”

Who would be appointed as the next Pungcheon Cult Leader and how the internal restructuring would proceed had already been thoroughly discussed with Gowol.

“I am truly grateful that you accepted my first proposal.”

“It is I who should be grateful. Thanks to you, I have brought a formidable expert like the Pungcheon Cult Leader to my side.”

“Did you not see how plump he had grown from neglecting his training and lazing about? We must put him through proper training when he returns.”

“You must be the one to do it. Who could say such a thing to the Supreme Beyond the World?”

“Please leave it to me.”

It felt as though I had accomplished something truly great. I had gained an exceptional strategist in Gowol, and on top of that, an absolute master in the Pungcheon Cult Leader. Though the Pungcheon Cult Leader had come to the main cult and been pushed around, he remained the strongest in the realm beyond. A fight between masters has so many variables that nothing is ever certain, but I believe there is not a single Demon Venerable confident enough to claim they could defeat him.

“I say, Strategist Gowol.”

“Yes, Young Master.”

“You must know that a strategist’s profession demands one to look into others. Even so, take care of yourself whenever you have the chance.”

I had said “take care” rather than “look into yourself.” Even without explicit advice, clever Gowol would surely grasp the deeper meaning behind my words.

“The Young Master is truly a warm and kind person.”

“Words I hear often. Perhaps it is evidence that I act my part well.”

Gowol did not believe me when I called it an act.

“I shall cover the weaknesses that such kindness brings.”

“Take your time. That is fine.”

“Yes.”

“I shall step out for a moment. I have been neglecting my old subordinates while looking after you. It is time I tended to them as well.”

“Please return safely.”

I headed straight for the Heavenly Demon Hall. Caring for others must be done from the top down.

* * *

Father was in the training ground.

He used to test me with a surprise attack whenever I entered, but today he stood in the center, gazing down at a Go board.

“Do you play Go as well?”

I had known that Father enjoyed Go, but I had never once seen him play.

“There are but a handful in this cult who can defeat me.”

“At Go, you mean?”

“Naturally.”

“Whenever the board turns unfavorable, do you not signal by dragging a hand across your throat? And each time, Uncle Hwi silently appears behind your opponent, and—thrust.”

“On occasion. One must win the match, after all.”

I laughed at Father’s joke and went to his side. I knew Father’s Go skill well. It was not as remarkable as he claimed; he was decent at best. If he had won many games, it was because many had thrown them for him. Such was the downward leveling in the presence of the Heavenly Demon.

“It is a problem given by Strategist Sama, and I cannot find the answer at all.”

It was quite a difficult life-and-death problem.

I examined it quietly before speaking to Father.

“If you play here, will White not live?”

At least at Go, I was the stronger player. Father, having confirmed that it was the correct answer, showed surprise.

“Not bad.”

“Is it not true that a kibitzer standing by often sees the board more clearly?”

“A kibitzer? But I was solving the problem by myself.”

“Did you not have an opponent, Father?”

“Who?”

“Your pride. Your competitive spirit, insisting that you must solve this problem, was seated right there beside you.”

Father scoffed and sat before the Go board.

“Sit beside it as well. Let us play a match.”

“Very well. But no hand-to-throat signals if things turn against you.”

I played Go with Father.

Hunting, then a meal, and now Go. Truly, these were things I could not have dreamed of before my regression.

I focused solely on the game without any idle chatter. Clack, clack. In the silence, the sound of placing stones was crisp.

It was nothing special. Just a father and son playing a game of Go. How had we never done this simple thing even once before? Why had I trembled so at the mere sight of his face?

The beauty of Go is that no words are needed until it ends.

Yet much happens. I built my house, and Father built his. At times I invaded his house, and at times I was hounded like a cornered horse. At times I conceded, and at times I played bold moves.

The game with Father was incredibly enjoyable, yet I displayed my full strength. I well knew that Father was the very embodiment of competitive spirit, so I did not hold back. Should he detect even a hint of being indulged, it would bring a result far worse than losing by a wide margin.

As the situation turned against him, Father stroked his chin. It was the first time I had seen him contemplate so seriously. I suspected he had not looked even at the war table during the war against the Martial Alliance with such earnestness.

I forcibly held back the urge to make such a quip. If I had, Father might have overturned the unfavorable board in spite.

Perhaps even Father wished he could make a playful throat-slitting gesture at this moment.

Naturally, no such thing occurred.

The game ended, and I won by six points.

“Fearing you might not be the Heavenly Demon after all, you truly play a vicious fighting game. I barely won.”

“When did you learn Go?”

“I studied it in my spare time, after hearing that Father was learning from Strategist Sama. Ah, and I welcome a rematch anytime.”

At my triumphant display, Father extended his hand over the board.

Swishhhh.

The stones rose into the air as they were, and the white and black stones crossed paths rapidly. Displaying movements as dazzling as a performance, the stones entered their respective bowls. It was as if they were alive, returning to their homes of their own accord.

“Ah!”

I exclaimed in genuine admiration. This single move seemed to speak to me thus:

*The final realm of controlling objects through the void is something like this. Do you understand?*

This technique alone was enough to crush my spirit, but it seemed Father was not yet satisfied.

“I cannot let you leave while I am the loser, so let us have a duel.”

“That is too much. I was planning to savor the joy of victory and stride proudly out of the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

“If you have been negligent in your martial arts training, you will not walk out of this place.”

He meant to deal with me roughly. It seemed like venting frustration over losing at Go, but it was not.

I knew. He was angry over the matter with the Pungcheon Cult Leader. It was a rebuke for associating with him and accepting a strategist despite knowing that he wished to keep the Pungcheon Cult and its leader at arm’s length.

“They say a great horse does not die easily; surely I will not perish?”

“You had best hope the house you have built is not a prison.”

When Father drew the Heavenly Demon Sword, the surrounding air grew heavy, and an intangible aura began to press down upon me. It was not a sharp, piercing force. It was the kind of pressure that felt like massive boulders crushing me from all sides.

I drew the Black Demon Sword as well and naturally unleashed the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art.

Having withstood the first wave of pressure without changing my expression, Father’s aura grew even more intense.

I raised the Heavenly Demon Guardian Art to greater heights. Having reached a considerable realm after experiencing the state of no-self, my Heavenly Demon Guardian Art held firm once more.

Then, this time, killing intent filled Father’s aura.

It was the first time I had directly felt Father’s killing intent. It was horrifying precisely because it was not gruesome.

Death had clearly settled somewhere upon my body, yet it was as light as a butterfly, so light that I could not tell where it had landed—the horror of searching frantically for where that killing intent lay, only to suddenly realize I had been plunged into a sea of it. And the despair of having no idea what to do. Father’s killing intent was a convergence of all such negative emotions.

I did not oppose Father’s killing intent but felt and accepted it as it was. Amidst the terrible fear and despair, I strove above all else not to lose myself. I strove not to be consumed by fear, but to feel myself feeling the fear.

Resisting the urge to fight back and simply enduring.

At times, the very act of naturally accepting and enduring can exert tremendous pressure on the opponent.

Indeed, Father was startled. That surprise was conveyed to me in full.

*Has your realm truly risen this far?*

The moment I read that emotion in his eyes, Father charged at me.

As Father surged forward with the Bright King Step, I employed the Bright King Step as well. I did not evade, but charged straight at him head-on.

Clang!

Only the cold ring of metal remained as we crossed paths.

From our opponent’s starting point, we turned simultaneously.

From the aftermath of the clash, the Black Demon Sword in my hand quivered. Father’s Heavenly Demon Sword trembled as well. In the past, I would not have seen it, but with my eyesight improved through the Divine Eye Art, I could see that minute trembling.

Did Father’s wrist hurt as much as mine did right now?

“I thought you only busied yourself with scheming, but you are not half bad.”

“Is it not said that romance and martial arts training are done in secret? I have been training diligently.”

Truly, I had been devoting myself to training, cutting back on sleep.

In truth, even when I did not wish to train, my body would not allow otherwise. The vigorous yet serene internal energy accumulated in my dantian longed to be unleashed upon the world, and the Wind God Steps always yearned to fight a strong foe. The Flying Heaven Sword Art was advancing beyond the Great Completion toward a new realm, and thanks to the Divine Eye Art, my eyes had grown sharper as well.

“Then shall we see if you can block this as well?”

The moment the words ended, Father vanished from sight. At the same time, I vanished as well. Like Father, I had used the Dark Shadow Step. Today, I had decided to become an imitator.

Revealing ourselves from a corner of the training ground, our swords clashed in midair.

Identical stances, as if performed by twins!

Beyond the crossed swords, a flicker of joy passed across Father’s face. It was an emotion that lasted but an instant, yet it was unmistakably the joy of meeting a worthy rival.

I well understand the solitude of an absolute being, but even so, Father, I am your son. Not your enemy.

The moment the swords parted, the Heavenly Demon Sword and the Black Demon Sword embroidered the air.

Toward one another, the Equal Heaven Form unfolded.

The blinding white sword light created by the two blades bisected the air.

Two streaks of light met and vanished like two meteors crossing in the night sky. It was but a fleeting moment, yet I found the sight beautiful.

This time, we unleashed the Changing Heaven Form simultaneously.

Twelve transformations occurred in the air. There were twelve from the Heavenly Demon Sword, and twelve from the Black Demon Sword.

If one side was splendid, the other was equally so. If one was heavy, so was the other; if one was fast, so was the other. Transformation met transformation and canceled out.

Our attacks were identical. Just as folding a paper painted with ink on one side produces the same image on the other, we exchanged the exact same strikes.

Thankfully, Father accepted my intention to imitate him, and did not launch any irregular attacks.

The next attack was the sacrificial Blazing Heaven Form.

Fierce sword qi swept through the training ground. As the two sword qis collided, the training ground trembled violently. It was a force that would have collapsed the training ground had but a little more internal energy been poured in.

“Enough!”

Father halted the duel. It seemed he judged the subsequent forms to be too dangerous. That in itself meant I had grown stronger.

Father was someone who thought there could be nothing more to surprise him about me, but today’s surprise was something new once again.

“Your skills have improved greatly.”

Father had used the word “greatly.” Those words were the highest praise, signifying that my skills had improved tremendously.

“Then that is enough.”

Forgiveness was contained in that single phrase.

Regarding the various recent matters concerning the Pungcheon Cult, he had intended to scold me harshly today if I had been negligent in my martial arts training, but having seen that my skills had improved greatly, he had forgiven me.

In the end, it was not a punishment but a reward. A twin-like spar with Father was not something one could buy even for a million nyang.

*How much would it take, Father? How much for a proper match?* Had I asked that, even as his son, I might have died.

While executing the same forms, I felt a great deal. To an outsider, they would have looked identical, but I felt the difference clearly. It is only when one senses that minute difference that one becomes a true transcendent master.

Today, I felt as though I had vaguely come to understand which direction the path of the Flying Heaven Sword Art’s Twelve Forms Great Completion should take.

“Are you not hungry? Today, your son shall buy you a meal. Let us go out to Maga Village and dine.”

I had never heard of Father going out to Maga Village recently. It was a proposal I expected to be refused outright.

“Let us go.”

I was startled by the unexpected permission.

“Are you truly going?”

“Were you merely speaking in vain?”

“Of course not. Let us go!”

Father changed into ordinary martial attire and donned a bamboo hat that could hide his face. Were he to make a formal appearance, Maga Village would be thrown into utter chaos.

And so, I departed the main compound with Father. It was Father’s and my first trip to Maga Village.

Chapter 84: This Is My Regular Place.
-------------------------------------
I entered Maga Village with Father.

Walking through the marketplace together brought a fresh wave of emotion. There is always a certain wonder that comes with doing something for the first time.

“How long has it been since you last came out to Maga Village?”

“Quite some time.”

From the way he said it, it seemed it had been a long while since he had come here.

“Doesn’t it feel nice to get some fresh air?”

I wondered what this street meant to Father.

We walked slowly, looking over the shops lining the road, the peddlers set up before them, and the people passing to and fro as we strolled through the place at an unhurried pace.

It seemed there truly was such a thing as an aura one was born with. Father had not stirred his internal energy or released his qi, yet the path before him opened naturally.

Everyone moved aside without realizing it, never blocking Father’s way. There was something about him that could not be explained merely by the word presence.

I stopped in front of a fabric shop that sold silk and clothing.

“Father, since we’ve come out like this, why don’t we commemorate it by having a set of martial robes made together?”

“I have dozens of martial robes made by master clothiers.”
