“But you don’t have any martial robes made together with your son, do you? Just one set. Please?”

I entered the fabric shop first. Father followed me in, as if he had no choice.

Inside the shop, all manner of silk, cloth, and garments were piled high.

Before Father could change his mind, I hurriedly chose the martial robes.

“What do you think of those robes hanging there?”

They were martial robes embroidered with the Heavenly Demon Soul, the symbol representing our cult. Originally, it was a pattern that could not be used without permission, but since banning it did not stop it from being sold, it had at some point become tolerated like a souvenir.

But Father pointed somewhere else.

“What about those?”

They were bright white martial robes. Plum blossoms were embroidered on the sleeves and legs, making them look, at a glance, like something worn by members of the righteous factions.

“You want those?”

“You said we should go visit the Central Plains next time, didn’t you? We can’t go wearing something like that then, can we?”

“!”

I was truly startled. To think he had remembered my words about going to the Central Plains together!

“I like them too. Give us two sets of those martial robes.”

Amazingly, Father even paid for them.

“You said you would buy the meal, didn’t you? I’ll buy the clothes.”

“Thank you, Father.”

I carefully took the two sets of martial robes, each wrapped in paper.

There was much I wanted to say, but I did not. If I made some needless joke, Father would likely respond with a sharp remark to smooth over the awkward atmosphere. For now, the best thing was simply to enjoy this feeling and this moment.

“Where are you buying the meal?”

“Over there.”

I escorted Father to Pungryu Tavern.

If I were bringing Father somewhere, I should have taken him to the finest inn, tavern, or pleasure house in the area, but I did not want to. At best, such places would be frequented by the Demon Supremes, and I thought a place meaningful to me would be better.

“This is my regular place.”

Jo Chunbae welcomed us with a broad smile.

“You’ve brought a guest I’m seeing for the first time today.”

“He isn’t a guest. He’s family.”

“Family is a first, isn’t it? Right? Hm? Family? If you say family…”

Jo Chunbae flinched and studied Father.

Though he could not see Father’s face beneath the bamboo hat, he sensed from the air about him that Father was not young, and without realizing it, Jo Chunbae took a step back.

Then he asked me with his eyes.

No, right? Surely not, right?

I answered by nodding.

That’s right. The very person you’re thinking of.

Realizing that the person who had come with me was the Heavenly Demon, Jo Chunbae turned deathly pale and began trembling like an aspen tree. How could I possibly miss such great entertainment?

I ordered the dishes Father had chosen.

“Now then, please make them delicious.”

“Yes.”

Jo Chunbae’s legs shook as he walked toward the kitchen. Thanks to that, he heard my sound transmission for the first time as well.

—Cook at ease. Surely my father wouldn’t kill you just because it tastes bad, would he?

Jo Chunbae staggered even more violently and barely managed to enter the kitchen.

“When was the last time you drank in a tavern like this?”

“When I was young, I came to places like this.”

“You haven’t come even once since becoming cult leader, have you?”

Father nodded.

“Doesn’t it feel stifling?”

“It is fine.”

“Well, if you came out into the world, it might feel even more stifling. There are so many people everywhere you’d want to kill, but you can’t kill them all.”

Father gave a faint laugh, and I poured him a drink. Father also filled my cup. Exchanging drinks had become natural now. Then again, we were even getting matching clothes.

“Your internal energy has grown by quite a lot. Where did you obtain it?”

“I received the Blood God Pill from the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

At my honest answer, Father was startled.

“He gave you that? That greedy man?”

“Everyone must think I look pitiful. Whenever they see me, they seem to want to give me spiritual medicines.”

“Hardly.”

Father drank, and I emptied my cup as well. He looked as if he had much to say, but in the end, he said only this.

“As long as you did not let anything of yours be taken, that is enough.”

“Do not worry. I take after you, Father, so when it comes to martial arts, I am quite greedy myself.”

While we were speaking, the dishes came out.

Jo Chunbae was originally a man who strode freely through the tavern. He had the skill to run about with both hands full of dishes without spilling a single thing.

Even when liquor was spilled on the floor, he never slipped. He would run gracefully like a master using Treading on Duckweed to Cross Water, and if someone at another table ordered an expensive dish, he would dash over as swiftly as if using Shifting Form and Changing Position to take their order.

But today’s Jo Chunbae was different. He came tiptoeing, almost crawling, as if afraid the dishes might be disturbed in the slightest. It was the first time I had ever seen him bring food so carefully.

“I thought an assassin was coming.”

At my teasing, Jo Chunbae approached with a desperate expression that begged me not to speak to him, and respectfully set down the dishes he had brought.

“If you need anything else, please call me at any time!”

Then Jo Chunbae went down to the first floor with a face that looked as though he might suffocate.

He could not even stand by the stairs on the first floor to eavesdrop on Father’s evaluation of the food. If he dared to eavesdrop on the Heavenly Demon, even ten necks would not be enough.

Father tasted the food and nodded.

“The flavor is not bad.”

“Please tell the owner directly later. He will truly be happy.”

“Pointless talk.”

“That pointless talk may become a lifelong memory and source of pride for someone. If you don’t say it, they won’t know.”

Father merely used his chopsticks in silence.

Then he abruptly asked,

“Why did you shake the Pungcheon Cult Leader so much? Tell me that.”

Ordinarily, I would have had to answer, “It was because I started out trying to gain a divine artifact, and later wanted to gain the person himself.”

But as a successor, I gave a more fitting answer.

“I did it to separate the Pungcheon Cult from the Eight Demon Supremes.”

“And? Did you succeed?”

“For now, it appears that I have.”

“What did you promise him in return?”

“I want to open a Pungcheon Cult branch in the Central Plains. Even if it is very small.”

“Impossible!”

“Why not?”

Father gave an unexpected answer.

“Do you know why I oppose them entering the Central Plains?”

“Isn’t it because of the history between the Blood Cult and our cult?”

Father shook his head.

“What does the past matter? That is not the reason.”

“Then is it because they joined hands with the Eight Demon Supremes?”

“I deliberately let them join hands.”

Words that would have shocked the Demon Supremes if they heard them flowed from Father’s mouth.

“Whether I strike the Pungcheon Cult or strike the Eight Demon Supremes, if they are entangled with one another, it is easier to create a pretext.”

I could see that Father truly did not fear either the Pungcheon Cult or the Eight Demon Supremes in the slightest.

“Then why do you oppose it?”

“The time has not yet come. Know only that.”

The words that the time had not yet come struck me deeply.

I could guess Father’s thoughts. The Pungcheon Cult’s advance into the Central Plains held great significance not only for us, but also for the Martial Alliance and the Heretical Alliance.

In particular, if the Martial Alliance, which had suffered great damage from the Blood Cult’s invasion of the Central Plains in the past, began framing the Pungcheon Cult’s advance as the revival of the Blood Cult, it could cause enormous political waves.

When he said the time had not yet come, did that not mean, in other words, that he would be the one to decide when that time was?

‘Father, do you perhaps dream, deep in your heart, of unifying the murim?’

Because of Hwa Mugi, he had not managed to realize that dream, but considering Father’s ability, perhaps it was possible. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon called me a sleeping dragon, but perhaps the true sleeping dragon had yet to awaken…

I felt the urge to probe Father indirectly, but if I did so needlessly, this pleasant occasion might be ruined, so I restrained myself for the moment. There would be plenty of chances to find out later.

“I understand. I will handle that matter myself.”

“How?”

“Since I became entangled with the Pungcheon Cult Leader on a personal level, I will resolve it on a personal level.”

“On a personal level? That is the most difficult method.”

“But it should also be the most effective. Do not worry. I will not indulge in the arrogance of thinking I know another person’s heart. I will not be greedy enough to think I can gain another person’s heart, and I will never fully trust it either.”

It was only half true. I did not want to live such a life. What meaning would there be in becoming the Heavenly Demon while suspecting everyone?

I may not be able to know every human heart, but I would strive to understand them. I may not be able to gain every human heart, but I would at least gain enough that we would not face one another with lies and hypocrisy. And those worth trusting, I still intended to trust.

“Please leave the matter of the Pungcheon Cult Leader to me. I will handle it well.”

Father nodded, then rose from his seat.

“I ate well. Come by next month to play go.”

“Yes! You had better polish the stones well!”

When Father extended his hand, one of the two sets of martial robes placed beside me flew to him with a whoosh.

Holding it, Father went down to the first floor.

More than buying the martial robes together, more than him paying for them, the sight of him taking his own martial robes without my having said anything moved me even more.

And Jo Chunbae completed Father’s unexpected side.

As Father passed by, Jo Chunbae, who had been standing beside the counter, bowed so deeply that his waist nearly touched the floor.

“It was an honor to serve you.”

Father, who had been about to leave without a word, stopped walking.

“The flavor was decent.”

Leaving behind Jo Chunbae, who stood there gasping in shock and emotion, Father went outside.

Jo Chunbae, who had been standing there blankly, belatedly shouted,

“Yes! Thank you! Please visit us again next time!”

Father was already walking far into the distance.

Only then did the tension leave Jo Chunbae, and he sank down with a long sigh of relief.

He took out the liquor he had hidden beneath the counter and gulped it down. He was a man who did not drink while doing business unless something truly extraordinary happened, but today had gone beyond extraordinary.

Leaning against the railing on the second floor, I asked him,

“How do you feel?”

“I can die now without regrets. Even if I am born again, I will live as the owner of Pungryu Tavern.”

“If you are born again, you should live as a hero of the murim.”

“No, thank you. I am satisfied with this life of watching someone like you from the side, Young Master. Serving delicious food is enough for me.”

“You could become a master and still live this kind of life, couldn’t you?”

“Then I would be even unhappier. I would have to endure things even though I was a master.”

“I see.”

After watching the rare sight of Jo Chunbae drinking, I looked outside the tavern. Far away, among the people, I could see Father’s back as he walked slowly. Who among them would know? That they had just passed by the Heavenly Demon.

Before my regression, I did not look closely.

Not at Father’s back like that, not at Jo Chunbae smiling happily with his flushed face, not at the people hurrying along the road to wherever they were going. I did not look. I did not look into their eyes or mouths to see with what hearts they lived. Because I did not look, I did not know, and because I did not know, I could not win.

Yes, I did not lose to Hwa Mugi. I lost to my own life. To my indifferent life.

“Young Master, shall I bring you more liquor?”

Jo Chunbae asked from below.

“No. There are people I need to see today.”

Leaving Pungryu Tavern, I walked along the very road Father had taken and returned to the cult.

Chapter 85: Family Is the Enemy.
--------------------------------
When the Ilhwa Sword Venerable stepped out from her thatched hut, I was crouching and watching the flowers growing in a corner of the courtyard.

“Amazing, isn’t it?”

“They’ve grown so much already.”

“This is the joy of raising them.”

“I should try growing some myself next time.”

“You are busy, so it won’t be easy.”

“It would become a patch of weeds, wouldn’t it? I should stick to what I’m good at.”

I, who had been crouching, rose to my feet.

“Today, I have come as your sparring friend.”

“Welcome.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s expression brightened brilliantly. She truly looked as if she had been waiting for this day for a long time.

“Thank you so much for all your help as of late.”

“What help is there to speak of?”

Whether it was leaking information to Saujong, Cheongseon becoming a Demon Lord, or going to meet the Pungcheon Cult Leader. She claimed to be only half-convinced, but her actions had been those of someone fully committed—if not more.

“This is a gift from the heart, to express my gratitude.”

“What need is there for a gift?”

“It is a token of my sincerity, so please accept it.”

She opened the small box I handed her. Inside was a Nightshining Pearl. It was one of the Nightshining Pearls I had obtained from the Golden Palm Lord, Geum Asu.

“Something this precious?”

“It is different from what I gave Elder Doma.”

I had given Doma Blood Poison Wine. When I emphasized that, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable laughed, as though she found it amusing. This was how I tore down the dam of ill feelings I had built up with Blood Sky Doma. If I removed the stones one by one, someday the other side would come into view.

“Truly beautiful.”

“If I may boast a little, it is a top-grade Nightshining Pearl. It is not something easily obtained even with money.”

“It is more than enough to boast about.”

“Even so, it cannot compare to your beauty, Senior.”

“You lay the gold on this old woman too thick.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable waved her hands in refusal, but her face was beaming. Though she was changing in many ways from before, this weakness to compliments about her appearance was one thing that would never change.

“You have more than enough in you to become a rogue later on.”

“I intend to live that way. I’m going to travel around and meet all the great beauties of the Central Plains.”

“If you could live such a life, it would be good, but…”

The words “once you become the Heavenly Demon, that will be impossible” were left unsaid. Because wandering around now and wandering around as the Heavenly Demon were as different as heaven and earth.

Indeed, it wasn’t as if Father stayed at the main cult because he had no frustrations. With the fate of the martial world hanging upon his every step, it was only natural for him to be cautious.

“You had better enjoy yourself now. Later will be difficult.”

“I will.”

“Now then, shall we loosen up after so long?”

“It is a moment I have long awaited.”

The person I had originally wanted to tend to next after Father was Blood Sky Doma.

But after sparring with Father, my body itched for more. Wanting to continue with another round, I had sought her out first.

It was definitely different from our last spar.

Her minute movements were felt more clearly. Before I could sense them through intuition, my eyes simply saw them.

Just as Father had done to me, I pressed her more fiercely than before. It was possible because I had the confidence that I could control my sword. She fought desperately, and I too gave my all.

When the spar ended, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable breathed roughly, surprise plain upon her face. Save for her final hidden move, she had poured her entire strength into it, yet she had been unable to subdue me.

“You’ve grown stronger than before!”

“I learned something in the interim.”

“Truly amazing. Truly amazing.”

She was happy, yet at the same time regretful.

“If you grow this quickly, soon I won’t be a match for you.”

“I gave my all for the sake of our next spar, Senior.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable understood my meaning and nodded.

“It is the hope that this old senior will grow too, is it not?”

“Audacious as it is, yes. I wish to be sparring friends with you for a lifetime, Senior.”

At my honest words, an uncontainable surge of emotion flickered in her eyes.

It was also part of my effort to make her my right wing.

Before I left, we exchanged various stories about sparring.

Though my skills were clearly ahead, it was not as if there was nothing to learn from her. There was a learning of a different nature from what I gained from my father.

Starting with martial arts, we shared stories of life. It was such a pleasant time that when we bid farewell, she was left quite reluctant to part.

Next, I went to Blood Sky Doma’s abode. But he was not home.

After waiting for a long time and returning empty-handed, I found Blood Sky Doma waiting in front of my house.

“Where have you been wandering around in the dead of night? Hiding some woman somewhere?”

It seemed our paths had crossed.

“If you were here, why not wait inside? Advisor Go is in there.”

“Forget it.”

“We are family now. Why stand on ceremony?”

“What family? Family are the worst enemies.”

He still seemed awkward with Gowol. Well, he had only grown comfortable with me; this prickly old man had never liked interacting with people.

“What brings you here?”

“I came to deliver some news.”

“What news?”

Blood Sky Doma spoke with a serious face.

“Mabul has left to fetch the Eldest Young Master.”

I was not surprised. It was an expected move. The news that the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s advisor had come to me would have reached the Demon Lords supporting my brother. They must have decided they could no longer simply stand by.

Coincidentally, even in my previous life, my brother had returned to the main cult around this time. What is meant to happen happens all the same.

“Mabul is always busy. Fetching the Pungcheon Cult Leader, fetching my brother.”

“That little human is incredibly, enormously diligent. What on earth is your brother doing in the frontier?”

“I wouldn’t know.”

Of course, I knew. My brother was currently passing through the gateway that would change his life. That fact was only revealed after he arrived, and afterward, he completely consolidated his position as successor.

“Are you alright, Elder?”

“What do you mean?”

“You were supporting my brother before coming to me. He may resent you.”

“Let him if he wants.”

In such matters, Blood Sky Doma held clear values.

“If there is a better option, you choose it. Anyone who badmouths you over personal ties is someone utterly useless in life.”

“You speak rightly.”

“What’s right? The same applies to when I cast you aside.”

I truly liked this frankness of his.

“Are you alright?”

“What do you mean?”

After a brief pause, Blood Sky Doma spoke.

“What will you do if the time comes when you must kill your brother?”

“If he does something deserving death, I must kill him.”

“Putting on a brave face.”

I smirked. Yes, it was a brave face. Killing one’s own blood was never easy.

It was not a matter of whether my martial arts were stronger than my brother’s. A fight with my brother was one that demanded every ounce of mental strength. The words Blood Sky Doma had spoken jokingly earlier were correct. Family are the worst enemies.

“That’s right. Of course I’m not alright. I am not a cold-blooded man; how could killing my brother be alright? And he is not one to die easily.”

I turned and shouted into the house.

“Advisor Go, come out.”

Gowol came out of the house.

“Welcome back?”

After greeting me, Gowol politely greeted Blood Sky Doma as well.

“Mabul has gone to retrieve my brother. What do you think?”

Gowol could not answer easily. Perhaps for another enemy, but when the elder brother of the leader he served became the enemy, an advisor could not help but be cautious.

After pondering briefly, he spoke carefully.

“The Eldest Young Master’s life or death is not a matter for the young lord to decide. The Eldest Young Master will decide for himself.”

In the end, it meant my brother’s fate would be determined by what kind of person he turned out to be.

I nodded, but Blood Sky Doma still did not trust him.

“I helped because you wanted to recruit him, but…”

Blood Sky Doma slowly approached and stood before Gowol. Staring into his eyes, he said:

“Is there any guarantee that this one won’t betray us?”

The word *us* reached my ears. It was the first time Blood Sky Doma had used that expression with me. It was likely said without thought, but it meant a great deal to me. The man who had poked and threatened me until my sides bruised had now become *us*.

Even under Blood Sky Doma’s pressure, Gowol showed no fear.

“What about you, Demon Lord? Is there any guarantee that you will not betray the young lord?”

At the question of whether he too could betray, the corners of Blood Sky Doma’s lips curled up.

“There is no guarantee. I’ll betray whenever I feel like it.”

“That is why I am here—to prevent that.”

“Then who will stop you?”

“I will not betray.”

“How am I to believe that?”

“You need only trust the young lord.”

“Do not forget that I am watching you.”

I did not stop their war of nerves. Was that not my life philosophy? Not to let wounds and conflicts fester, but to bring them out, dig them up, and fight to resolve them.

Blood Sky Doma spoke to me with a hesitant expression, unsure whether he should say what was on his mind.

“The Cult Leader will not wish for you and the Eldest Young Master to fight. He does not wish for the death of the Eldest Young Master, nor yours.”

I could tell once again that Blood Sky Doma knew my father well. To be confident enough to draw a line on the ground and say that Father thought this much of me, he must have shared a special relationship with him.

“I had felt it too.”

After my regression, when I went hunting with Father, he had casually asked if I couldn’t embrace my brother and go with him. There had been true feeling contained in that question.

“The Cult Leader is a man who never looks back. The one thing such a man regrets is this.”

Even in a heart of eternal ice, wounds remain.

“You said you knew the Cult Leader’s heart?”

“Yes.”

“Then that is enough.”

*Then that is enough*—those were the words Father had spoken to me after sparring. Both Father’s words and Blood Sky Doma’s words now strangely gave me strength.

“Is there anything I should prepare?”

“My request to you is always the same.”

“What is it?”

“That you reconcile.”

“What reconciliation? Surely you don’t mean…?”

Blood Sky Doma realized I meant a reconciliation between him and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“The Eldest Young Master is coming, and you speak of reconciliation. You are truly a madman, without a doubt.”

How exasperated must he have been to seek agreement from Gowol?

“Your master is this kind of madman.”

Then Gowol answered:

“I am not normal either.”

Blood Sky Doma shook his head, saying we were a pair of madmen.

I tried to persuade him again.

“When my brother returns, the other Demon Lords will move in earnest as well. You cannot fight with only one wing, can you?”

“I would like that too, but haven’t you seen enough? The Sword Venerable snaps at me viciously the moment she sees me.”

“Yes, I have seen enough. Her fierceness has been subsiding as time goes on. The gaze with which she glares at you has been gradually softening.”

Still, Blood Sky Doma shook his head.

“Our reconciliation is a more difficult task than unifying the martial world. I’m going.”

I shouted after him as he turned to leave.

“You need not unify the martial world, but you must reconcile.”

Without answering, Blood Sky Doma slowly walked and disappeared into the darkness.

I immediately looked after Gowol.

“Are you alright?”

“Yes. I am fine.”

“Don’t pay it too much mind. He is an old man with an innately foul temper.”

“I must pay mind. He is your left wing, young lord. Please do not worry about me. As you know, I have not lived an easy life.”

But I know. Even such a person is not immune to being hurt.

“I understand. Still, if you have something to say to me, please do. If I have something troubling me, I will speak to you as well.”

“Yes. I shall.”

“By the way, my candidate for the right wing is the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. Keep that in mind.”

“Understood. Then please rest well.”

That day, I could not fall asleep until late.

There was no need to think about how my brother had turned out in my previous life.

When my brother returned, I would see him. In my previous life, I had not seen anyone properly; now, I would see him clearly.

I would see with my own eyes what kind of person he was. I would clearly see what the substance of all those things in my memory had been.

As always, at times like these, the Hwamugi helped.

If I were a vessel unable to overcome my brother, I would die to the Hwamugi anyway.

Chapter 86: Long Time No See, Brother.
--------------------------------------
Two months had passed since the spar with Father.

In that time, I reached the eleventh star of the Soaring Sky Sword Art. It was through the enlightenment gained from the spar with Father and One-Stroke Sword Sovereign that I ascended to the next level.

Now only one step remained.

The final step, like those before it, would require some kind of trigger.

It might come from another spar, or a real battle; perhaps while viewing flowers with One-Stroke Sword Sovereign, or reading a poetry collection beside Blood Heaven Blade Demon, or maybe taking a moonlit walk with Ian. That was the realm of the twelfth star’s completion—utterly unpredictable.

Leaving the completion of the Soaring Sky Sword Art to another twist of fate, I devoted myself to training in the Spacetime Exchange Art.

When practicing martial arts, there are moments when you feel it—a tingling sensation stimulating your entire body, the art itself whispering, “Just a little more.” That is the moment you grow to the next level.

That stimulation came constantly with the Spacetime Exchange Art.

Over ten days ago, I had reduced the time needed to unfold space to slowly counting to about one hundred. After that, as if the restrictions had been lifted, the time decreased every time I performed the Spacetime Exchange Art.

If there is compatibility between a martial artist and a martial art, then this Spacetime Exchange Art suited me all too well.

Perhaps it was because this art itself valued enlightenment and talent more than training duration. And in those two aspects, I had ascended to nearly the highest realm.

The more I trained, the more the time continued to decrease.

From one hundred to ninety-nine, ninety-eight, ninety-seven…….

I didn’t stop, pushing all the way to the end. I created space like a madman, as if riding a galloping carriage. At this rate, I thought, if I took a wrong turn, the carriage might fall off a cliff, but I continued to refine it without rest.

Training and training again.

And finally, the carriage arrived at its last station. The horse did not collapse, and I did not fall from the carriage either.

Snap!

The moment I flicked my fingers, my surroundings changed.

At the same time, a cheer burst from my lips.

“I did it!”

I could now perform the Spacetime Exchange Art instantly. It unfolded so naturally, as if it had been my martial art all along.

Right now, I was standing on a white sandy beach with an endlessly stretching blue sea. The sky was blue, and the sun was hot. On the beach stood a broad-leaved tree, and beneath its shade sat a chair that looked comfortable even at a glance. In the distance, seagulls were flying.

This was the space of my dreams, belonging only to me.

It was perfect.

Just as the time to create it had decreased, the internal energy required to maintain this place had also lessened.

I leaned back comfortably in the chair for a moment, then took off my clothes and ran into the sea.

After swimming for a while, I lay back and looked at the sky. Watching the drifting clouds, my mind was incredibly at peace.

It was a perfect time belonging only to me, undisturbed by anyone. I was happy.

Then, suddenly, a thought occurred to me.

What if I could make time pass more slowly here than outside? Then what if I could train in martial arts here? Even if I couldn’t stop time completely, what if I could slow it down by even half?

It was a dream-like thought, but from the beginning, wasn’t creating a space like this itself a dream-like feat?

I thought that when the Wind Sky Cult Leader returned, I should discuss this with him. Though he would probably say it was nonsense, of course.

I had been lying on the beach enjoying my freedom for quite some time when I sensed someone arriving outside the Spacetime Exchange Art.

When I had entered spaces created by the Heaven-Devouring Demon Lord or the Wind Sky Cult Leader, I couldn’t sense the outside situation at all; but since I was creating the space myself, I could perceive what was happening outside.

I immediately dispelled the Spacetime Exchange Art and revealed myself.

“Young Master, are you there?”

The one calling for me at the door was Jang Ho.

“I’m here.”

When I appeared suddenly from behind him, Jang Ho started and turned around.

“Young Master? Where were you?”

On a beach with surging blue waves.

I forced myself to swallow the urge to answer like that.

“I was briefly training in a concealment art. But what brings you here?”

“A message has come from the Iron Workshop. They have finished crafting the item you entrusted to them, made from ten-thousand-year cold iron.”

“Then I must trouble you. Go fetch the item, and gather everyone who was here that day.”

“Understood.”

* * *

Seven people stood in a circle, looking down at the objects placed on the table.

Including me, the other six were Ian, Seo Dae-ryong, Jang Ho, Blood Heaven Blade Demon and One-Stroke Sword Sovereign, and Go Wol.

Among the items on the table, I first picked up the fan and handed it to Go Wol.

“This is the fan you requested. I examined it, and it was well made.”

“Thank you so very much.”

“The materials are valuable, but what makes it more precious is that it was crafted by the finest artisan of our cult.”

“I truly didn’t think you would have it made for me.”

I had also made daggers in the number of people gathered that day, so I gave him a dagger as well. Upon receiving both the fan and the dagger, Go Wol looked apologetic.

“I am receiving two things.”

“These are daggers that wouldn’t have existed in the first place if not for you. Consider them a gift from you to everyone.”

“That’s not true. It was you, Young Master, for whom the Wind Sky Cult Leader removed those shackles. Thank you. I will never forget this tremendous gift.”

It was indeed a great gift.

Ten-thousand-year cold iron daggers were incomparably sharp and absolutely unbreakable, making them truly priceless. If they could be sold at proper value, the money would be enough for several large families to live on without working.

But the gifts for him did not end there.

I untied the supreme-grade heavenly silkworm thread from the hilt of the Black Demon Sword. I cut just enough to fill the fan and handed it to Go Wol.

“This is supreme-grade heavenly silkworm thread. If you attach this to the fan, it will become an excellent weapon.”

Go Wol’s pupils trembled with emotion. These three gifts were truly things one couldn’t receive in a lifetime.

“I will repay this grace with my life if necessary.”

“There is no need. I give this because I believe it holds that much value for me now, not as collateral for future loyalty or your life.”

I said this to Go Wol, but at the same time, it was also a message to the others.

I distributed the remaining daggers to everyone.

“Thank you for standing by my side.”

“Thank you.”

“I will use it well.”

“Thank you.”

Everyone received the daggers gladly.

Seo Dae-ryong looked down at his dagger and spoke worriedly.

“I’m afraid I might lose it.”

“Train harder in martial arts. The stronger you become, the less likely you are to lose it. When training is difficult, look at that dagger.”

“Yes.”

At my words, Seo Dae-ryong nodded vigorously. These daggers would come to hold various meanings for each of them.

Then Blood Heaven Blade Demon needlessly picked a fight with Go Wol.

“If that frail scholar has one, what are you worried about?”

Just then, someone entered the room and spoke.

“Rather than me, isn’t your own disciple more frail?”

The one who entered was none other than the Wind Sky Cult Leader.

I asked him with a surprised expression.

“Have you returned already?”

He had come back earlier than I thought. I had assumed it would take at least six months.

“I am an impatient man by nature.”

As he spoke, he glanced at Go Wol. At the appearance of his staunch supporter, Go Wol smiled.

“You seem to have starved wherever you went. Returning precisely on the fateful day to receive a dagger? Give me my dagger.”

The Wind Sky Cult Leader examined the dagger.

“It really is well made. This could be considered a divine weapon. Thank you for getting one for me as well.”

“You are too kind. In the first place, we could make them because you graciously removed the shackles, Cult Leader.”

I welcomed his return with pleasant words. He had returned on a fateful day, as he said. But that fate was not the day to receive a dagger.

Someone opened the door and entered.

The visitor was tall, with features that anyone would call handsome. Dressed in a white ceremonial robe embroidered with splendid yet refined patterns, he was my elder brother, the First Young Master, Geom Mu-yang.

Everyone’s attention focused on him. Three people followed behind Geom Mu-yang.

The first to enter was Ma Bul, and after him came an old man and a young man together.

Geom Mu-yang, spotting me among the crowd, spread his arms wide and strode over briskly.

“Little brother, have you been well?”

That’s right. This was how my brother was. A man who was brimming with confidence and keenly aware of his own superiority.

His tendency to consider himself clever often became a problem, but he also had the knack of making his arrogance appear as confidence to those on his side.

“I’ve been well. It’s been a while, Brother.”

I embraced my brother tightly. Not with pretense, but with genuine feeling. I could sense him slightly flustered by my reaction. He wouldn’t know what I was thinking right now.

‘Brother, I’ll make sure I don’t embrace your corpse and regret it. I’ll make sure you don’t walk Father’s path.’

I know. Go Wol said the outcome depends on my brother, but that’s not true. This matter depends on my effort.

‘So I’ll work hard. Not for my brother, but for myself.’

Perhaps because I felt quite different from when I left the cult, a strange light flickered in Geom Mu-yang’s eyes.

“I heard you made quite a name for yourself while I was away?”

“A fox reigns as king in a valley without a tiger.”

“How can a younger brother be a fox when his elder brother is a tiger? You need not act humble before me.”

At a glance, he truly looked like an affectionate elder brother who cherished his younger sibling.

But in my past life, my brother had tormented me relentlessly. At the time, I hadn’t realized it was torment.

Ever since I was very young, Brother had tried to instill negative impressions of martial arts and power.

Stories of martial artists killing each other in battle, stories of going mad after falling into demonic cultivation, stories of demon lords betraying the Heavenly Demon.

Most of the stories he told me were cruel and negative. If I ever tried to open the Conception and Governing Vessels, he would shake my heart with tales of my blood vessels bursting and dying.

I overcame it with innate resilience, but it had undoubtedly affected me negatively, knowingly or unknowingly.

I can’t fault using all means and methods in a succession struggle, but you shouldn’t do that to a younger brother of seven or eight years old, Brother.

Then Ma Bul interjected.

“Second Young Master. Now that the First Young Master has returned, you must help your elder brother greatly. Who is there in this harsh martial world to look after one another? Humans betray as easily as they eat, so there is no one to trust.”

As he spoke, Ma Bul looked at Blood Heaven Blade Demon. He had aimed those words at him from the start, openly criticizing Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had supported Geom Mu-yang before turning to me.

Blood Heaven Blade Demon, feeling guilty toward the First Young Master, merely snorted.

Unlike the provocative Ma Bul, Geom Mu-yang was courteous to Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Have you been well, Elder?”

“I’ve been well, thanks to you. Are you on your way back now?”

“Yes. I have just arrived.”

“Have you seen the Cult Leader?”

“Father had already retired to his quarters, so I was unable to see him. I suppose I must pay my respects tomorrow morning.”

“The Cult Leader will be pleased.”

After exchanging greetings, Geom Mu-yang spoke nonchalantly.

“I heard you have been helping my younger brother a great deal recently, Elder.”

“Old men are notoriously fickle by nature. You must understand.”

“Not at all. My brother’s affairs are my affairs, and the cult’s affairs. As his elder brother, I thank you.”

“Thank you for understanding it that way.”

This time, Geom Mu-yang spoke to One-Stroke Sword Sovereign.

“You are still as beautiful as ever.”

“As expected of brothers, our First Young Master is also quite the ladies’ man.”

Geom Mu-yang took something from the sleeve of his robe and handed it to One-Stroke Sword Sovereign.

“It is a cosmetic I obtained with difficulty from outside the realm. It is said to be far superior to what the women of the Central Plains use, so please try it.”

“Thank you, First Young Master.”

As if he would have brought a gift only for One-Stroke Sword Sovereign? He would have bought plenty to suit the tastes of the other demon lords as well.

Even in this brief greeting, I could clearly see why my brother had been surpassing me around this time. Back then, I hadn’t known my brother was taking such meticulous care of the demon lords. I must have only been looking at myself. I must have lived only seeing my own greed.

“Lord Jang, though belated, congratulations on becoming a lord.”

“Thank you, First Young Master.”

That was the end of the greetings. He did not exchange greetings with Ian and Seo Dae-ryong. My brother was a thorough authoritarian. His philosophy was that if he secured his superiors, the subordinates would naturally follow.

After Geom Mu-yang finished greeting the two demon lords, Ma Bul stepped forward this time.

When he saw the Wind Sky Cult Leader, he immediately picked a fight.

“Did someone smear honey on the cult’s pillar? Why do you keep lingering around?”

Of course, the Wind Sky Cult Leader was no pushover either.

“Because someone took a shit and didn’t clean up. A small, cute golden shit, that is.”

Ma Bul’s expression instantly stiffened. The two glared at each other as if they wanted to kill. As if to show that while closeness between people takes ages, estrangement takes but a moment, the two who had been closest were now so divided.

As the two formed a taut tension, Geom Mu-yang signaled with his eyes to the two people behind him.

Then the old man and young man who had entered with Ma Bul came forward to greet.

A cold smile formed at the corners of my lips. The moment these two entered was what I had been waiting for the most. In fact, I had waited decades for this moment.

Chapter 87: Blood Falls from the Sky.
-------------------------------------
Of the two, the younger-looking man stepped forward first, clasped his hands in greeting, and bowed.

“I am called Sotae, from Shaanxi. I have long held the Divine Cult in the highest regard, and by chance formed a connection with the Demonic Lord, which has led me to meet you like this. This person here is my master.”

The old man followed with a greeting of his own.

“I am called Jongmak, from Shaanxi. It is a pleasure to meet the heroes of the Divine Cult.”

There are no martial brothers named Sotae and Jongmak living in Shaanxi. There never have been, and there never will be.

Their true identities were the ones known as the Yunnan Twin Fiends.

As masters of disguise, deceiving others about their age was child’s play; sometimes they appeared as beanpoles, sometimes as fat men, as merchants, monks, and even women.

In reality, the young wretch who had greeted first was the First Fiend of the Twin Fiends and was actually older than the Second Fiend disguised as that old man over there.

They deceived people by disguising themselves as all manner of folk, committing countless evils in the process. They looted the wealth of merchants and killed people they had just met simply because their faces annoyed them.

Their lust was equally strong; if a beautiful woman caught their eye, they inevitably violated her, and they murdered anyone who tried to stop them—truly vicious men.

The Martial Alliance declared them Public Enemies of the Martial World but ultimately failed to capture them.

This was because their martial arts were powerful, and they were masters of disguise, so they could not easily be caught.

They enjoyed full natural lifespans. Once, this saying was popular:

“Heaven is not heartless. Because they are already dead.”

It was a saying that came about entirely because of these bastards.

And decisively, these bastards had ruined my life as well.

The First Fiend, disguised as a young man, stepped forward and spoke.

“If you, Second Young Master, whom I have always respected, would teach me a move, it would be the greatest honor for my family.”

What is meant to happen, happens.

In my life before the regression, the same event had occurred around this time.

Back then, I was truly an idiot. The worst of idiots, a complete and utter moron.

Why on earth had I accepted the duel?

Of course, at that time it had not been here but in a different situation where I crossed blades with this bastard.

Even then, they had followed Mabul. Mabul had introduced those Yunnan Twin Fiends at a banquet attended by the demonic members of the main cult and fanned the flames so that I could not refuse.

He had exploited my psychology flawlessly. Instead of urging me to teach him a move, he scolded the First Fiend.

“Who are you to challenge such a noble person to a duel? This is not your place. Should you make one wrong move and the Second Young Master be injured, you will not keep your life.”

He had spoken those very same words back then, just as he did now.

Just as telling a man to “flip the table like a man” is less effective than asking, “Would that coward dare to flip it?”, the twenty-year-old me, who lived and died by the judgment of others, had fallen for that shallow provocation.

Moreover, back then I had lost a duel due to sand poison in the contest between the Divine and Demonic, and was burning with desperate need to restore my fallen reputation.

I had proven with my own body that the lower one’s self-esteem, the more easily one falls for an opponent’s taunts.

The result had been devastating.

I had foolishly believed there was no way I would lose to someone my age, but we were not the same age at all.

My arm was broken, my ribs cracked, and my face bruised so badly that I could not go outside for a time.

The problem was not my wounds. My pride had crumbled at losing to a supposed peer, and the self-loathing of having so easily fallen for the provocation tormented me.

My father, as well as the demonic members of the main cult, had been disappointed in me. Having been thoroughly thrashed by an outsider, my authority and prestige had fallen into the dust.

The Yunnan Twin Fiends had fled that very day, so at the time their true identities had not even been revealed.

And now those very bastards had appeared before my eyes again. So when they followed Mabul into my room, just how excited do you think I was?

“Of course, we can’t have this precious body getting hurt. These days, rising experts are so frightening.”

I could simply back out like this. The more confident one is in one’s skills, the more leisurely one acts. Of course, I had no intention of doing that today.

“But it would be unmannerly to refuse when someone asks so earnestly, would it not?”

I was curious. Did my brother know what kind of people these were that associated with Mabul? Brother, this is a problem whether you know or not.

“Thank you for accepting the duel. Truly, you live up to your renowned name, Second Young Master.”

“However, you have been flattering me to my face from the start. Have you heard anything about my reputation? If you know of anything I have done recently, name just one.”

Apparently not expecting me to ask so bluntly, the First Fiend was flustered.

“My memory is poor, so I cannot recall. I am uneducated and lacking in manners. I hope you will understand, Second Young Master.”

“I thought as much. Asking for a duel the moment we meet shows you had no manners to begin with, and the fact that this idiot acts this way without knowing my personality or strength shows your head is indeed quite rotten. Still, it is a small mercy that you know yourself.”

At my mockery, the Twin Fiends’ expressions stiffened simultaneously. I could feel them forcibly suppressing the malice they could no longer hide.

“Since your head is rotten, enjoy a round with your body instead, Second Young Master.”

“Let’s do that.”

And so we went outside and stood facing each other in the courtyard. Everyone who had been inside followed.

“You will use fists and palms?”

“Yes.”

“I use the sword, so be careful not to lose both your hands to my blade.”

“You should also be careful, Second Young Master. From now on, you may need to hold that sword in your left hand.”

As he tensed his eyes to heighten the tension, the Blood Heaven Demon Blade stepped forward.

“I shall take my leave first. As I have grown old, I sleep more in the evenings.”

He appeared to have no interest in the duel’s outcome and seemed unwilling to remain long where the Eldest Young Master and I were together.

At that, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable followed suit.

“I shall also take my leave. If I don’t sleep, my skin becomes dry.”

As the two bid farewell, Geom Muyang respectfully greeted them.

“Then please retire. I shall visit you separately later.”

Mabul asked the question my brother had wanted to ask.

“You do not seem worried about the Second Young Master.”

The Blood Heaven Demon Blade did not even glance at me as he tossed out a word and walked away.

“Worry my ass.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable followed behind him. At least in this moment, they were of one mind.

They were the type to know well that it was best to avoid the scandal of having been present at such a fight, and more than anything, they did not have so much as a speck of concern for me.

Surprisingly, Jangho followed as well.

“Then I also have business to attend to, so I shall take my leave.”

As the quick-witted bear made to leave, the nine-tailed fox followed.

“Then I shall also take my leave.”

I spoke to Ian.

“Ian, you’re leaving too?”

“Yes. I must go train.”

“If anyone should stay and at least pretend to worry, it’s you. You’ve changed!”

“Of course I have. Because someone kept singing in my ear that I had to change, had to change.”

As she walked, she turned to Seo Daeryong.

“Aren’t you going?”

“I will stay and watch. I think it will help with my martial training.”

Then the Blood Heaven Demon Blade, walking far away, shouted at him.

“Watching won’t help you one damn bit. Go get some sleep.”

With his master having spoken, he could not stay. Seo Daeryong grinned at me.

“So he says. How sharp his ears are.”

Once even Seo Daeryong had left, the Wind Heaven Cult Leader moved as well.

“If everyone leaves and I stay, I’ll look like an idle person, won’t I?”

And so they all left, each leaving a parting word.

“You’re all too much!”

No one turned back at my hollow cry.

I understood. Their reasons for leaving were all different, but it was an act filled with respect for me. A show for my brother.

We trust the Second Young Master this much.

By leaving, they had demonstrated their loyalty to me in a manner that was, in its own way, quite sophisticated.

Now only Gowol, who stayed with me, remained.

“You are truly my most loyal subject.”

“I was about to tell you that I would retire first as well. I do not enjoy watching fights.”

“That won’t do. At least one person must stay and watch.”

“Then please finish it quickly.”

“I shall.”

All who watched this looked on with expressions of shock and disbelief.

Perhaps feeling thoroughly ignored, the Yunnan Twin Fiends openly revealed their displeasure, and even Geom Muyang, who rarely showed emotion, could not completely hide his surprise at the situation.

The First Fiend stepped forward and spoke.

“The spectators have left, so the excitement has died down, but my fists have not lost their strength. Let us have a round.”

He was anxious, perhaps fearing the duel might be canceled.

Do not worry, evildoer.

“Let’s do it. This is a fight I have long waited for.”

Far longer than you could ever imagine.

I accepted this duel partly for revenge, but more so to pass judgment on the evils they had committed.

They were included among the absolute evil of which I spoke.

There are those called absolute evil because they are too strong, but there are also those like them who are absolute evil because they are cunning and wicked.

Those skilled in disguise who, once they hide, are impossible to find. Those who treat others’ lives like flies yet treasure their own lives dearly, desperate to preserve them at all costs.

Therefore, those who live comfortably until they die of old age—these, too, are absolute evil.

Perhaps Heaven grew displeased with the popular saying that it was dead. Seeing as it sent them before me.

“Now, I will not hold back against you just because you are the Second Young Master, so be careful!”

The First Fiend showed no mercy from the first move. Whatever happened, their mission was to beat me and vanish.

How much had they received from Mabul? Coming all the way to the main cult to face me, they must have been paid handsomely.

I dodged the flying fist and lightly seized his wrist.

The bastard was shocked, never having imagined his wrist would be caught on the first move.

He tried to shake free, but the moment his tightly gripped hand would not budge—

—Could it be? The moment the bastard’s eyes flew wide open, my sword was drawn like lightning.

Shhk!

In a single move, his wrist was severed.

“Ugh!”

A short scream burst out, and he instinctively tried to stanch the bleeding.

Shhiiik! Shhk!

Without a moment’s hesitation, I cut off his other wrist as well.

“Aaaaaaaagh!”

He screamed in agony, flailing his severed hands and spraying blood like a fountain.

Blood poured down around us like rain. I did not dodge, and neither did my brother.

Amidst the gushing blood, we simply looked at each other.

Then the Second Fiend ambushed me from behind. He likely intended to subdue me and then treat the First Fiend. I began to turn to block, but when I saw my brother draw his sword, I simply stayed put.

Swhaaaaaaack!

A ferocious sword aura burst forth in an instant, brushing past the side of my face, but I did not even blink and kept looking at my brother.

The sword aura that had brushed past me bisected the Second Fiend, who had been rushing from behind, cleanly in two.

In an instant, that place became a sea of blood.

I asked Mabul.

“Just what were you expecting?”

It was a question laden with meaning. My brother had distanced himself, but you have heard and seen everything about me.

Did you think those Yunnan Twin Fiends could beat and humiliate me? Did you think, as in the past, that I would fall victim to them and hide in the corner of my room?

I stripped the face mask from the unconscious First Fiend.

Scriiiitch.

And revealed the face of a middle-aged man beneath.

“Who are you?”

I deliberately tried to reveal his identity to gauge my brother’s reaction.

The First Fiend only glared at me with resentful eyes and said nothing.

“I’ll cut off your ankles too.”

When I raised my sword to sever his ankles, the bastard shouted.

“…We are the Yunnan Twin Fiends.”

Thud!

At that moment, the First Fiend’s head exploded. Golden light settled around the flowing blood and then vanished. It was Mabul who had killed him.

Mabul’s eyes flickered with golden light, and an immense energy pressed down on the surroundings. This was not the Mabul who argued with the Wind Heaven Cult Leader. He exuded a fiercer spirit than anyone and spoke coldly.

“You asked what I was expecting? I wanted to show your brother reality clearly. Your competitor is someone who can cut down the Yunnan Twin Fiends in a single move. I wanted to show him you cutting them down with the Demonic Sword that the Cult Leader did not give him. This is the reality you now face.”

He was not speaking to me. He was speaking to my brother.

This man, Mabul! He is certainly a wicked man, but his feelings for my brother are genuine.

My gaze turned to Geom Muyang. Blood splattered across his face was streaming down, just as it was on mine.

Geom Muyang asked me in a calm tone.

“Are you hurt?”

“Thanks to you.”

“Seeing your martial arts improve like this makes me glad. It is a good thing for the main cult as well.”

“My martial arts have improved, but your acting has improved, Brother.”

“What do you mean?”

“You are not the type to associate with just anyone, are you?”

“If you are asking whether I knew they were the Yunnan Twin Fiends, I did not. See you tomorrow morning when we greet Father.”

And so my brother and Mabul left that place as well. Seeing him maintain his composure even in this situation, I acknowledged my brother. I could understand why the Blood Heaven Demon Blade had supported him in the past. It made sense.

I walked toward the corpses of the Yunnan Twin Fiends sprawled on the ground. I had killed them easily because I did not want to imbue their deaths with any meaning. Allowing them so much as a single last word was too good for such men. The cold rice served in the dungeon was too good for them. Killing them like bugs and erasing their very existence was the greatest punishment.

I searched the two corpses and found promissory notes. They each carried one note worth one hundred thousand nyang.

“Remember this. Two hundred thousand nyang is enough to get the Second Young Master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult beaten.”

Gowol smiled faintly at my jest. I handed him the notes.

“Use them for the work you are undertaking. Or do you think it is dirty money that came from their pockets?”

“Money bears no name. Its worth is determined by how it is used.”

Gowol does not make me regret [recruiting him]. That is why I went to such lengths to bring him here as my strategist.

“I will dispose of the corpses. Please go inside and wash off the blood first.”

I looked up at the sky and spoke as if to myself.

“With my brother’s return, blood rain falls from the heavens at once.”

Chapter 88: From the Moment the Umbilical Cord Was Cut.
-------------------------------------------------------
Geom Muyang, having washed off the blood, stood before the mirror while Sibi helped him dress in fresh clothes.

Beneath his bare chest, he wore a thin inner armor capable of deflecting blades. Over it, he layered another, slightly thicker inner armor. At least toward Mabul, he openly displayed his obsessive fear of death.

Mabul sat in a chair, watching him.

He had every right to fly into a rage over recruiting the Yunnan Twin Demons, yet Geom Muyang said nothing. Mabul could tell that he was furious.

“The Second Young Lord….”

He had originally planned to begin with the words, “You have changed too much.” But thinking it too obvious, he changed his words.

“…What kind of person is he?”

Geom Muyang answered from within the mirror.

“I thought I knew my younger brother well, but seeing him earlier, I am no longer sure. He was not this kind of child before…”

“You must forget the past!”

As Mabul suddenly raised his voice, Sibi, who had been adjusting his collar, scratched Geom Muyang’s neck with her fingernail.

“Ah! Please forgive me, spare my life!”

Sibi flopped down on the spot, begging for forgiveness. The atmosphere turned cold in an instant.

“Why would I kill you over a trifle like this?”

Geom Muyang spoke softly, but Mabul cut in coldly.

“But must you not kill her? You would let a servant live after she wounded the precious body of one destined to become the Heavenly Demon? How ridiculous would that look to others?”

Sibi pleaded desperately.

“Please! Spare me!”

Geom Muyang raised her to her feet.

“Everyone makes mistakes. Now leave us.”

“Thank you, my lord. Truly, thank you.”

Sibi left the room.

“You must not show others that you forgive your subordinates’ mistakes so easily. You ought to punish her, if only to set an example.”

Then Geom Muyang asked abruptly.

“Did you ask what kind of person my younger brother is?”

“Yes, I did.”

“He is the kind of child who, were the same thing to happen, would buy a startled Sibi a meal. He has had a warm heart since he was young.”

“That does not mean you should lower your guard.”

“I did not say it to suggest lowering our guard. Didn’t you see Mugeugi’s people earlier?”

The gaze in the mirror that had been directed at Mabul turned toward Geom Muyang himself.

“To be honest, I was a bit envious.”

Mabul sometimes found this Geom Muyang unpredictable.

He handled affairs with brilliance and cold reason, yet would reveal his inner thoughts so abruptly.

While Mabul was contemplating how to react, Geom Muyang walked over and sat facing him. He poured cold tea into his cup and drank.

“What is the Demon Venerable so worried about?”

“The Second Young Lord’s strength is one thing, but above all, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon is a cunning old man. The fact that he chose the Second Young Lord means there is clearly good reason for it. Whether because he buys Sibi meals, or because of something else we do not know.”

Then Geom Muyang said something unexpected.

“I believe the answer is Father.”

At those words, Mabul was startled.

“What do you mean by that?”

“While I was away, Father pushed Mugeugi forward. He bestowed the Black Demon Sword upon him and seated him in the position of Yellow Heaven Pavilion Lord. He is testing how I will deal with an empowered Mugeugi. I believe it is the final test to become the successor.”

For a moment, it seemed plausible, but Mabul soon shook his head.

“That cannot be. In the process of the Second Young Lord’s rise, the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable died. Though they say the cause was qi deviation, there are many suspicious points.”

At first, he had believed the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader’s claim that it was not Geom Mugeuk’s doing, but now he did not.

“It may have been the Second Young Lord’s doing. And yet you think it is the Cult Leader’s test? The Cult Leader cherished the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable as a main pillar of our cult.”

“Father likely did not expect things to go that far. Mugeugi possesses capabilities that exceed Father’s expectations.”

“Then did the Second Young Lord truly kill the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable?”

Then Geom Muyang asked a question instead of answering.

“Could the Demon Venerable have killed the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable?”

“I… of course I could kill him!”

The brief hesitation revealed the caliber of the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable. Even Mabul had not been confident against that opponent.

“Correct. The Demon Venerable could have killed him. But it would not have been easy.”

After saving Mabul’s pride, Geom Muyang continued with what he had originally intended to say.

“Neither I nor Mugeugi could kill the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable with our current martial arts prowess. However, with one person’s help, it would be possible.”

“Who would that be?”

As if telling him to figure it out himself, Geom Muyang did not answer. After pondering for a moment, Mabul started and cried out.

“Surely not? The Heavenly Wind Cult Leader?”

“Correct. With his help, one could have killed the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable.”

Mabul thought it possible, yet one question remained.

“Why would the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader do that?”

“To advance into the Central Plains. In the end, did he not take the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable’s disciple as his own disciple?”

“Ah!”

Mabul had never harbored such suspicion until now. The Heavenly Wind Cult Leader had been behind it from the start?

“And after that, he brazenly came to examine the corpse? Is that what you are saying?”

Mabul’s relationship with the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader had soured of late.

Suspicion and rage clouded his objective judgment.

“The Heavenly Wind Cult Leader knew that the Demon Venerables would summon him. It was also the only way he could rightfully enter the main cult.”

This was the final doubt holding Mabul back.

“But the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader lacks the guts for such a thing. If it were discovered that he killed the Soul-Devouring Demon Venerable, the entire Heavenly Wind Cult would be annihilated. He would not do that.”

“There is something Father always told me since I was young. Do not presume that you know someone.”

Mabul was bewildered. The Heavenly Wind Cult Leader he knew was not a man capable of such a great crime. Yet Geom Muyang’s words fit perfectly. And another reason was added.

“Is it not obvious just from seeing the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader’s military advisor go to Mugeugi? As you said, would that meticulous Blood Heaven Blade Demon have simply joined hands with him? He must have done so because there was such a background.”

If this were true?

“Damn it! To think I went and brought that ill omen into our midst with my own two feet!”

“Do not blame yourself. Rather, I intend to turn this situation around and seize control of the Demon Venerables.”

“What if the situation proves unfavorable?”

Geom Muyang mentioned without concern the words Mabul could not bring himself to speak.

“Whether I will kill Mugeuk? No, I will not kill him. How would Father look upon an elder brother who kills his younger brother?”

Mabul realized it. The reason he would not kill Geom Mugeuk was not because he was his younger brother, but because their father was watching.

“Then what do you intend to do?”

“I will draw everyone who has gathered around Mugeuk over to my side. Mugeuk has strong pride; if his own people turn their backs on him, he will leave the cult of his own accord. You saw them earlier, did you not? The people who left without watching Mugeuk’s duel. I will make those people truly leave Mugeuk. Let us see if he can still laugh and joke then.”

“Leaving the Second Young Lord alive will become a future threat.”

“Even if he leaves that way, should a crisis befall our cult, he will come to save us, not to kill us. That is his greatest weakness.”

Geom Muyang stood up again and walked to the mirror. Gazing at himself in the mirror, he spoke calmly.

“The seat of the Grand Preceptor of the Heavenly Demon Hall is the firmest seat in this martial world. It is not a seat that a softling like Mugeuk can occupy. Nor should he.”

* * *

The next morning, when I arrived at the Heavenly Demon Hall, I met my older brother at the entrance.

“It has been a while since we entered the Heavenly Demon Hall side by side like this.”

“Indeed.”

We walked together.

“Nervous?”

“Not really.”

But my brother was nervous.

He always stiffened up especially before our father. Since he was young, my brother had always wanted to impress Father.

Once, when we were children, my brother had heard the words “our firstborn” from Father just once, and he had boasted about it for years, not days.

If I was currently captivated by Hwamugi, then my brother was captivated by Father.

My brother and I entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

There, our father and Chief Strategist Sima Myeong were together. We walked side by side along the Path of Blood. The Demon statues erected all around watched my brother’s return—our entrance—with cold yet peculiar eyes.

“Father, have you been well?”

My brother performed a deep bow before Father.

“You have returned well.”

I observed Father’s expression. Joy was visible on his face toward my brother. On the other hand, my brother was nervous. I could feel him trembling.

“You seem pleased that your firstborn has returned.”

Father made no reply to my words. The Father from when we walked together in Magachon and the Father now felt like different people.

I understand a Father who has become sterner.

Before my regression, around this time, Father had already been thinking of my brother as the successor. Though I had shaken Father’s heart through this regression, the one who had first entered that heart was my brother.

If I failed to understand Father’s heart toward my brother, I could never win this fight. And I must not forget: my ultimate enemy is neither Father nor my brother.

“How did your affairs go?”

To Father’s question, my brother answered confidently.

“I have cleared every trial, up to the final gate.”

Father’s expression brightened at those words. My brother had not been assigned trivial duties in the frontier.

The Demonic Nine Walls.

The Demonic Nine Walls was a secret training ground of the main cult hidden in the frontier. Father had opened it to my brother. Like the Lesser Heavenly Cave, the Demonic Nine Walls could only be entered with the permission of the Heavenly Demon. Passing the Demonic Nine Walls was truly no easy feat. It was a gate one level higher than the Lesser Heavenly Cave that I had previously cleared. However, one could give up halfway and exit the Demonic Nine Walls. It could be considered a safer, yet more difficult trial.

Sima Myeong was also greatly pleased.

“My congratulations, First Young Lord. To pass the Demonic Nine Walls… it seems blood truly cannot be deceived.”

My brother was also one who inherited Father’s blood. In terms of martial arts talent, he was second to none.

I asked, pretending ignorance.

“What did you pass?”

Then Sima Myeong said with a smile.

“The First Young Lord has passed the Demonic Nine Walls.”

I embraced my brother tightly.

“Amazing, Brother!”

My action startled Father and Sima Myeong. My brother, having already experienced my change, accepted my congratulations readily.

“Thank you.”

“Then you’ve also achieved the grand completion of the Flying Heaven Sword Technique?”

Had we been alone, he would not have answered, but since Father was present, my brother answered honestly. Of course, he did not speak to me; he spoke to Father.

“Father, in the process of clearing the Demonic Nine Walls, I also achieved the grand completion of the Flying Heaven Sword Technique.”

“Congratulations.”

“It is all thanks to Father’s teachings. Thank you, Father.”

Father’s gaze turned toward my brother and me.

What was Father thinking right now? Even in this moment, was he scoring who would be more suitable as successor?

Father made no mention of what I had said. A competition of good faith? Had it been just the two of us, he might have called it cheap sentimentality worthy of a death wish.

The reason I said it anyway was to tell Father. That I would try to carry my brother with me.

“You may both return and rest.”

My brother and I paid our respects to Father and walked out of the Heavenly Demon Hall along the Path of Blood.

At the entrance, I asked.

“Why do you want to become the Heavenly Demon, Brother?”

Then my brother said abruptly.

“Because if I don’t become the Heavenly Demon, I’ll die.”

I had expected a different answer from my brother; it was truly surprising.

“Do you mean you’ll die if you lose the succession struggle?”

“Until now, they have all died.”

“Do we really have to kill each other? The Geom Muyang and Geom Mugeuk brothers, displaying a brotherly love that will remain immortal in the history of the Demonic Cult.”

“Do you think the other brothers lacked such feelings? In the end… all humans betray one another.”

“But we don’t have to?”

At my words, Geom Muyang scoffed and began walking first.

To my brother, who was walking ahead, I shouted.

“I meant it. About competing in good faith.”

My brother stopped walking, turned back to look at me, and said.

“Then you should have first asked if there is such a thing as ‘good faith’ in my heart. Do you have it?”

“I do.”

“Then I start this fight at an advantage.”

Watching my brother’s back as he resumed walking, I finally scoffed.

Right, as if this would be easy. Making someone who bears me goodwill into my person is already such a difficult task.

‘Still, Brother, we must pull through. The dagger that will stab us in the back is not brotherly love, but Hwamugi.’

Chapter 89: I'm Not a Fish You've Caught
----------------------------------------
After parting with my brother, I went to see Ian.

I do not know why Ian suddenly came to mind, but perhaps I wanted to see the person who understood me best.

Ian was training hard today as well.

Ian, who did nothing but train every day.

The sight of her overlapped with the me who had gathered materials for the great ritual before my regression. The me who had never stopped, aiming only for a single goal.

After training for quite some time, she belatedly realized I had arrived.

“Young Master! When did you get here?”

“A while ago.”

“What if you had been an assassin?”

“I suppose I would have died about twenty times?”

“Ahhh!”

I drew my sword and stepped in front of her.

“Let’s reduce those twenty times to nineteen.”

Realizing that I was going to demonstrate the Flying Heaven Sword Art, Ian’s eyes sparkled.

“Thank you!”

It had been a long time since I had shown her a demonstration. I did not deliberately lower my realm, but displayed it as it was, at the realm of the Eleventh Star.

There was a great difference between when I had been at the grand completion of the Tenth Star and now. Of course, she would not be able to feel it as keenly as I did. Her achievement would depend on how much of that difference she could perceive.

When I finished the forms from beginning to end, Ian stood there, sunk deep in thought.

I could tell. At some point, Ian had fallen into a state of selflessness.

So as not to disturb her, I even stilled my breathing and quietly sat down cross-legged. There was no telling when she would emerge from that state, and I could not simply wait around idly.

I began training in the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art. Perhaps our training compatibility was good; last time, I had been the one to fall into a state of selflessness, and today it was her.

When a full six double-hours had passed, I sensed her coming out of it.

Congratulations, Ian.

In my heart, I offered her my congratulations. Falling into a state of selflessness for six double-hours was not something that came easily in a martial artist’s life.

Seeing that the surroundings had grown pitch-black, Ian looked bewildered.

“Young Master?”

“How was it? Your first time in a state of selflessness.”

“A state of selflessness? Don’t tell me I was in one?”

“You were.”

“I thought I was only dazed for a moment.”

“Well, if you think about the length of our lives, six double-hours is only a moment.”

“Six double-hours passed?”

Ian was startled. In a state of selflessness, the moment right after awakening was always the most important.

“Perform the Flying Heaven Sword Art at once.”

“Yes.”

Ian performed the Flying Heaven Sword Art before me.

It had indeed taken effect. More than merely taken effect. Her Flying Heaven Sword Art had risen to the realm of the Eighth Star.

“How can this be? All I did was watch you perform the forms.”

“How do you think? It was possible because you normally do nothing but train like a bear. Did you think it would be possible just from watching my demonstration?”

“Even so…”

“All the effort you have built up until now melted together in this state of selflessness.”

“Ah!”

I taught her, and she, with her exceptional talent in martial arts, worked herself to the bone. My realm, having reached the Eleventh Star, gave her deeper inspiration, and most decisive of all, she spent six double-hours in a state of selflessness. It was a miraculous achievement created by all of these things coming together.

“There is no need to be too pleased. As you know, progress in martial arts is fast at the beginning and slows the farther you go. Think of the Flying Heaven Sword Art as truly beginning from now on. Let alone grand completion, it may take you a lifetime just to go from the Eighth Star to the Ninth.”

“Even if it takes my whole life, I will definitely reach grand completion. Heaven has granted me such a precious connection; if I fail to reach it, I would be too pathetic, and it would be too unfair.”

At the same time, the moment to perform the Divine Poison Purification Art for her had taken another step closer.

In order to perform the Divine Poison Purification Art safely without side effects, the realm of my own martial learning was important.

But afterward, her realm would be important. The thought of the greatest beauty under heaven being weak in martial arts was enough to conjure up terrible things awaiting her.

The proper time I had in mind was to perform the Divine Poison Purification Art once she reached the Ninth Star. With the Flying Heaven Sword Art at the Ninth Star, she would be able to protect herself well enough wherever she went.

But with her achievement today, I thought that moment might come much sooner than I had expected.

“Instead of dying twenty times, now you’d die about twice.”

Overcome with emotion, Ian bowed deeply on the spot.

“Thank you, Young Master.”

“No need for that between us.”

“What exactly is between us? I was only a mere guard warrior. Why do you treat me so well?”

Because you threw yourself forward to protect me, and that was how this entire story could begin.

“Ian.”

“Yes.”

“I was troubled after meeting my brother and coming back. Do you know who came to mind then? You. That is why I treat you well.”

She looked at me with an emotional expression.

“And you have used two out of your five questions.”

We had agreed that she could only ask why I treated her well five times.

“You have three left now.”

“I will use them sparingly.”

“Now that your Flying Heaven Sword Art has reached the Eighth Star, you must use it carefully. Its power will be completely different from before.”

“Yes, Young Master.”

“Ian. This may not suit you when you must be wildly excited, but… do not live a life where you do nothing but train in martial arts.”

“Yes!”

Ian nodded with a bright smile.

Ian, this is why.

Because you are someone who nods beside me like this.

Even though you are clever and intelligent, and surely have all your own thoughts, you are someone who smiles like this and nods at my words.

Because you are the kind of person one hopes to have at least one of in life.

Because you are someone I could give a different answer to even if you asked not four times, but a hundred.

Stay strong, Ian. Let’s make it to the Ninth Star!

* * *

When I returned after meeting Ian, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was waiting for me near my residence, his Heaven-Extinguishing Great Saber thrust into the ground.

“Why are you here?”

“I was recalling the day I first met you here.”

“Now that my brother has returned, do you want to turn everything back?”

“I thought I should at least show you that I am not a fish you have already caught.”

He pulled out the Heaven-Extinguishing Great Saber and drew a long line across the ground.

“When I first met you, it was here.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon drew a line at the very beginning. That day when he first came to find me, he had drawn a line on the ground just like this, poking me in the ribs all the while.

“And now, it is here.”

He drew a new line a little past the halfway point.

“Oh! It is past halfway!”

“Barely past. Is that something to be so happy about?”

“At this much, it is enough.”

“Enough?”

“Whether friend or foe, you are a friend; whether life or death, it would be life. Besides, does it not become burdensome when someone is too close? I am not confident I can take care of that person until the end, yet that person keeps crossing the line and coming over to my side.”

I drew the Black Demon Sword and made a mark near the end of the line.

“To come this far, would one not have to be my child? Well, my father would probably be around here.”

This time, I drew another line at a point that did not even reach the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s mark.

“How are you so good with words? You prepare them all night before coming out, do you not? You spend the whole night memorizing what to say and how to say it today, do you not?”

“Please say things like that when I come to visit you. Not on a day when you have suddenly come to see me, Senior.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head as if he could not win. The reason he had come to find me was my brother.

“I came because there is something I must tell you about the Eldest Young Master.”

“Please speak.”

“The first Demon Venerable the Eldest Young Master meets will probably be the Extreme Evil Young Demon. Prepare accordingly.”

In fact, his judgment was accurate. In the past, the three Demon Venerables who had supported my brother most strongly were the Demon Buddha, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

In the life before my regression, my brother committed many cruel acts at the point in the future when he was chosen as successor. It was not because of my brother’s decisions alone. The Demon Buddha, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and the Extreme Evil Young Demon, who had attached themselves to my brother at the time, had greatly influenced him.

Would my brother’s fate change as well, like the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s or the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s? For that to happen, the fates of the Demon Buddha and the Extreme Evil Young Demon would have to change too, would they not?

“After the Demon Buddha, the person closest to him will become the Extreme Evil Young Demon.”

“Originally, it was me, but now the Extreme Evil Young Demon will take that place. I expect the Eldest Young Master will meet all the other Demon Venerables except me. Only the order will differ.”

I silently nodded.

“The Extreme Evil Young Demon is no ordinary figure either.”

“I know.”

How else would the words extreme evil be attached to his epithet?

“You must prepare. Do you intend to simply stay as you are?”

“Yes.”

“You are going to leave it alone?”

“So long as I have you and Senior Sword Venerable, that is enough for me.”

“That is pleasant to hear, but if five Demon Venerables side with the Eldest Young Master, it will be difficult to deal with.”

“Why five, and not six?”

“Since the Cult Leader Pungcheon has come, has the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable not naturally become one of ours?”

“Cheongseon is still young and ambitious, so we cannot necessarily say that. In fact, if my brother tries to win her over, she is the one most easily won over.”
