She might act like an ally and then turn her back on us. My brother might use her as a spy. There were many possibilities.

“It does not matter even if the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable is on the other side. If my brother wins her over, we might instead be able to make use of her at a decisive moment.”

“You had thought that far?”

“I have to. Of course, I intend to prevent that from happening if possible.”

Not because of Cheongseon, but because of Cult Leader Pungcheon. I also wanted to see the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable smoothly become his disciple and grow well.

“Then the opponent has six. No matter how capable you are, this will be difficult. No, I will have a hard time, so it will not do. We must definitely draw at least one of the Demon Venerables over to our side.”

“Who do you mean?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon parted his lips as if to name someone, then stopped.

“That is for the clever you to decide.”

There must be someone he thought could be on our side, but he seemed to believe it would not be right for him to say it. It also meant he trusted my judgment.

“Move quickly before the Eldest Young Master wins over all the Demon Venerables.”

“My brother will meet someone else before the Extreme Evil Young Demon.”

“Who else?”

I stared straight at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Me?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon blinked, his expression asking what nonsense that was.

“Me?”

“Yes. My brother will go to see you first, Senior.”

“That cannot be. Does he not know that I have sided with you? He may go to all the other Demon Venerables, but he will not come to me.”

“No. Even if he does not go to the other Demon Venerables, he will surely come to you. And after you, it will be Cult Leader Pungcheon.”

Even with my certainty, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon tilted his head as if he could not believe it.

“What is the reason you think so?”

“My brother will want to show Father that the reason you came to me was only because he had been absent. He will think that taking you back from me will earn him more points with Father than bringing all the other Demon Venerables under him.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon slowly nodded. Seeing as he did not say that was impossible, it seemed he thought it could happen.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon stared at me and asked,

“Then are you not anxious? Afraid that I might go back to the Eldest Young Master?”

After a brief pause, I said,

“I am anxious.”

“Truly?”

With a face devoid of any playfulness, I said to him,

“Of course. You saw my brother that day, did you not? When he gave the gift to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. The gift itself was not what was impressive; the meticulousness was. And he will try to give you something even more remarkable.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded. He, too, knew enough about my brother.

“I would like you not to go, Senior. No matter what good offer my brother makes, please be sure to tell me. I will hold onto you with a better offer.”

“You are not going to tell me to stay out of loyalty?”

“We are not twenty-year-old children, are we?”

“You are, are you not?”

“Ah, that is true. But I am an old man in a child’s body, you see.”

I smiled awkwardly.

“At the very least, he will not be able to take you away.”

“Why?”

“Because even if I have to stake everything I have, I will make you a better offer. And if by any chance he finds a weakness and threatens you with it, you must tell me that as well.”

“Why that too?”

“Because I will definitely resolve it for you. If you ask whether I can, I believe I have shown you enough by now. If you do not want to show me that weakness, then please think of us as people who have seen everything there is to see of each other.”

After gazing at me in silence for a moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon withdrew the Heaven-Extinguishing Great Saber that had been deeply embedded in the ground and rose.

“Let us wait and see. Whether, as you say, he comes to me first.”

As he was about to leave, he glanced down at the line drawn on the ground.

“And where this line will move.”

Chapter 90: A Relationship Worse Than Not Being Close.
------------------------------------------------------
Truth be told, even when he said, “Just you wait and see,” Hyeolcheon Doma had been deeply skeptical.

That Geom Muyang would come to him first? Without first securing the other Demon Lords? It was unthinkable.

But just as if he had colluded with Geom Mugeuk, that very night, Geom Muyang came to find him.

“What brings you here?”

At that, Geom Muyang held up the wine bottle he had brought.

“I wished to share a drink with you, Elder. If my memory serves, you favored this wine, did you not?”

“You remember remarkably well. Come, sit here.”

Hyeolcheon Doma did not invite him inside, but instead sat facing him on a flat rock set out in the courtyard.

“It is cool here. Let us drink.”

“Very well.”

Hyeolcheon Doma had Sibi bring wine cups and simple refreshments.

He had met Geom Muyang on the day of his return, but then had not been a situation to observe one another closely.

Only now did Hyeolcheon Doma calmly take stock of Geom Muyang’s bearing. As expected, his demeanor was different from when he had left.

“I hear you have crossed the Demon Path Barrier?”

“Thanks to your concern, I passed through.”

“My congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

“The Cult Leader must have been pleased.”

“He was.”

Hyeolcheon Doma asked this and that about the Demon Path Barrier, and Geom Muyang answered earnestly to the best of his ability. Even in the past, the two had often conversed about martial arts.

Geom Muyang enjoyed speaking of martial arts with Hyeolcheon Doma. Strangely, they understood each other well.

When the wine was nearly gone, Hyeolcheon Doma spoke first.

“You have come because there is something you wish to ask, I suppose. Ask freely, then.”

“Yes. Then I shall ask directly, like a man, without beating around the bush. Why did you choose my younger brother? I believe there must have been a cause or a reason.”

“Of course there was.”

Hyeolcheon Doma pondered for a moment what to say, and then answered thus:

“Your younger brother possesses the power to draw people’s hearts.”

Geom Muyang nodded silently.

“The power to draw hearts…”

He had anticipated various answers, and had thought this sort of reply was possible. Yet hearing it directly, Geom Muyang was overcome with both emptiness and bitterness.

Never in his life had anyone ever told him that he was someone who drew others’ hearts.

“I have never once felt such power from Mugeuk. Ah, I do not say this to belittle Mugeuk. I am simply curious what sort of feeling it is.”

“Of course you would not have felt it. There would have been no reason for the Second Young Lord to draw your heart, and neither did you have the leeway to accept your younger brother’s heart, did you?”

“Yes, that was so.”

Hyeolcheon Doma poured wine for Geom Muyang, who nodded readily.

“You are an excellent martial artist. You certainly possess the qualities to become the Heavenly Demon.”

Hyeolcheon Doma truly thought so. Had Geom Mugeuk not charged up like a madman, he would have continued to support Geom Muyang.

“And yet you chose my younger brother?”

“Sometimes in life, people are swept away by a sudden wind.”

“I wish I were in the place where that wind blows. I wish to walk this path together with you, Elder.”

The light shining in his eyes as he looked at Hyeolcheon Doma was bright. At least in this moment, Geom Muyang was speaking from the heart.

“Are there not many other Demon Lords?”

“No. I absolutely need you, Elder.”

“The reason?”

Although Mabul followed him like a right arm, in the depths of Geom Muyang’s heart, he liked Hyeolcheon Doma more.

“Because… you are someone absolutely necessary to me.”

He offered no other reason whatsoever.

Hyeolcheon Doma thought that if he had not heard from Geom Mugeuk that Geom Muyang would come, he might perhaps have been moved by this earnestness.

“May I offer you a few proposals, Elder?”

“Speak.”

“If I become the Heavenly Demon, I will seat you in the position of First Demon Lord.”

“First Demon Lord?”

“Yes. I will establish the highest seat among the Demon Lords recognized even within the Heavenly Demon Temple. Furthermore, I will expand the scale of Namdojong to three times what it is now. I will remove the limit on the number of Dao Ghosts and drastically increase support for Namdojong. Moreover, I will specially open the Heavenly Demon Library to you. You may go and peruse it whenever you wish.”

Geom Muyang knew that Hyeolcheon Doma loved books. The Heavenly Demon Library did not contain only martial arts secret manuals. There were also many volumes of poetry and collected literary works that were difficult to obtain in the secular world.

Considering fairness with the other Demon Lords, it was truly a groundbreaking proposal.

Of course, Hyeolcheon Doma was not so naive as to believe those words at face value.

Later, after becoming the Heavenly Demon, what if he said, “The opposition from the other Demon Lords is more severe than expected, so Lord Doma, please yield a little”?

Moreover, by then he would be even older than he was now, a toothless tiger.

“Wouldn’t Mabul feel slighted if he heard this? I’m sure he believes he will become the First Demon Lord.”

As if naturally expecting this reaction, Geom Muyang did not fluster in the slightest.

“It cannot be helped. Though I respect and like Elder Mabul, I believe the one suited for that seat is Elder Doma. You may convey these very words to Elder Mabul.”

He seemed to have faith that Hyeolcheon Doma would never tell Mabul such words. Where did that confidence come from? Did he think of him as such a noble person?

“I shall consider your proposal.”

“Yes, thank you.”

They toasted their final cups, and Geom Muyang rose.

As he was about to leave, Geom Muyang added one last thing.

“Elder, I will definitely become the Heavenly Demon.”

After Geom Muyang disappeared, Hyeolcheon Doma took something from his mouth. It was the blood poison wine Geom Mugeuk had given him as a gift. He had not fully trusted Geom Muyang, who had brought the alcohol.

Hyeolcheon Doma muttered to himself.

“…Yes, there was a time when I was certain of that too.”

* * *

When Geom Muyang returned, Mabul was waiting for him.

“Did you happen to visit Hyeolcheon Doma?”

“The main cult is still the same. There are no secrets.”

“You marched there boldly in broad daylight; what talk of secrets is that? Just why did you do it?”

“I went to win him over.”

“And?”

“He said he would think about it.”

“He is a traitor.”

Mabul deliberately used such a strong term.

“Someone whose heart has turned once will turn again. You know that, do you not?”

Then Geom Muyang said something surprising.

“As he drank, he was carrying blood poison wine.”

“That madman!”

Unlike Mabul, who flared up in anger, Geom Muyang was composed.

“It did not seem he was trying to hide it. It was so like Elder Doma—an attitude of saying, I do not trust you.”

“Have nothing more to do with him.”

Something even more surprising came from Geom Muyang’s mouth.

“Ah, and I proposed to Elder Doma that I would give him the seat of First Demon Lord.”

For a moment, Mabul was startled. Caught off guard by such an unexpected remark, he froze briefly.

He had believed the seat of First Demon Lord would naturally be his.

So Mabul waited for the next words. Words saying it was merely said to win him over, so not to worry.

But Geom Muyang did not say those words.

*So what? Does that mean you will really give it?*

Mabul unknowingly bit down hard on his molars. The way a person clenches their teeth—one can tell if they observe their opponent closely. How could a master like Geom Muyang not know? Yet Geom Muyang pretended not to see and showed no reaction.

All manner of thoughts flashed through Mabul’s mind.

Was it a mistake? Or indifference? Or did he know and was doing it intentionally?

He wondered if this was perhaps punishment for bringing in the Yunnan Twin Devils this time.

There had been similar incidents in the past. In such moments, what was most agonizing was that Geom Muyang was a person more clever and intelligent than anyone.

Therefore, Mabul was always thrown into confusion by the thought that it might not simply be a mistake, but an act with some intention behind it.

And above all, the worst thing was this:

That he could not speak of these feelings.

Had it been anyone else, he would have already asked directly.

Why are you doing this? Are you looking down on me? Please stop doing this.

But those words did not come easily when it came to Geom Muyang. Was it because the first button of their relationship had been fastened wrong? Or had Geom Muyang intentionally erected some invisible wall between them?

The relationship between Geom Muyang and himself was one where only he writhed in pain alone.

A relationship worse than not being close at all…….

In the end, as always, Mabul reconciled it like this.

*Yes, that can happen. He is still young.*

He, the adult, had to endure. The task he had taken upon himself was to make this hot-blooded youth into the Heavenly Demon.

“You must look after our people as well. They are all watching.”

You ought to know that these words came after so many such thoughts!

Could it be that he had read Mabul’s inner mind?

“I do not feel much affection for the other Demon Lords.”

“What do you mean?”

“Do you not and Elder Doma stand up boldly and declare whom you support? Yet the remaining Demon Lords merely watch the situation. They will stick to the advantageous side later? Truly shallow.”

If only Hyeolcheon Doma had been left out of those words, it would have been truly good. No, if he truly intended to deceive me, he would have left him out intentionally. Speaking this honestly must be his way of respecting me.

“Still, they are absolutely necessary.”

“I go forward trusting only you, Elder.”

Mabul and Geom Muyang looked at each other and smiled.

To some extent, Mabul’s mood had eased, but the lump in his heart did not dissolve.

*So who are you going to give the First Demon Lord seat to?*

But in the end, neither Geom Muyang nor Mabul brought up the matter.

* * *

Hyeolcheon Doma came to me with a freshly surprised expression.

“You were right. The First Young Lord truly came to find me. How did you know?”

“This is a secret… I have the ability to see the future.”

“Seeing that you are still a madman, I suppose no one has stolen your mind with a soul-stealing art.”

At Hyeolcheon Doma’s jest, I smiled and said.

“Did I not tell you? My brother has always been that sort of person.”

“This time, I felt something anew quite deeply.”

Hyeolcheon Doma hesitated briefly over the words he was about to speak. But they were words he had intended to say anyway. Indeed, they were words befitting his character to hesitate over.

“I felt anew what kind of person you are to me.”

“!”

I tensed up. It was the first time Hyeolcheon Doma had spoken to me in this manner.

“What do you mean?”

“No matter what temptation the First Young Lord offered, it did not enter my ears. He made a groundbreaking proposal, just as you said. But I did not agree at all.”

In my ears, I heard the sound of the door to his heart opening a little more.

I was grateful to Hyeolcheon Doma. I was so grateful that he told me this fact.

Yes, only when you speak like this do I know. Everyone acts as if they know, but if you do not say it, they do not know.

I immediately drew the Black Demon Sword and drew a long line on the ground. And I drew it a little to the right of the line Hyeolcheon Doma had drawn before.

“Has the heart that cherishes me now reached about here?”

Hyeolcheon Doma did not deny it, but he did not forget to warn me either.

“But you know what will happen if you become arrogant, don’t you?”

“What will happen?”

“You will pay a severe price. Among the goods that come with a price, arrogance is displayed right at the very front.”

Perhaps because he reads many books, Hyeolcheon Doma sometimes uses expressions that do not quite suit him. I truly like this side of him.

“I will keep it firmly in mind.”

“If it is as you say, the First Young Lord will visit the Wind Heaven Cult Leader as well. Is it fine to leave it be?”

“We must leave it.”

“Will that person truly be as steadfast as I am?”

“But over there, are there not two people?”

“Two people? Who? Gowol?”

“The Wind Heaven Cult Leader will surely discuss that matter with Gowol.”

“You trust Gowol?”

“Yes.”

“One should not trust people so easily.”

I believe Hyeolcheon Doma’s wariness contains a bit of jealousy. Since I have no intention of using that jealousy to capture his heart, I meekly followed his words.

“Yes, I will always maintain a wary heart. Please watch over me well too, Elder.”

“I shall. By the way, the First Young Lord is running around stirring things up like this; do you intend to simply stay still?”

“Since he has returned after a long time, I should yield the first move at least once. Let us see how my brother makes his move.”

I rather thought it was a good thing. My brother will put us all on the proving ground.

“Let us see whether the ground hardens after the rain, or whether we will end up rolling naked in a mud pit.”

Chapter 91: Is It That Obvious?
-------------------------------
The Pungcheon Cult Leader sat by the window, looking outside.

In the courtyard, Cheongseon, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, was training in martial arts. After officially becoming the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, the time she spent training had decreased, but even so, her skills were improving day by day. She unquestionably had talent for the Soul-Capturing Art.

As he toyed with the ten-thousand-year cold iron dagger and watched Cheongseon train, the Pungcheon Cult Leader habitually looked back. There stood the empty display cabinets where his sacred relics had once been placed.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a sigh.

When he received the dagger, he had said it would do well enough as a sacred relic, but now the only sacred relic in his room was the ten-thousand-year cold iron dagger in his hand. In a sense, he had exchanged all those sacred relics for this single dagger.

‘No. Let’s stop living a life of looking back.’

He had to turn this emptiness into a sense of release.

In the past, had he ever been able to go out somewhere for even a cup of tea without worrying about his sacred relics?

But now, with this one dagger at his side, he could leave for anywhere. They said one became free only by emptying oneself, and those words truly fit. Yes, this was how he should think.

Meanwhile, Cheongseon finished her training.

“Master, then I shall take my leave for today.”

“You worked hard.”

Cheongseon, who had been about to turn away, came back and brought up the words she had been hesitating over.

“Master.”

“What is it?”

“When will you pass down your true secret techniques to me?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had yet to pass down his secret techniques to her.

“Cheongseon.”

“Yes, Master.”

“Why are you in such a hurry?”

“Because I am weak. Since I am weak, my heart grows impatient on its own.”

She was the one who had killed her senior brother and risen to the seat of Demon Venerable. At first, all the ghost arts practitioners of Seohwanjin had been wild with enthusiasm over her, but now she could feel that popularity slowly cooling. These days, she was tormented by the pressure of having to show the ghost arts practitioners some overwhelming martial art.

On top of that, she also disliked Saujong, who tried to meet her whenever he had the chance. She had liked him so much before, yet the moment she took the seat of Demon Venerable, she came to hate him as if it had all been a lie. Every single thing he did irritated her.

In her heart, she wanted to cut him off cleanly, but fearing he might go around spreading strange rumors, she still met him from time to time.

She had even begun to think that perhaps she might kill him, so she was troubled in many ways.

“What kind of Demon Venerable do you want to become?”

“I do not even have the leisure for such extravagant thoughts. With my current level, how could I possibly sleep?”

“If an ambitious and talented person like you trains hard for about thirty years, you will become me. What do you think? Of your future self.”

For a moment, Cheongseon flinched.

“I think I would have to work ten times harder than I do now.”

Even after saying it, Cheongseon worried that she had made a needless joke, but fortunately, the Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed heartily and took it in good humor.

“Could you not have made it about five times?”

“My apologies. Originally, I was going to say twenty times.”

Since she had already started, she joked once more, and the Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed even louder.

It was the first time since they had met that the two of them had exchanged jokes.

Cheongseon saw a new side of the Pungcheon Cult Leader. She had thought he was simply a rigid, inflexible person. This was unexpected.

“Very well. You have eyes and ears, so you likely have some idea what I went through and what situation I am in.”

He had not told her directly. But she must already know enough. That he had given up the position of cult leader and joined hands with Geom Mugeuk. How, then, did she see him?

“I know full well that, deep down, you think me pathetic.”

“No. You made a choice I could never make.”

“Which makes me even more pathetic, does it not?”

“Which makes me respect you even more.”

“The reason I brought this up is that while martial arts are important, something else is more important.”

“What is that?”

“People. More precisely, the eye to judge people.”

“Yes.”

“You just thought this to yourself, didn’t you? ‘People, my ass.’”

“No, Master.”

“I would have thought the same. So it is all right.”

“Absolutely not.”

When the Pungcheon Cult Leader stared at her, Cheongseon revealed what she truly felt.

“I think that if your martial arts are weak, people are all useless in the end. Because they will all leave you eventually. I am sorry to say this, but for now, I still believe martial arts are more important than people.”

“Yes. I thought the same. Someday, the day will come when you understand what I mean.”

“Yes! I understand.”

Understand what? He had been the same. At that age, no matter who told him anything, it had not entered his ears.

Only after she was bewitched by someone like Geom Mugeuk, only after sacred relics vanished every time she turned around, only after shackles wrapped around her neck and she was dragged all over the place, would she think: Ah, everything my master said back then was right.

Martial arts were important only when killing people. In reality, an eye for people was a hundred times more important. Only then would she realize it.

“I will observe your accomplishments soon and move on to the next stage.”

“Thank you, Master.”

Cheongseon left the place with a pleased smile.

When he first took Cheongseon as his disciple, he had thought of her only as a vicious woman full of ambition. Back then, his mind had been elsewhere, and he had not given much thought to this Cheongseon.

He had wondered whether he could truly grow fond of her, but once he actually began teaching her, a certain affection of its own had started to form.

Especially lately, after being pushed around this way and that by clever people, having someone he could nag a little and act like an elder toward made him feel as if he could breathe again.

But that lasted only a short while.

Not long after she left, a visitor came. It was the Eldest Young Master, Geom Muyang.

“I came because I wished to meet you, Cult Leader. I apologize for not sending word in advance.”

“It is fine. Come in.”

Geom Muyang sat facing the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“That day, things were so hectic that I could not greet you properly.”

“It was the same for me, so do not mind it.”

“I heard that you have become quite close with my younger brother.”

“There were many twists and turns.”

“It must have been a great fortuitous encounter for Mugeuk.”

“In truth, it was not exactly so.”

“What do you mean?”

“Your younger brother is so clever that there was much for me to learn.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke indirectly of his own hardships, but Geom Muyang misunderstood, thinking the Pungcheon Cult Leader was speaking highly of Geom Mugeuk.

“My younger brother is indeed clever.”

“He was the cleverest person I have ever seen.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had said he moved hearts, and the Pungcheon Cult Leader said he was the cleverest person he had ever seen. Clever, yet possessing a charm that moved hearts?

Geom Muyang still did not feel that such praise suited his younger brother.

“By the way, what brings such a busy man here? Are you not the busiest person in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?”

“Please help make that true in reality as well. I am the sort of person who likes being busy.”

Geom Muyang spoke bluntly.

“If you join hands with me, I will grant you the two things you desire most, Cult Leader.”

Hearing him out, the Pungcheon Cult Leader found that they truly were the two things he desired most. And throughout the conversation, he thought of one person.

* * *

The Pungcheon Cult Leader came to see Gowol.

“Where is the Second Young Master?”

“He has not returned yet.”

Perhaps because Geom Mugeuk was absent, the Pungcheon Cult Leader needlessly took out his irritation on Gowol.

“If you stay here alone and lonely, how is it any different from being bound in shackles?”

“You keep saying such things because you have never been bound in shackles. Would you like to try it once?”

“Enough, enough.”

Even with the same sort of joke, he felt more at ease than before.

“Listen. I have one request.”

“Speak.”

“When it is just the two of us, can you not speak comfortably to me?”

Gowol looked at him for a moment, then finally opened his mouth.

“Cult Leader.”

“Yes! That is it!”

“You even gave up the important position of cult leader for my sake, so how could I not grant such a small request? However, there is one thing I am worried about.”

“What is it?”

“You like it now, but what will you do if you come to hate it later? If you come to hate me, this will be the first thought that crosses your mind: ‘This young bastard, daring to speak down to me.’ By then, it will irritate you terribly.”

“By then, our relationship will already be shattered, so what does it matter? I will have lost you anyway.”

For a moment, Gowol’s heart surged. He had truly hated this man a great deal, and yet there was no one who liked him as much as this man did.

“Fine, good. Just do not regret it later.”

“Regret is mine to bear. Do not worry.”

“Why did you come today? Surely not just to say this.”

“I simply came to see you.”

After quietly examining the Pungcheon Cult Leader, Gowol suddenly said,

“The Eldest Young Master came to see you, didn’t he?”

For a moment, the Pungcheon Cult Leader flinched.

“How did you know?”

“I thought he would.”

“This is that, isn’t it? You secretly watched me, found out that way, and now you are pretending to be clever, aren’t you? You people are all frauds, aren’t you?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader wore an expression that said, what else could make me say such things?

“What did the Eldest Young Master offer you?”

“He promised an advance into the Central Plains if I took his side. He said that if the Second Young Master had promised a branch, he would establish a branch altar for me.”

“That is a promise he cannot keep. It is a lie.”

“Why are you so certain?”

“The Eldest Young Master is someone who obeys his father’s words unconditionally. Unlike the Second Young Master, he has no irregularity in him at all. He will follow his father’s will and absolutely refuse to allow the Pungcheon Cult to advance into the Central Plains. If the Eldest Young Master becomes the Heavenly Demon, the Pungcheon Cult will never so much as hang a palm-sized signboard in the Central Plains.”

“You are judging unfairly because you are on the Second Young Master’s side.”

Ignoring the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s words, Gowol asked a question.

“What else did he propose?”

“There was no other proposal.”

At that, Gowol’s gaze toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader narrowed.

“Do not look at me like that. He did not set any other conditions.”

“It shows when you lie.”

“It shows? How?”

Gowol did not tell him. Instead, as if proving those words true, he guessed Geom Muyang’s proposal exactly.

“He used me, didn’t he?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s eyes flew open.

“How did you know?”

“He must have promised to send me back to you. If it was a proposal that could shake you, that would be the only one.”

To be honest, the Pungcheon Cult Leader had been tempted by the Eldest Young Master’s offer.

If he could get Gowol back? It might sound insane, but he had even thought he could give up something as trivial as advancing into the Central Plains.

Seeing how the Eldest Young Master shook the heart of a man who had abandoned even his position as cult leader, he thought the Eldest Young Master was formidable.

“The Eldest Young Master was incredibly clever too.”

“The Eldest Young Master is not a clever man. If he were truly clever, he would not fight the Second Young Master.”

“You have an illness. A disease. Second Young Master fever.”

“So what did you say?”

“Do not worry. I firmly refused.”

“Do not lie. You are probably scheming over how to use the conflict between the two of them to get me back. You came to see me to probe whether there was any way to do it.”

After a brief silence, the Pungcheon Cult Leader asked,

“Does it really all show?”

“Cult Leader. Why do you keep forgetting? You said it clearly with your own mouth, didn’t you? That you could never beat these clever bastards. You say that, so why are you trying to use petty tricks again?”

“That is… because of you.”

“If you do this, you will instead… lose me forever.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a sigh.

“I am sorry. Even knowing it, I acted like a fool again.”

“You must fight with other strengths. Against men who use their heads, you must fight by making some other charm your weapon.”

“What charm do I have?”

“If you had no charm, I would still be bound in shackles.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s eyes trembled faintly.

“You are the only one who says such things to me.”

“No. It is because you only look at me. Because you sit on a throne and look down on others carelessly. Now come out into the real world and show people what kind of person you are, and people who say such things will line up. By then, perhaps you will be the one saying this about me: ‘Ah, those shackles back then? I wonder if he is still living well.’”

“What nonsense.”

The two looked at each other and chuckled.

“Cult Leader, think of me as a campfire. Warm yourself a little distance away. If you keep me too close, you will get burned, and soot will cover you all over.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader nodded silently and said,

“…Just do not remember me as the cult leader I was back then.”

Gowol’s heart also trembled. Even Geom Mugeuk had not stirred such ripples in his heart.

“Cult Leader, starting tonight, recite these words five times before you sleep.”

“What words?”

“Second Young Master to the end. Second Young Master to the end. Now, say it.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader forcibly filled in the remaining three times.

Contrary to his words, in his heart he cried out like this.

‘I envy you, Second Young Master. I envy you, Second Young Master. You bastard, I envy you.’

Still, he thought it was fortunate that Gowol was here. Because today, Gowol felt closer to him than he had when he kept him beside him bound in shackles.

It seemed that even in relying on someone, compatibility existed. To be honest, he even thought he would not mind burning to death in that campfire.

Chapter 92: The Power of Words Is Frightening.
----------------------------------------------
When I returned to my dwelling after finishing my martial arts training, Gowol was reading a book in the room, while the Pungcheon Cult Leader stood in the yard, gazing up at the night sky.

He was quite different from when I had first seen him. He had definitely become somehow calmer.

“When did you arrive?”

“I came to see that precious military advisor of yours, so there’s no need to worry that I may have waited long.”

“Did you enjoy seeing that precious military advisor?”

“I only got scolded.”

“Dear me. That won’t do. How dare that military advisor scold the Cult Leader. I shall give that military advisor a thorough scolding.”

“Don’t say things you don’t mean. That remark of yours is even more annoying.”

I grinned, and he too smiled pleasantly.

“I waited on purpose to see you before I left.”

“What is it?”

“The Eldest Young Master came to see me. I waited because I wanted to tell you directly that I was absolutely unmoved.”

“Ah, I am truly moved.”

“Yes, you should be. Do you know how favorable the terms the Eldest Young Master offered were? I refused them all.”

“Thank you, Cult Leader.”

I didn’t ask what terms had been offered.

“Then I shall be off. They say one shouldn’t linger at times like this, or the emotion is halved.”

“Yes. You’re right. It would have been perfect if you hadn’t said even this much.”

“Is that so?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed awkwardly. I shared a truly deep bond with this person. I felt that we would forge an even deeper bond going forward.

Snap!

With a flick of my fingers, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and I were standing in the middle of a green plain.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled.

“You’ve completed the Space-Time Transference Art!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked around. The trees rising straight up, the grass swaying in the wind, and the winding country road leading to the mountains in the distance.

“Perfect.”

“It is thanks to you having imparted such an excellent martial art.”

“Since the martial art known as the Space-Time Transference Art came into existence, you are probably the first to complete it so quickly. You wouldn’t know. How many long years I trained to create this space…….”

“I was simply lucky.”

“Don’t be humble. Of all things, this is one for which there is truly no need for humility.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader showed that he had grown through the recent tribulations.

He suddenly grabbed my hand.

“Congratulations.”

To his heartfelt congratulations, I too conveyed my sincerity.

“Thank you. At least within this space, Cult Leader, you are my teacher.”

“You really…….”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader could not continue.

“Ah, and there is one thing I would like to ask.”

“Speak.”

“Is it possible to make the time flowing within the Space-Time Transference Art different from real time? What I mean is, can you make it flow slower than outside time?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at me with wide eyes.

“Why do you look at me like that?”

“If it were before, I would have undoubtedly reacted like this: ‘Hey, you brat, does that even make sense?’”

“And now?”

“‘Hey, you brat, does that even make sense!’”

I laughed, and the Pungcheon Cult Leader laughed too before telling me about it.

“I actually had such thoughts before. I even researched it. But it was impossible. I only thought of it several years after completing the space, yet you think of it the moment you complete it.”

This meant there was no secret technique passed down for it. To accomplish this, I would have to research and contemplate it again when my martial realm had risen further.

“I understand. Now then, I shall dispel the space.”

“Before that, wait! Was what you said earlier sincere? That at least here, you consider me your teacher.”

“It was sincere.”

“Then open this space for me occasionally. When else in my life would I get the chance to play the role of your teacher?”

“Yes, I shall do that.”

Seeing the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face brighten, I flicked my fingers with a snap! and we returned to reality.

“Then please take care on your way.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been walking some distance away, suddenly turned back and shouted.

“I’m on the Second Young Master’s side to the end!”

Watching him disappear hurriedly into the darkness as if embarrassed, I snickered.

To think the Pungcheon Cult Leader had such a cute side? But then, it must be because he possessed such a heart that he was able to abandon everything and walk away.

* * *

Mabul was furious.

He could understand Geom Muyang going to see Hyeolcheon Doma earlier. After all, there was an unresolved matter between them.

But that he had gone to see even the Pungcheon Cult Leader was utterly intolerable. He had clearly seen Mabul and the Pungcheon Cult Leader locked in a battle of nerves, yet he had gone to see him anyway.

“Eldest Young Master.”

“Yes, Elder.”

“You met the Pungcheon Cult Leader?”

“Yes. I met him to draw him to our side.”

His answer was so composed that there was no atmosphere in which to get angry. Moreover, his reason was perfectly sound.

“If we draw in the Pungcheon Cult Leader, wouldn’t the Soul-Devouring Demon Lord naturally become our side as well? That is why I met him.”

“What did the Pungcheon Cult Leader say?”

“He said he would think about it.”

Geom Muyang said no more about it.

Mabul pondered how to broach the subject without offending Geom Muyang while conveying his own feelings. In the end, he took a step back and began with an apology.

“Calling the Yunnan Twin Monsters was my mistake.”

“What do you mean?”

“I suppose my thinking was too short-sighted. I should not have drawn such people into the Eldest Young Master’s affairs.”

“I am well aware it stemmed from a heart that cares for me.”

“Thank you for thinking so. As expected, the Young Master is broad-minded.”

“But one thing must be set straight.”

“What do you mean?”

“You said you drew the Yunnan Twin Monsters into my affairs, but strictly speaking, it is not my affair, but yours, Elder.”

Geom Muyang drew a clear line. He was walking the path to become the successor. He wanted nothing to do with trash like the Yunnan Twin Monsters, not even a hair’s breadth of connection.

Mabul wondered if it had to go this far, but he hid such sentiments.

“Ah, I misspoke for a moment. You’re right, the matter of the Yunnan Twin Monsters is solely my affair.”

“Of course, I know well that you called them for my sake. So please don’t worry too much about it.”

Geom Muyang smiled first, and Mabul smiled along.

The conversation ended with smiles, but Mabul’s heart was not refreshed. Something was wrong; not knowing whether it was his problem or Geom Muyang’s made it all the worse.

As Mabul left Geom Muyang’s quarters and returned to his own dwelling, someone was waiting for him.

Surprisingly, it was Geom Mugik.

“Second Young Master?”

“I was waiting because I wished to see you, Elder.”

“Me? Why?”

Mabul was inwardly tense. Wondering if Geom Mugik intended to kill him in a deserted place, he secretly circulated his internal energy. But Geom Mugik’s reason for coming was very unexpected.

“Would you have a drink with me at that tavern over there?”

“A drink with me? And at that tavern?”

On any other day, he would have refused outright, fearing rumors that the two of them were scheming together.

But today’s Mabul was not his usual self.

“Very well. There’s no harm in that.”

If Geom Muyang was going around meeting Hyeolcheon Doma and the Pungcheon Cult Leader, couldn’t he just as well meet Geom Mugik? He rather hoped this would reach Geom Muyang’s ears.

‘Second Young Master, you are truly a lucky person.’

To come looking for me even on a day like this.

“This is the first time I have walked side by side with you like this, Elder.”

“Don’t act like we’re close for no reason.”

A normal person would have walked quietly out of embarrassment, but Geom Mugik did not.

“There won’t be many chances to walk with you like this; if I don’t act close now, when will I? Please forgive my excited behavior just for today.”

“I suppose Doma was also won over like this.”

“Yes, that’s right. He knocked on the door endlessly.”

“The door?”

“The door to the heart, I mean.”

Mabul snorted, then suddenly wondered if Geom Muyang had ever knocked on his door. But he soon composed his mind with cold clarity.

‘Second Young Master, no matter what you do, I am not a monkey that falls from a tree just because it shakes, like Doma.’

The place the two arrived at was the Pungnyu Tavern in Magachon.

Even without Geom Mugik’s introduction, Jo Chunbae recognized Mabul at a glance.

He was not flustered. After receiving the Heavenly Demon as a guest, he had changed. He was confident that even if the Martial Alliance Leader and Vice Alliance Leader came together, he would receive them gladly, thinking it was no big deal.

“Welcome, Chief.”

“A precious guest has arrived, so I ask for your services again today.”

“Yes, I will serve you with all my heart.”

And so Geom Mugik and Mabul sat facing each other at a table on the upper floor.

“This is my regular establishment.”

“I don’t want to know. Why did you ask to see me?”

“There are many reasons, but you won’t believe them all. If I must give a believable one, it is a sort of check.”

“A check?”

“My brother has begun meeting my people. So I wanted to show that I too can meet his people. You, Elder, are the first.”

“What will change just because we meet?”

“I know your heart toward my brother is firm.”

“What do you know?”

“Did you not show it the day my brother returned? I felt deeply then the heart that cares for him.”

As Geom Mugik tried to pour the wine, Mabul snatched the bottle and poured for himself before drinking.

“Don’t speak carelessly, claiming to know me.”

Mabul emptied his cup, and Geom Mugik drank after him.

Again Geom Mugik tried to pour, but Mabul filled his own cup.

“There will be times when it is hard because my brother is selfish. Please look after him well, Demon Lord, and admonish him.”

“You are truly a hypocritical man. Did you think I would be moved by such words?”

“It’s not for that reason; I was cursing my brother behind his back. Calling him a selfish man.”

Mabul praised Geom Muyang as if to show that sowing discord would not work.

“Your brother is a brilliant man.”

“He lacks a bit of humanity.”

“That’s fine. One who will accomplish great things must not be swayed by petty emotions.”

“He will sometimes slight you as well.”

“That’s fine. He is one who will become the Heavenly Demon someday—what does it matter if he slights me?”

“He will break important promises as well.”

“The words and actions of the Heavenly Demon are the law itself. Important or unimportant, promise or not—all that is for the Heavenly Demon to decide.”

“You are truly impregnable. I envy my brother for having you on his side. I will drink this wine with a heart of respect for you, Elder.”

Geom Mugik emptied his cup.

Mabul curled one corner of his mouth and sneered.

“I acknowledge that the Second Young Master has a good hand for dealing with people. Still, you won’t be able to win me over. I am a different person from Doma or the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

“I know. As I told you earlier, I never had any intention of making you my person.”

“Then why do this?”

“I wanted to know what kind of person you are. I was curious what kind of person is watching over my brother from beside him. And I have reached a conclusion.”

“And that is?”

Mabul was curious how the Second Young Master would evaluate him.

“If my brother hurts your feelings, he will be the biggest fool in our sect.”

Mabul snorted and emptied his cup.

Geom Mugik tried to pour again. This time, Mabul did not snatch the bottle.

As he poured, Geom Mugik spoke.

“When I see you, Elder, I think of a small giant. You are greater than anyone.”

The power of words is truly frightening. Even knowing full well it was flattery, he couldn’t stop himself from feeling pleased. Especially the expression “small giant” suited his fancy very well. He had thought of himself that way before, but no one had ever called him that.

Lest his inner thoughts be discovered, Mabul glared at Geom Mugik with an even more displeased expression.

Whether or not he knew that heart, Geom Mugik threw out an unexpected remark.

“Please take good care of my brother.”

“Entrusting the Eldest Young Master to me? What is that supposed to mean?”

“I intend to put an end to the killing of one another in the succession struggle in this generation. To do that, we must not treat each other emotionally, but my brother has strong pride, so that adjustment will be difficult. Please help him from beside him, Elder. If it’s you, you should be able to stop my brother even if he runs rampant.”

Mabul, who had been glaring at Geom Mugik, spoke coldly.

“You don’t know me at all. If the Eldest Young Master runs rampant, I will pour oil on the fire. One cannot become the successor with a small explosion, so I will urge him to blow everything away. So if you are looking for a path to survival through me, I tell you to wake from that dream.”

Mabul rose from his seat. As he was about to leave, he spoke one last time.

“If you believe the successor can be chosen peacefully, you are either a fool or a madman.”

“Seeing how all the elders who know me call me a madman, I am probably a madman.”

Mabul, gazing at Geom Mugik, spoke firmly.

“I hate madmen.”

Mabul went straight down to the first floor.

Geom Mugik looked down at Jo Chunbae sitting at the counter.

How much would he freak out later—Mabul passed right by him, yet he was dozing off, nodding away. But then, whether it was Mabul or even Old Man Mabul, being sleepy from fatigue was unavoidable.

Geom Mugik’s gaze, which had been on Jo Chunbae, turned toward outside the tavern.

Far away, a golden radiance was walking toward the horizon. His height was half that of ordinary people, but the shadow stretched out in the sunset glow was large and long.

Chapter 93: Because Villains Understand Each Other.
---------------------------------------------------
Demon Buddha did not go to his own quarters, but went to see Geom Muyang again.

After meeting Geom Mugeuk, he found himself wanting to see Geom Muyang once more. He felt he would only be able to sleep after doing so.

“You have come?”

As always, he was upright and courteous.

Since they had met and parted not long ago, normally he should have asked, Why have you come again? But Geom Muyang did not readily reveal personal curiosity or emotion.

So much so that one might even think he had an obsession with behaving that way in order to become the Heavenly Demon.

Never had that sense of distance felt as pronounced as it did today, but Demon Buddha was not someone who would be shaken by such a thing.

“The Second Young Master came to see me.”

“Mugeuk did?”

“Yes.”

“I see.”

Geom Muyang did not ask what they had spoken about after meeting. He merely nodded once.

“Why do you not ask? Why he came, what we talked about.”

“It is fine. I trust you, Elder.”

Demon Buddha thought he had done well to come see Geom Muyang.

‘Yes, the First Young Master has always been this kind of person. Not delicate, and blunt, but genuine to the core. Yes, I should trust him too. This is how I let it all go.’

Demon Buddha tried to release all the emotions that had piled up within him.

The meeting with the Wind Heaven Cult Leader, the offer to give Blood Heaven Blade Demon the position of First Demon Venerable—he shook them all off.

“Then please rest.”

Demon Buddha rose from his seat. It would have been better if it had ended right there. If only the task of discarding all these remnants of emotion had simply been left to him.

Geom Muyang spoke from behind him.

“The reason I offered the position of First Demon Venerable to Blood Heaven Blade Demon was to win him over.”

Demon Buddha flinched and stopped moving.

‘Why now, of all times? Why now? When I hoped so desperately you would say it, you did not, so why are you saying this after I told you I met Geom Mugeuk?’

Afraid that his sunken inner thoughts would show on his face, Demon Buddha did not turn around right away.

Behind him, Geom Muyang spoke again.

“When I become the Heavenly Demon, the position of First Demon Venerable will naturally be yours, Elder.”

Demon Buddha grew angry.

Until now, he had been treated like a fish already caught, and now that Geom Mugeuk might win him over, Geom Muyang had grown anxious and said this—that was how it felt.

‘Why are you making me think this? You said you trusted me, did you not?’

Even if he tried to understand it as only natural human feeling, being hurt was still being hurt.

‘First Young Master, were you not the one who looked down on your younger brother? Were you not the one who stood proud, saying someone like Mugeuk was nothing?’

But he could not get angry. After all, it was he himself who had tried to show Geom Muyang just how much Geom Mugeuk had changed, even mobilizing the Yunnan Twin Freaks to do it.

That is right. Your younger brother is someone you must be this wary of.

But that did not need to lead to distrust toward him…

Forcing a smile, Demon Buddha turned around.

“I naturally assumed that would be the case.”

“Since I am telling you this after you met my younger brother, I worry you might misunderstand my intentions.”

Ah, he should not have said that either. If he had not, Demon Buddha might have forced himself to think it was mere coincidence, that it was thoughtlessness, that he himself had misunderstood.

“What misunderstanding could I possibly have? I am only grateful that you value me so highly.”

“It is only natural. Please do not say such things.”

“Then I shall take my leave.”

Demon Buddha gave a courteous bow and left Geom Muyang’s quarters.

When he returned to his own quarters, walking alone down a dark path with no moonlight tonight, Demon Buddha sorted his thoughts like this.

‘It is because he is still young. If he were as perfect as the Second Young Master, why would he need me? A First Young Master with weaknesses is better than a crafty Second Young Master. I need only look after him well.’

His loyalty to Geom Muyang shone as brightly as the radiance emanating from his body.

* * *

Ilhwa Sword Venerable came to see me in the office of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

“Here, this is a gift.”

She held out a flowerpot with blossoms in full bloom.

“Thank you. It’s beautiful.”

I placed the flowerpot by the window.

While I was exchanging a few words with her about the flowers, Seo Daeryong entered with tea. Feeling mischievous, I spoke to Seo Daeryong solemnly.

“From now on, whenever I’m not here, that flower is Inspector Seo’s responsibility. It’s a gift specially given by the Sword Venerable, so you know what will happen if you let it die, don’t you?”

If it had been just the two of us, all sorts of gloomy yet amusing remarks would have come pouring out, but because Ilhwa Sword Venerable was there, Seo Daeryong obediently answered yes and left.

They had all gone together when we visited the Wind Heaven Cult Leader before, but even so, Ilhwa Sword Venerable was still far too difficult a person for Seo Daeryong to deal with.

“I hope I have not come when you are busy.”

“Not at all. The inspectors do the work. I’m just slacking off.”

There were far too many documents piled on my desk for that to be convincing.

The Yellow Springs Pavilion did have a lot of work.

No matter how many villains we locked up, more kept appearing. I even wondered whether the number of villains had been fixed from the beginning, and whenever one disappeared, the vacant spot was automatically filled.

But no matter how busy I was, the work of the Yellow Springs Pavilion was just as important as becoming successor.

Though I bore the once-in-a-lifetime task of having to kill Hwa Mugi, at the same time, I was living in this era.

“My elder brother came to see you too, didn’t he, Senior?”

I guessed exactly why she had visited today.

“He did. He came to see me.”

“My brother really is diligent.”

“That is why he is so popular.”

I nodded as if acknowledging it. Though he had treated me cruelly and viciously, the image he showed to outsiders was different.

He did not have the arrogance or complacency of thinking, I am the First Young Master, so of course I should become the successor. In one way or another, he used his head a great deal, worried a great deal, and above all, he was a diligent person.

“I know many people like my brother.”

“The First Young Master is someone well suited to our cult. If things are left to him, there is a sense of stability, as if he will somehow lead it well.”

“What about me?”

“You are truly someone who does not suit us at all. You make people uneasy, wondering where on earth you will lead us. And yet, seeing that you are this popular…”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable paused briefly, then continued.

“It makes me think there is hope for us too.”

“What kind of hope?”

“The hope that the new demonic path you spoke of may actually be established.”

“Thank you for seeing me in a good light.”

“But do not forget. There are still many in our cult who dislike change. There are also many who believe evil is our strength and our essence.”

“I will keep that in mind.”

“In any case, with the First Young Master moving so quickly, are you just going to sit and watch?”

“Other than Elder Blade Demon and you, Senior, I don’t really know the other Demon Venerables well. I don’t know whom I should draw to our side.”

Because I was curious what Ilhwa Sword Venerable was thinking, I pretended not to know anything.

Then she said something unexpected.

“Do you know who voted for Cheongseon when we were selecting the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable, aside from me and Blade Demon? The person Blade Demon persuaded, I mean.”

“I don’t. Elder never told me.”

“Shall I tell you?”

Originally, matters and decisions among the Demon Venerables were kept strictly secret from outsiders. She was about to break that principle for my sake.

“Is it all right for you to tell me?”

“No.”

“Then why?”

“I am thinking of living while breaking the rules too.”

As she said so, she smiled. How could such a person be said to have only come halfway over?

“In truth, I am a rigid and inflexible person. If there is a set rule, I do not try to break it. If I have an appointment with someone, I must arrive early, and if I am misunderstood, I worry so much that I cannot sleep. I have many worries I need not have, and I am sensitive to the world’s assessment of me. In many ways, I have a tiring personality.”

“Is that not the very weariness that created your current reputation, Senior?”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled bitterly.

“In some respects, perhaps that is true. In any case, I am trying to become a little more flexible now. What of it if I break a rule or two? If it helps you, then that alone gives it value.”

“Is there a reason your heart changed?”

“Perhaps because I am aging, I keep looking back on my life. This was regrettable, that was regrettable—it has been a life full of regrets, but among them, the greatest regret is this.”

Chaang.

She gently drew her sword. Her gaze turned toward the blade.

“I devoted my entire life to ensuring this sword would never be broken by anyone. In exchange, my life became just as hard.”

Lifting her gaze from the sword, she looked at me and said,

“I regret so deeply that I lived my life too rigidly.”

I could feel that she truly regretted it.

As if comforting her, I spoke gently.

“You feel hurt because it seems no one recognizes that life, don’t you? You lived so thoroughly and meticulously, yet it feels like there is no one who truly understands that. Everyone else lives just fine in a half-hearted way, and you wonder if you alone were being foolish, don’t you?”

At that, surprise appeared on Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s face.

“How do you understand my heart so well?”

Because I, too, have felt that at least once.

“It is because I respect you greatly, Senior.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled brightly and said,

“Yes, I should tell you. When there is someone who understands my heart so well and respects me, what are some trifling rules that I should keep everything hidden? The one who cast the vote in favor was the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon.”

At her words, I was startled.

“Surprising, isn’t it?”

“Yes. Isn’t the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon one of my brother’s people?”

“That is right.”

It was unexpected.

It was unexpected that the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon had taken our side, and even more unexpected that Blood Heaven Blade Demon had persuaded him.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable interpreted that part this way.

“They are both villains, so I suppose they got along well.”

I chuckled, but she was serious.

“If it were someone else, I would not know, but I am telling you because the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon is involved. He is not someone to be taken lightly. It is possible Blade Demon may be harboring other thoughts.”

I asked with a smile,

“Do you hate Elder Blade Demon that much?”

“I do not hate him. To hate someone means there is some intense emotion involved. I simply dislike him a little.”

Forcibly pushing Blade Demon out of her life, she rose from her seat.

“Then I shall go now.”

“Thank you for telling me such important information.”

“Think of it as a gift from your sparring friend.”

As she was about to leave, Seo Daeryong was standing in the hallway holding a water bottle for the flowerpot.

In his own way, it was an effort to look good in front of Ilhwa Sword Venerable, but the problem was that she had become slightly sensitive after mentioning Blade Demon.

To Seo Daeryong, who had gone from an inspector of the Yellow Springs Pavilion to the disciple of the villainous Blade Demon, Ilhwa Sword Venerable spoke coldly.

“That flower is the flower I cherish most. If it dies because of someone’s mistake, I will be very, very sad.”

After she left like that, Seo Daeryong looked ready to cry.

“From now on, I’m the third most important thing in this office. That flower’s name is now Number Two.”

At his self-deprecating joke, I laughed and left the office.

“Where are you going?”

“To meet the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon.”

“Huk! How can you say something so frightening as if you were saying you’re going to eat?”

“Is the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon scary?”

Seo Daeryong opened his eyes wide in astonishment and asked,

“Then you are not scared? Among our cult’s Demon Venerables, I find the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon the scariest.”

“Before, didn’t you say the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable was the scariest?”

“He is dead now.”

“Why is the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon scary? Because he has the words utterly evil attached to him?”

“Haven’t you heard? They say when the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon gets angry, he is truly terrifying.”

“I’m scary when I get angry too. I think you would be the scariest.”

“Please leave me out of it. I am someone who swallows my anger alone when I get mad. My dream is to live without anything ever making me truly angry. That was the kind of person I was…”

Since he knew and I knew that he had changed quite a bit now, we looked at each other and chuckled.

“Are you really not afraid?”

“Why would I be afraid when I’m going to see someone funny?”

“Funny?”

“He’s a Laughing Demon, isn’t he?”

Because the words Utterly Evil stood before it, people often forgot that he was a Laughing Demon. Seo Daeryong was no different.

“I doubt it means the character for laughter. If you peel off the character for laughter, underneath it there will be characters like burn to death, tear to death, or burst to death. Huk! Surely you are not trying to draw the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon to our side, are you? Please tell me that is not it.”

“Are you afraid of him being our enemy? Or afraid of him being our ally?”

“Of course… Even so, the Utterly Evil Laughing Demon is out of the question!”

Though I left after saying that, I knew better than anyone. The Utterly Evil Laughing Demon was someone who could never become an ally.

Even so, the reason I was going to meet him was to make him not an ally, but not an enemy either.

Chapter 94: I Fight Because I Know.
-----------------------------------
Before going to meet the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil, I stopped by the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s quarters. There was something I needed to confirm with him.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon was sitting by the window, basking in the warm sunlight as he read a book.

“So you really do read books in your spare time.”

“It’s all for show. When I hear your footsteps from afar, I run to the bookshelf.”

Setting the book down, he asked,

“What brings you here?”

Without entering, I stood outside the window and spoke to him.

“I came to ask you about someone.”

“Who?”

“Do you know of the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil?”

For an instant, I sensed the Blood Heaven Saber Demon flinch.

“Why are you asking about him?”

“I’m thinking of drawing him to our side.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s expression creased slightly.

“Damn it. That loose-lipped woman.”

“What do you mean?”

“Don’t play dumb.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon was so sharp that he had realized the One Flower Sword Venerable had told me about it. In any case, he wasn’t the sort of person to go around spreading this, so I spoke freely.

“It was the Sword Venerable who told me that the one who persuaded him in the first place wasn’t herself, but you, Elder. She did it for your sake.”

“It wasn’t for my sake. She just has a moral obsession with cleanliness.”

“You know each other so well, so why do you quarrel so much?”

“Do strangers ever fight? People fight because they know each other.”

Since I couldn’t refute that, I merely let out a faint laugh.

“So what do you want to ask?”

“How did you persuade the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil?”

That was why I had come to see the Blood Heaven Saber Demon today.

Then an astonishing fact came to light.

“He came to see me first.”

“The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil came to you first?”

“Yes. Why would I go out of my way to seek out that unpleasant fellow and ask for a favor?”

“Senior Sword Venerable was wrong, then.”

“What did she say?”

“She said the persuasion must have worked because you were both villains.”

“What? She lumped me together with a man like that?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon sprang up, visibly offended.

It might seem like I was tattling for no reason, but this too was part of my effort to reconcile the two of them.

I had to keep bringing each of them up to the other like this. To show that they had not forgotten each other. That they still hated each other. Only then, when an opportunity came one day, would all these fragments come together and make reconciliation possible. There was no such thing as a reconciliation that happened all at once out of nowhere.

“What condition did he offer?”

“He said he’d vote for Cheongseon, so when he asked for help later, I should help him.”

“You accepted without knowing what he might ask of you later?”

“He’ll ask for something for himself, I suppose. If he asks for something absurd, I can simply refuse.”

“You could have at least made a show of it to me. ‘Because of you, I ended up owing him a debt. What are you going to do about it?’ Why did you keep quiet?”

“Make a show of it, my foot!”

When I first met the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, he had truly been calculating and meticulous. The sort of man who seemed as if he would make a fuss over every favor and collect on every debt.

But beyond the door to his heart, once it was opened, there was another Blood Heaven Saber Demon. A man who tossed such showiness into a corner and enjoyed reading books beneath the sunlight.

“Elder, you have to make a show of things sometimes for the other party to know.”

“They don’t need to know. And it didn’t particularly feel as though he helped this time because he wanted to ask something of me. Why would the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil go out of his way to me?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon looked at me with an expression that said, You know that doesn’t make sense either.

If that intuition was right, then why had he helped us?

“In any case, thank you. I’ll go meet the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil.”

When I bid him farewell, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon reopened the book he had closed, as if he had been waiting for that.

“You’re not worried, even though I’m going to meet the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil?”

“About whom? You? Or the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil?”

I turned away with a smile.

After leaving the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s quarters, I headed for the Valley of Villains, located in the northeast of the Inner Court.

The Valley of Villains was, as its name suggested, a valley.

It was not a natural valley, but an artificially made one. Along the stream, large and small dwellings where the Faceless Guests resided were lined up on either side.

They seemed to have been built irregularly, anywhere and everywhere, but in truth, it was a kind of fortress constructed under a meticulous plan according to the skill and status of the Faceless Guests, as well as in preparation for outside invasion.

When I sent word at the entrance of the Valley of Villains that I had come to see the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil, a Faceless Guest wearing a white mask guided me in.

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil’s subordinates were called Faceless Guests. They all went about wearing masks—white masks with only eyeholes cut into them.

The eyeholes were shaped like crescent moons laid on their sides, as if smiling. The eyeholes smiled, but the real eyes within did not, and looking at those masks whose elements did not harmonize naturally sent a chill down one’s spine.

At the deepest part of the valley was the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil’s residence. The building had been modeled after a white mask. There was a single window where the eyes would be, and that was likely where the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil resided.

Feeling as though I was being swallowed by the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil, I walked in through the mouth that the mask did not have.

After climbing the spiral staircase that wound upward, I reached the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil’s room.

The emotion I felt the moment I stepped into his room was dread.

Not only the walls on all sides, but even the floor had been painted entirely white, and there was nothing in the room. No table, no decorative cabinet, not even a chair or cushion.

In that truly empty space, only the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil stood alone.

He was facing the wall, dressed in white. If not for the black hair visible at the back of his head, one might not even have known he was standing there. He had blended into the space itself.

Even though I had entered, he continued to stare only at the wall, so I slowly walked over and stood beside him.

“What are you looking at?”

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil turned his head toward me. Like the Faceless Guests, he wore a white mask, and within the smiling eyeholes, his gaze was clear and cold.

The mask specially made for the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil had been crafted so that his eyes could not be seen from the outside, but my Divine Eye Art pierced through the darkness, allowing me to see his eyes clearly.

Only then did the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil answer.

“I was looking at nothing.”

His low, deep voice was pleasant to hear, and his tall stature and long limbs gave the impression that he was strikingly handsome. And unlike what one might expect from the name Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil, he was courteous.

Because he was courteous, he was eerie. That was the sort of feeling he gave off.

“It has been a long time, Elder.”

In truth, one could say this was the first time in this life. Aside from seeing him from a distance at official gatherings, this was my first time facing him personally like this.

“Elder? I am still young, so please treat me comfortably, as you would a friend.”

“How many people have departed this world after being deceived by those words?”

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil laughed. As befitted his name, he was a man who laughed easily.

“What about you, Second Young Master? Are you usually the one being deceived? Or the one deceiving?”

“If I had to say, I’m the type who pretends to be deceived.”

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil laughed out of habit.

“Isn’t it tiring to always laugh like that?”

For an instant, a strange light flowed from his two eyes within the hollow openings.

“Why would laughing be tiring?”

“I wondered if laughing wasn’t harder than crying. Living while crying is easy, but living while laughing is difficult, isn’t it?”

“At last, someone has appeared who understands my hardships.”

Even as he said so, he laughed again. This laugh was loud and exaggerated, like that of an actor in the marketplace.

I do not trust his laughter.

That laughter is like the tendril on the head of a fish that lives in the deep sea. If you are bewitched by that dazzling tendril swaying gently and let your attention be stolen, you will find yourself being drawn into a great mouth before you know it.

Never forget. He is not laughing because he likes you.

“Second Young Master, why have you come to see me?”

I could not reveal that the One Flower Sword Venerable had broken the rules and told me the contents of the vote.

“We have never had a conversation like this before, have we? So I came to seek your guidance and learn what sort of person you are. To be honest, I also came hoping that you would support me instead of my elder brother.”

“Shall we walk for a moment?”

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil slowly began walking along the white wall.

“When I organize my thoughts, I walk like this.”

I followed behind him. The string tying his mask dangled at the back of his head. That knot, which would have come undone if a child tugged at it, had never been untied by anyone in the martial world.

As he walked, he spoke.

“If you answer one question, I will consider supporting you positively.”

“Please ask.”

“How did you kill the Soul-Stealing Demon Venerable?”

He tossed out that important question as if it were nothing, and I too answered calmly as I followed behind him.

“I was not the one who killed the Soul-Stealing Demon Venerable.”

“No. You certainly killed him. What I’m curious about is not whether you killed him or not. It is how you killed him.”

He was convinced that I had killed him.

“Second Young Master, I will keep it an absolute secret from everyone else. Tell only me. As I did with Cheongseon, I will give you one more vote.”

“I’ll say it again, I did not kill him.”

At that, the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil abruptly stopped and turned around, striding back toward me. He came right up in front of me and held out his hand.

“Let us be friends.”

I know very well that he is the sort of person who makes such sudden moves.

And this is the second time I have heard these words from him.

When I returned to the main cult to search for the materials for the Great Regression Art, the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil and I had crossed paths. Back then as well, he had said those exact words to me.

“No.”

“Why not?”

Because everyone who became his friend died. With one sole exception.

“Because there are as many as three reasons we cannot be friends.”

“Oh, there are three reasons?”

He openly displayed his curiosity.

“What is the first reason?”

“If we were to act like friends, I would make a mistake someday. A comfortable heart inevitably invites rudeness. Formal speech will prevent my mistakes.”

“What does it matter if you make a few mistakes?”

“That’s true. It would be fine, so long as the other person were not the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil.”

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil laughed loudly.

“Our Second Young Master is even more entertaining than the rumors say. Then what is the second reason?”

“The Pavilion Master of Yellow Springs and the Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil do not suit each other.”

“They say evil and good are like two sides of the same coin. If you learn evil from me, it will help you uphold good.”

“That saying was probably made up by the side of evil.”

“What do you mean?”

“How could good and evil be stuck together like two sides of a coin? Evil must lie beneath an endless abyss, while good must be atop that high mountain. It is nothing more than a phrase villains invented to justify themselves.”

Of course, what he had said earlier was a metaphor used to mean that good and evil can coexist within a single human being. But I deliberately separated them far apart, speaking only of good and evil themselves.

“Then you must think of me as a hypocrite, Second Young Master.”

“How could that be? How could a person who proudly attaches the word ‘evil’ to his own title be a hypocrite?”

“Then do you think I am impressive?”

“Evil can never be impressive under any circumstances. The only time evil can appear impressive is when it subdues another evil.”

He laughed. He laughed heartily, but his eyes within the eyeholes had settled coldly. If not for the Divine Eye Art, I would not have known either.

It was merely astonishing that one could laugh so excitedly while having such a sunken gaze.

“So the rumors were true.”

“What rumors do you mean?”

“The rumors that the Second Young Master has become a stiff, suffocating scrap of the righteous sects.”

“If that is how you felt, then perhaps it is because of this. At some point, we became worse than those stiff, suffocating scraps of the righteous sects. So I intend to set things right, even if only now.”

The Laughing Demon of Utmost Evil, who had been laughing softly, asked,

“Is this the new demonic path you dream of, Second Young Master?”

“You could say that.”

“Then you and I can never coexist. I will continue supporting the First Young Master.”

“I suppose it cannot be helped. Then I will take my leave.”

I bowed politely and turned away.

Then he asked from behind me,

“Ah, what is the third reason?”

I stopped on my way to the door and turned back to him.

“I do not become friends with people who wear masks.”

It was a moment when laughter should have come, but he did not laugh.

I saw it.

Though he did not laugh aloud, his two eyes within the eyeholes were smiling. It meant he was truly angry.

Until now, he had never removed his mask in front of anyone. Naturally, no one had ever seen the face beneath that mask. No one knew what he looked like, or about how old he was. Telling him to remove his mask was taboo, and a reverse scale.

But even he had shown his face to another person exactly once in his life.

To me before the regression, when I had come to obtain the materials for the Great Art. To me, the only one who became his friend and survived.

I had seen the face beneath that mask.

Chapter 95: Then it's settled.
------------------------------
At my provocation, the air around us grew heavy and stifling. The demonic qi flowing from the Extreme Evil Laughing Demon had seized control of the surroundings.

His demonic qi was special. The reason he had earned the epithet “Extreme Evil” was precisely because of this demonic qi known as the Laughing Evil Heart.

The Laughing Evil Heart awakened humanity’s primal violence.

When exposed to the Laughing Evil Heart, one’s heart would pound violently and the urge to kill someone would arise. In truth, if one’s cultivation was weak, one might even draw a sword and stab the person beside them to death.

Even experts with deep internal energy found it difficult to fight normally under the influence of the Laughing Evil Heart.

“Behind this mask are two faces. If the face other than the one Second Young Master has seen until now awakens, even I find it difficult to handle. That is why removing the mask is hard.”

If the face I had seen until now was the Laughing Demon, then the other face must have been Extreme Evil.

“I did not say that to be rude to you, Laughing Demon. It is just that, to my mind, a friend is someone whose face you should be able to see without reserve, whether they have two faces or three. It was said out of youthful bravado, so if I was rude, please forgive me.”

I had been exposed to this Laughing Evil Heart before my regression as well. Back then, I had not learned the Heavenly Demon Protection Art, so I had to endure it with great difficulty.

But now was different.

The Heavenly Demon Protection Art activated naturally and blocked his Laughing Evil Heart. My Heavenly Demon Protection Art had not yet reached great completion, but it had reached a level where it could easily fend off the Laughing Evil Heart being emitted now.

Instead of intensifying the Laughing Evil Heart further, the Extreme Evil Laughing Demon slowly withdrew his demonic qi. The smiling eyes in the holes of his mask gradually settled into coldness. The laughter fading meant that he had calmed his anger.

“As expected, Second Young Master is remarkable. You are not shaken at all by my Laughing Evil Heart.”

“I was trained in malice and venom from a young age, you see. You know, don’t you? That my elder brother was harsh to me, if no one else.”

The Extreme Evil Laughing Demon laughed out loud. As if he had never created such tension, he had returned to the way he had been when I first saw him.

“As expected, Second Young Master is an amusing person. It is truly a pity that we could not become friends.”

“Then I will take my leave.”

As I opened the door to leave, the Extreme Evil Laughing Demon spoke.

“As expected, I still believe Second Young Master killed the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.”

“You misunderstand.”

I did not deny it vehemently. This very tension between him and me was the most important element in maintaining our relationship.

As the door closed, I saw the Extreme Evil Laughing Demon left alone in the white room.

* * *

At the entrance to the Valley of Villains, I met Seo Daeryong.

“Investigator Seo, what brings you here?”

“I am in the middle of training. I was running as part of my physical conditioning.”

“Of all places, in front of the Valley of Villains?”

“It just turned out that way.”

“If things got dangerous, were you planning to rush in and save me?”

He was even carrying the great saber he used for training.

“I’m touched.”

“I said that’s not it. How could I possibly break through that place?”

“That’s why I’m touched. You came to save me even though you knew full well you’d die.”

“I just came to take a look. If I heard sounds of fighting from inside, I was going to inform Master.”

“As expected, you’re smart. Of course that’s what you should do. You shouldn’t charge in when you don’t have the skill and die pointlessly. With a right arm this wise, what do I have to worry about?”

Only then did Seo Daeryong seem relieved that I had come out safely, and he belatedly let out a sigh of relief.

“Phew, my legs are still shaking. Even if it was you, Pavilion Master, I was a little worried.”

“Investigator Seo.”

“Yes.”

“Keep worrying about me from now on too.”

“What?”

“I’m asking you to worry about me. I can make mistakes, miss the mark, and do foolish things too. So keep worrying and helping me.”

Seo Daeryong smiled faintly.

“Of course. If your right arm doesn’t do it, who will?”

“My left arm, I suppose.”

“Do you have to ruin this nice atmosphere like that?”

The two of us walked out, bantering back and forth like that, until I stopped and looked up at the tree overhead.

“What are you doing up there?”

To my surprise, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was sitting on a tree branch.

“Watching the sunset.”

“Here, of all places?”

“And keeping an eye on whether my disciple is slacking off.”
